# Letzte Schriften über die Philosophie der Psychologie – I

<details>

<summary>1948–1949, 998 remarks, Ms-137, Ms-167, Ms-138</summary>

<img class="viz" src="/viz/W-LW-1.svg">

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#76a.6) <span class="fac">[76a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-76a6}

<span class="series-number">1</span> 22.10.1948

A language in which there is a word “to be afraid” which means: to torment oneself with fearful thoughts. – And now one could, for example, assume that this verb does not have a first person in the present tense. The English “I am …ing.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#76a.7+76b.1) <span class="fac">[76a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76a.webp),[76b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-76a776b1}

<span class="series-number">2</span> If I say to someone “I hope you will come,” is it less urgent if the hoping lasted only 30 seconds than if it lasted 2 minutes? “I am glad that you succeeded!” – “How long are you glad?” A strange question. But it could have sense. The answer could be: “Whenever I think of it,” or “At first, I wasn’t glad about it, but then I was,” or “I keep thinking about it and I’m glad,” or “It only occurs to me for moments, but then I’m glad,” etc. One also says “It is a lasting joy to me,” and “For a moment, I was glad about his misfortune.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#76b.2) <span class="fac">[76b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-76b2}

<span class="series-number">3</span> “I move the knight” – “How long do you move it?”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#76b.3) <span class="fac">[76b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-76b3}

<span class="series-number">4</span> As an example of the sentential form “If p, then q,” consider: “If he comes, I will tell him.” If he now _does not_ come, – have I thereby kept my promise? – have I broken it? – Can one say that this sentence asserts a ‘connection’? Would I answer him with “It _doesn’t_ have to be”? It is not as if the sentence were: “If _these_ two meet, there will be a fight.” Here, that answer would be possible.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#76b.4) <span class="fac">[76b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-76b4}

<span class="series-number">5</span> But if the material implication is asserted (& there is such a case!) – can one also answer “It does not have to be” to “p ⊃ q”? And what does that mean here?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#76b.5+77a.1) <span class="fac">[76b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76b.webp),[77a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-76b577a1}

<span class="series-number">6</span> “When the two poles come close together, a spark jumps across” – What does one consider to be a verification of the sentence? The observation that they never come close together? – Can what we want to say here be expressed with the material implication? Certainly not; but perhaps with the ‘formal’ one? But no more than that. – What we want to state is after all a kind of law of nature; the kind of observation that leads to it is easy enough to imagine. One has observed that a spark always jumps across when they come close to each other. – Is the sentence perhaps of the kind “(x)․φx ⊃ ψx:(∃x)․φx”? If not, then this sentence must have some application, even if not the same one.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#77a.2) <span class="fac">[77a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-77a2}

<span class="series-number">7</span> “If he comes, I will tell him…” is an _intention_, a _promise_. If it is not to be a false promise, it must not be based on the certainty that he will not come. It is neither a material nor a formal implication.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#77a.3) <span class="fac">[77a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-77a3}

<span class="series-number">8</span> In the case of a scientific conditional prediction, one could distinguish between _warrant_ & _correctness_. One could call it “warranted” if it follows from a theory that is justified in such & such a way. So, if the antecedent does not hold true, then one can say: _were_ it to hold true, then _it would be_ ‥‥‥ But that does not give one the right to say that the antecedent has not come true.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#77a.4+77b.1) <span class="fac">[77a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77a.webp),[77b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-77a477b1}

<span class="series-number">9</span> Must a sentence like “Every body is in motion…” (the law of inertia) be formulated in the form “if – then”? “If something is a body, then it is in motion…” – Or must it say: “There are bodies; and if something is a body, then…?” (No one would think of expressing it that way.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#77b.2) <span class="fac">[77b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-77b2}

<span class="series-number">10</span> 23.10.1948

It is obvious that one could simply have an _application_ of the concept of “fear” to animals, & that the term would lack the first person. Its third person would be used very similarly to the third person of “to fear.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#77b.3) <span class="fac">[77b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-77b3}

<span class="series-number">11</span> 24.10.1948

Remember that the subjunctive has no sense except in a conditional sentence. If someone says “I _would have_ won this game,” one will ask: “_If_ –?”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#77b.4) <span class="fac">[77b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-77b4}

<span class="series-number">12</span> There is nothing more difficult than considering concepts without prejudice. For prejudice is a kind of understanding. And to give up on that, when it is precisely what matters to us, –.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#77b.5) <span class="fac">[77b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-77b5}

<span class="series-number">13</span> The English “I’m furious” is not an expression of self-observation. Similarly in German, “Ich bin wütend”; but not “Ich bin zornig”. (“Horribly, my fury changes in my breast ‥‥”. It is a trembling of fury.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#77b.6+78a.1) <span class="fac">[77b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77b.webp),[78a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-77b678a1}

<span class="series-number">14</span> One asks oneself: “What does ‘I am afraid’ actually mean? What am I thinking?” And, of course, no answer comes, or one that clearly does not suffice. The question is: “In what kind of context is it?”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#78a.2) <span class="fac">[78a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-78a2}

<span class="series-number">15</span> One could also say: “I just say it.” For this only means: Don’t worry about something that accompanies the speaking.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#78a.3) <span class="fac">[78a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-78a3}

<span class="series-number">16</span> Can the utterance not stand in different contexts? Which give it one face one time, and another face another time?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#78a.4) <span class="fac">[78a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-78a4}

<span class="series-number">17</span> I say “I am afraid…,” the other person asks me: “What did you mean by that? Was it like an exclamation; or did you allude to your state in the last few hours; did you simply want to communicate something?” Can I always give him a clear answer? Can I never give him one? – I will sometimes have to say: “I thought about how I spent today and, as it were, shook my head at myself” – but sometimes: “It was: Oh God! If only I weren’t so afraid!” – or: “It was just a cry of fear” – or: “I wanted you to know how I feel.” – Such explanations really do sometimes follow the utterance. But one could not _always_ give them.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#78a.5+78b.1) <span class="fac">[78a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78a.webp),[78b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-78a578b1}

<span class="series-number">18</span> One could imagine people who, as it were, think much more precisely than we do, and who use a lot of different words, sometimes the one, sometimes the other.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#78b.2) <span class="fac">[78b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-78b2}

<span class="series-number">19</span> Nothing is more important than the formation of fictional concepts, which teach us to understand our own concepts.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#78b.3) <span class="fac">[78b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-78b3}

<span class="series-number">20</span> “What is fear?” – “The _phenomena_ & occasions of fear are _these_: – – –” “So is ‘I am afraid – – –’ a description of my state?” It can be used in such a context & with such an intention. But if I simply want to communicate my fear to someone, then it is not such a description.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#78b.4) <span class="fac">[78b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-78b4}

<span class="series-number">21</span> October 25, 1948

“I am afraid” can, for example, simply be said to explain my way of acting. It is then far from being a moan; it can even be said with a smile.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#78b.5) <span class="fac">[78b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-78b5}

<span class="series-number">22</span> One asks oneself: “What does ‘I am afraid’ actually mean, what do I aim at (with it)?” And, of course, no answer comes, or one that is not sufficient. The question is: “In what kind of context is it?”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#78b.6+79a.1) <span class="fac">[78b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78b.webp),[79a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-78b679a1}

<span class="series-number">23</span> No answer comes if one wants to answer the question “What do I aim at,” “What am I thinking,” etc., by saying the words and, as it were, paying attention to oneself from the corner of one’s eye, observing one’s mind. However, I can, in a particular case, ask: “Why did I say that, what did I want with it?” and could also answer the question, but not on the basis of an observation of the accompanying phenomena of speaking. And my answer would supplement the earlier utterance, paraphrase it.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#79a.2) <span class="fac">[79a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-79a2}

<span class="series-number">24</span> What is fear? What does it mean to “be afraid”? If I wanted to explain it by means of _one_ example, I would _play_ fear.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#79a.3) <span class="fac">[79a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-79a3}

<span class="series-number">25</span> Could I also represent hope in this way? Hardly. Or even belief?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#79a.4) <span class="fac">[79a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-79a4}

<span class="series-number">26</span> “I think he will come.” “I keep saying to myself: ‘He will come.’” For the second one, people might have their own verb.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#79a.5) <span class="fac">[79a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-79a5}

<span class="series-number">27</span> Describing my state of mind (e.g., fear) is something I do in a very specific context. (Just as a particular action is only an experiment in a specific context. Is it really so surprising that I use the same expression in different games? And sometimes even as if between the games? “I was thinking of him” & “I was thinking about him” means something quite different.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#79a.6) <span class="fac">[79a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-79a6}

<span class="series-number">28</span> And do I always speak with a very specific intention? – And is what I say therefore senseless?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#79b.1) <span class="fac">[79b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-79b1}

<span class="series-number">29</span> “Now that you mention it: I think he’ll come.” I believe now, you are right: he would come.” “No. I am convinced: he will come.” One can imagine a context for all such expressions.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#79b.2) <span class="fac">[79b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-79b2}

<span class="series-number">30</span> What does it take to describe a state of mind? – Or might I ask: what does it take to _want_ to describe one’s state of mind?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#79b.3) <span class="fac">[79b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-79b3}

<span class="series-number">31</span> One could also ask: “What must it depend on then?”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#79b.4) <span class="fac">[79b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-79b4}

<span class="series-number">32</span> I wanted to describe my state of mind to you” – as opposed to “I just wanted to give vent to my feelings.” So I wanted him to know ‘how I feel’ (which often involves stating the duration of the state).

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#79b.5) <span class="fac">[79b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-79b5}

<span class="series-number">33</span> It is, after all, something else: calmly _admitting_ one’s fear – & giving it free _expression_. The words may be the same, but the tone & the gestures are different.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#79b.6) <span class="fac">[79b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-79b6}

<span class="series-number">34</span> When, in a eulogy, it says “We mourn our ‥‥․,” this is meant to give expression to the grief; not to communicate something to us. But in a different context, these words are a communication. In a _prayer_ at the graveside, they could also be a _kind_ of communication.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#79b.7+80a.1) <span class="fac">[79b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79b.webp),[80a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-79b780a1}

<span class="series-number">35</span> We don’t necessarily say that one is _complaining_ simply because one says one is in pain. Thus, the words “I am in pain” can be a complaint & also something else. (And it is similar with the expression of fear & other emotional states.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#80a.2) <span class="fac">[80a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-80a2}

<span class="series-number">36</span> But if “I am afraid” is always something similar to a complaint, why should it always be a description of my state of mind? What is a complaint?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#80a.3) <span class="fac">[80a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-80a3}

<span class="series-number">37</span> Because what distinguishes the complaint “I have pain” from a mere communication? It is the intention. And this will perhaps also be expressed in the tone.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#80a.4) <span class="fac">[80a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-80a4}

<span class="series-number">38</span> The circumstances in which a sentence stands are best represented in a drama, hence the best example of expression in a specific meaning is a quote from a drama. And who asks the person in the drama what he experiences while speaking?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#80a.5) <span class="fac">[80a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-80a5}

<span class="series-number">39</span> October 26, 1948

“You must know – I am afraid.” “You must know – I am apprehensive.” Yes, one can also say it in a smiling tone.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#80a.6+80b.1) <span class="fac">[80a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80a.webp),[80b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-80a680b1}

And do you want to tell me that he doesn’t feel it? How else would he _know_ it? – But even if it is a communication, he doesn’t read it off from his inner self. He couldn’t even then use his _sensations_ as proof for his statement. _They_ don’t teach it to him.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#80b.2) <span class="fac">[80b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-80b2}

<span class="series-number">40</span> For consider the sensations evoked by the _gestures_ of horror: the words “I am horrified” are also a gesture, & when I hear & feel their utterance, this belongs to those other sensations. Why should the unspoken gesture motivate the spoken one?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#80b.3) <span class="fac">[80b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-80b3}

<span class="series-number">41</span> [→ ] We learn to use the word “thinking” _under certain circumstances_. If the circumstances are different, we do not know how to use it. – But that does not mean that we must be able to describe those circumstances.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#80b.4) <span class="fac">[80b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-80b4}

<span class="series-number">42</span> “If people strongly disagreed in their color statements, they could not use our concept of color.” – If people strongly disagreed in their color statements, then, precisely because of that, they would not use _our concept of color_. They would not be playing our language-game: for consider how one would have to compare theirs and ours!

_---_

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#80b.5+81a.1) <span class="fac">[80b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80b.webp),[81a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-80b581a1}

<span class="series-number">43</span> So, when I hear someone say “I am afraid,” how can I know whether this is a ‘description of a state of mind’ or something else? Should I ask him, & will he certainly understand the question? – But he could answer it. How? For example, _like this_: “No; I just let off some steam,” or “Yes; I want you to know how I feel.” But such a question is hardly ever asked. Isn’t it because the tone & the context must provide the answer? For from these, one can see whether he is, for example, making fun of his own fear; whether he is, as it were, discovering it within himself; whether he is confessing it to me unwillingly but for the sake of openness; whether he is expressing it like a cry, etc. – & don’t the words, however they are uttered, instruct me about the same state of affairs, namely his state of mind?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#81a.2) <span class="fac">[81a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-81a2}

<span class="series-number">44</span> Does the sentence “Napoleon was crowned in 1804” have a different meaning depending on whether I say it to someone to inform him, or in a history exam to show what I know, or etc. etc.? In order to understand it, the meanings of its words must be explained to me in the same way for all these purposes. And if the meaning of the words and their combination constitute the meaning of the sentence, – – –.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#81a.3) <span class="fac">[81a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-81a3}

<span class="series-number">45</span> The problem is this: the cry, which cannot be called a description, which is more primitive than any description, nevertheless serves the purpose of a description of the state of mind.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#81a.4) <span class="fac">[81a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-81a4}

<span class="series-number">46</span> October 27, 1948.

Whoever can cry can’t necessarily communicate something to someone in conversation by doing so.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#81a.5+81b.1) <span class="fac">[81a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81a.webp),[81b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-81a581b1}

<span class="series-number">47</span> I hear someone say, “I am afraid.” I ask: “In what context did you say that? Was it a gasp, was it a confession, was it self-observation, ‥‥․?”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#81b.2) <span class="fac">[81b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-81b2}

<span class="series-number">48</span> Does someone who cries “Help!” want to describe how they feel? Nothing is further from their intention than to describe something.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#81b.3) <span class="fac">[81b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-81b3}

<span class="series-number">49</span> But there are transitions from what we would not call a description to what we would call a description.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#81b.4) <span class="fac">[81b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-81b4}

<span class="series-number">50</span> The phrase “description of a state of mind” characterizes a certain _game_. And if I only hear the words “I am afraid,” I may indeed _guess_ what game is being played here (from the tone, for example), but I will only know it when I know the context.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#81b.5) <span class="fac">[81b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-81b5}

<span class="series-number">51</span> For what we call “describing” includes one or another of a class of characteristics. The observing, considering, remembering behavior, an effort for accuracy, the ability to improve, the comparing. A cry is not a description. But there are transitions. And the words “I am afraid” can be closer to or further from a cry. They can be very close to it and _very_ far from it.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#82a.2) <span class="fac">[82a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-82a2}

<span class="series-number">52</span> [→] If a careful listening shows me that in that game I experience the word “soft” sometimes _so_, sometimes _so_, – doesn’t it also show me that, in the context of an entire sentence, which I understand and experience in some sense, I often don’t experience that word itself?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#82a.3) <span class="fac">[82a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-82a3}

<span class="series-number">53</span> “The meaning of the word was present in my mind.” – Would one say that if the word is in an unambiguous context?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#82a.4) <span class="fac">[82a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-82a4}

<span class="series-number">54</span> A script in which the crossed-out word, the crossed-out sentence, is a sign.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#82a.5) <span class="fac">[82a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-82a5}

<span class="series-number">55</span> You claim that you experienced the word, as you pronounced it just now, in that way. Then, with the same keen feeling, say whether you intend this word in the correct context, _in this sense_. For the fact that you intend it in another sense _so_ and not _so_, and will probably explain that later, is clear.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#82a.6) <span class="fac">[82a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-82a6}

<span class="series-number">56</span> But then the question remains, why, in the _game_ of intending, we also talk about an _‘intention’_. – That is a question of a different kind. It is precisely the phenomenon of the game that we talk about an ‘intention’ only in a finite sense.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#82b.2) <span class="fac">[82b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-82b2}

<span class="series-number">57</span> 28.10.1948

Is it as if one were posing a question of physics in a book on pure mathematics, as if that were the question you wanted to ask? Is it a misunderstanding? I am not using the word _for_ something else, but in a different situation. [Just as I do not use the word “knowledge” in two different ways when I say “I knew it in a dream.” Cf. also the feeling of unreality.] Is the technique of its application taught to me differently here?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#82b.3) <span class="fac">[82b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-82b3}

<span class="series-number">58</span> I hear a Beethoven piece & say “Beethoven!” – Does the word have a different meaning here than in the sentence “Beethoven was born in Bonn in 1770”? (If one did not understand the tone of the exclamation, one could perhaps explain it: “Only Beethoven would write like that.”)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#82b.4) <span class="fac">[82b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-82b4}

<span class="series-number">59</span> Would it be more correct to say that _e_ ‘corresponds’ to yellow, rather than “_e_ is yellow”? Isn’t it precisely the trick of the language-game that we say _e is_ yellow? Yes, if there were someone who was _inclined_ to say that _e_ ‘_corresponds_’ to yellow & not that it is yellow, would he not be almost as different from the other as someone for whom vowels & colors are not connected? And similarly for the experience of meaning.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#82b.5) <span class="fac">[82b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-82b5}

<span class="series-number">60</span> If, when learning the word “bank,” & in order to impress its double meaning upon myself, I alternately looked at the picture of a bench & a money bank & always said “bank,” or “This is a bank,” – would the ‘experience of meaning’ take place here? Certainly not, I would say. But if, for example, it seemed to me that the tone of my pronunciation indicated that I meant one or the other, – then yes.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#83a.1) <span class="fac">[83a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-83a1}

<span class="series-number">61</span> [→] It is not as if two things were persistently designated with the same word, & one asked: Why does one do this, if they are _really_ different? – The new use consists precisely in the fact that the old expression is used in a new situation; not to designate something new.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#83a.2) <span class="fac">[83a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-83a2}

<span class="series-number">62</span> The experience of the ‘appropriate word’. Is this the same as the experience of ‘meaning’?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#83a.3) <span class="fac">[83a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-83a3}

<span class="series-number">63</span> “Why does one call _this_ a ‘knowledge’ in a dream?” – One does not call anything a knowledge in a dream, but says “I knew in my dream ‥‥” Why does one call this ‘meaning’ & ‘meaning,’ when it is not about meaning? – What do I call a ‘meaning’ in the language-game: I _say_ “I meant this with the word now ‥‥․”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#83a.5) <span class="fac">[83a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-83a5}

But what do I call it? – An experience? And which experience? Can I describe it in any other way than through the expression: I ‘mean’ this word _in this way_ now?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#83a.4) <span class="fac">[83a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-83a4}

<span class="series-number">64</span> So I cannot say: I simply designate two related things with the same word. (For otherwise the problem would never have arisen.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#83a.6+83b.1) <span class="fac">[83a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83a.webp),[83b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-83a683b1}

<span class="series-number">65</span> “Why do we also talk about a ‘meaning’ in that language-game?” – What am I asking for? For a reason, for a cause? – Certainly not for a consideration that determines me to speak like this; nor for a justification; for it is not about anything of the sort.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#83b.2) <span class="fac">[83b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-83b2}

<span class="series-number">66</span> 29.10.1948

[→] Call it a dream!

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#83b.3) <span class="fac">[83b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-83b3}

<span class="series-number">67</span> But then the question remains, why does one, in the _game_ of ‘meaning’, also use the word “meaning”? Could one use a different word? Does one use the same word _for_: something else? Could one give a different explanation of it?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#83b.4) <span class="fac">[83b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-83b4}

<span class="series-number">68</span> Call it a dream. It changes nothing.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#83b.5) <span class="fac">[83b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-83b5}

<span class="series-number">69</span> 30.10.1948

“Schubert” – It is as if the name were an adjective.

[→ ] One cannot say, “Look at all that _fits_. For example, the name also fits the bearer.” [→ ] An addition would be an extension; & an extension is precisely what is _not_ the case here. Because one does not call a ‘fitting together’ what is _actually_ not a fitting together. It is as if one were merely extending this concept. Rather, there is something like a _deception_, a reflection. We believe we are seeing what is not there. But it is only _as if_ so. – We know very well that the name “Schubert” has no relationship of fitting to its bearer & to Schubert’s works; & yet we are under compulsion to express ourselves in this way.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#83b.6+84a.1) <span class="fac">[83b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83b.webp),[84a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-83b684a1}

<span class="series-number">70</span> One sees something under the image, under the concept, under the idea of fitting. One can see one thing as a variation of another. And now, in the most extreme case, what one sees _as_ a variation may have no similarity at all with what it is seen as a variation of. – Let's put it this way: first, _this_ figure is a simple projection of _that_. Then the projection rays curve a little; but for me, it is still a projection. Finally, they twist to the point of being unrecognizable, but for me, it is still the projection (As some still see the young in the old, in the completely changed person, they still see the former person). It may seem odd to connect the case of the personal name with this. But one can make a connection. Namely, one _sees_ the personal name as an image.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#84a.2) <span class="fac">[84a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-84a2}

<span class="series-number">71</span> Suppose I see a triangle as a square, by seeing it as the end of a change:

![](../graphics/137,84a.svg)

– Then the type of variation belongs to the seen aspect. But this is not what is happening when the name seems to be the image of the bearer.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#84a.3) <span class="fac">[84a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-84a3}

<span class="series-number">72</span> I say something (e.g. “The name ‘Schubert’ fits perfectly with Schubert”) – it means nothing.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#84a.4+84b.1) <span class="fac">[84a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84a.webp),[84b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-84a484b1}

<span class="series-number">73</span> The sentence “The name ‥‥ fits ‥‥” is, as we use it, not a communication about the name, or its bearer. It is a pathological communication about the communicator. – One does not teach a child that this name fits the person.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#84b.2) <span class="fac">[84b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-84b2}

<span class="series-number">74</span> October 31, 1948

Someone waves (to) me. “What did you want?” – “I wanted you to come.” That is the intention at the time of the action. The sign was the origin of a movement. Was it not also the origin of the explanation? Could this explanation itself _be_: “The waving with the hand was the origin of the explanation that I am giving you now: ‘Come to me’?”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#84b.3) <span class="fac">[84b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-84b3}

<span class="series-number">75</span> 02.11.1948

One could not say here “He [the name] does not quite _fit_”, or “He does not seem to quite _fit_”.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#84b.4) <span class="fac">[84b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-84b4}

It is not as if “to fit” is not _quite_ the right word. One could, however, also use other words; for example, “There is a kinship there.”

“What is always associated, one readily takes to be related. Is that the right expression? Not quite. But it is as if they are related.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#84b.5) <span class="fac">[84b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-84b5}

<span class="series-number">76</span> It is not that: “I consider them to be related, even though they are not” – for I need only, as it were, wake up in order to know that they are not. But I see them under the image of kinship. I use the word, the image. –

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#85a.1) <span class="fac">[85a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/85a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-85a1}

One can, of course, explain: Fitting together and association often go together in use; and therefore that deception (if one wants to call it a deception).

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#85a.2) <span class="fac">[85a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/85a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-85a2}

<span class="series-number">77</span> I think a physiological explanation of the strange phenomenon has been found. One now sees how the deception came about. In the brain, something happens that also happens when ‥‥․ Joyful excitement: Now we understand why one always said ‥‥! And if the explanation has now been given, the riddle solved, what does this leave us with? It has only removed a question that does not interest us; it leaves us with the fact that we use that expression, that image, and would like to use it, where the normal reason is lacking.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#85a.3) <span class="fac">[85a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/85a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-85a3}

<span class="series-number">78</span> [→ ] But then the question remains as to why, in that _game_ of believing, we also speak of a ‘_belief_’. The question does not (really) belong here. We use the word (here) because it has _this_ meaning. No other meaning would do for us. It is a fact to be accepted.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#85a.4) <span class="fac">[85a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/85a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-85a4}

<span class="series-number">79</span> 03.11.1948

But is the word now used in two meanings? No. (Otherwise, we would be obliged to provide an explanation.) Do we teach the use in two different ways?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#85b.1) <span class="fac">[85b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/85b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-85b1}

<span class="series-number">80</span> But doesn't an explanation of the strange phenomenon still interest us? Think of other related phenomena, & what their explanation achieves. Yes, it is indeed interesting to understand _why_ I, on this walk, have the impression that the city must be _there_; even though a simple consideration can convince me that this is not the case. I now want to assume that I know how the deception can arise: I drew false conclusions from similarities between the area and another area, and so on. – But I didn't explicitly draw the false conclusion, and it is also not explained why those similarities led me to this hasty conclusion. The explanation leaves the strangeness intact. (Likewise for the phenomenon of seeing colors, etc.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#85b.2) <span class="fac">[85b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/85b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-85b2}

<span class="series-number">81</span> If someone, when asked “Will N. return?” answers in the affirmative again and again, one can express it by saying that he is in a _state_ of believing. But no one will say that the answer “N. will return” describes the state of the person asked.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#85b.3+86a.1) <span class="fac">[85b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/85b.webp),[86a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-85b386a1}

<span class="series-number">82</span> If “I believe p” states that I am in a certain state, then so does “rp”. For the presence of the words “I believe” cannot bring it about, it can at most indicate it.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#86a.2) <span class="fac">[86a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-86a2}

<span class="series-number">83</span> A language in which “I believe that p” is expressed only through the tone of the assertion “p”. Instead of “He believes ‥‥”, it says there, “He is inclined to say ‥‥”, and there is also the assumption “Suppose I am inclined to say ‥‥”, but not an utterance “I am inclined to say ‥‥”.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#86a.3) <span class="fac">[86a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-86a3}

<span class="series-number">84</span> There are anomalies in other cases as well. One says “It will probably rain,” but not “Suppose it will probably rain, ‥‥”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#86a.4) <span class="fac">[86a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-86a4}

<span class="series-number">85</span> Moore's paradox would not exist in that language; instead, there would be a verb that does not have a first-person present tense.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#86a.5) <span class="fac">[86a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-86a5}

<span class="series-number">86</span> But that shouldn't surprise us. Remember that one can predict one's own future action in the utterance of an intention.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#86a.6) <span class="fac">[86a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-86a6}

<span class="series-number">87</span> Think of the expression “I say, ‥‥” e.g. “I say, it will rain today,” which is simply equivalent to the assertion “It will be ‥‥ today.” “He says, it will ‥‥” incidentally means “He believes, it will ‥‥”. “Suppose I say ‥‥” does _not_ mean “Suppose it will be ‥‥ today.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#86a.7) <span class="fac">[86a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-86a7}

<span class="series-number">88</span> Various concepts touch here and run along a path together. One must not assume that the lines are all _circles_.

![](../graphics/137,86a.svg)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#86a.8+86b.1) <span class="fac">[86a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86a.webp),[86b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-86a886b1}

<span class="series-number">89</span> Whoever sees something according to a certain interpretation does not, for that reason, experience an interpretation.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#86b.2) <span class="fac">[86b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-86b2}

<span class="series-number">90</span> Everyone who thinks knows how notes, pictures, which would mean nothing to another person, and which one cannot even explain oneself, can stand for thoughts, or individual features of thoughts. (The notation of the computer.) [Not yet successful.] Can stand for a thought, or individual features of the thought.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#86b.5) <span class="fac">[86b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-86b5}

<span class="series-number">91</span> “Did you, as you said the word, mean _that_?” – No, I was actually thinking of ….” – Is that an experience? No. An experience would not have the same interest. It could serve the psychologist in exploring the _unconscious_ intention.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#86b.4) <span class="fac">[86b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-86b4}

<span class="series-number">92</span> That is: If I, for example, learn that he saw this and that in front of him when uttering the word, it would be possible for me to draw a conclusion about a tendency in his unconscious – What he had in mind was not his intention in the word.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#87a.1) <span class="fac">[87a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-87a1}

<span class="series-number">93</span> “This plant grows from the seed, not that plant.” Imagine that one would actually express oneself in that way in a language!

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#87a.2) <span class="fac">[87a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-87a2}

<span class="series-number">94</span> But what is the germ? – The experience at the time of speaking. (Also, for example, an image – as indeed it often is.) But it is not, by its nature, the germ. Nor does something become the germ through later development. So, the only thing left is that the image of the germ forces itself upon us. (Quite natural, because we want in the experience

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#87a.3) <span class="fac">[87a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-87a3}

<span class="series-number">95</span> The question that must interest us is, therefore: What is the purpose of the reference to the time of speaking? What does it communicate to one?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#87a.4) <span class="fac">[87a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-87a4}

<span class="series-number">96</span> “I _thought_ that you would think of him in that context.” It was not in the picture he saw before him (which was not clearly discernible), nor in the name he imagined (which could also belong to someone else). It was the chain of interpretations, of explanations. For when he says “I was thinking of ‥․,” “I meant ‥․,” he is, _thereby_, connecting to that point in time. (He is not, for example, recalling a mental image that he saw before him & now showing it to him, to demonstrate that he was thinking of ‥‥.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#87a.5) <span class="fac">[87a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-87a5}

<span class="series-number">97</span> He does not read it from the mental image of what he was thinking.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#87a.6+87b.1) <span class="fac">[87a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87a.webp),[87b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-87a687b1}

<span class="series-number">98</span> He said something, and I had to think of N. When did N. occur to me? At what moment, during which of his words? – If I know at which point, what happened to me at that word? The thoughts took their origin from the word. That is where the chain began. But what makes it the chain? That I say it?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#87b.2) <span class="fac">[87b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-87b2}

<span class="series-number">99</span> “I noticed that you became thoughtful at this word.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#87b.3) <span class="fac">[87b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-87b3}

<span class="series-number">100</span> “As soon as I heard this word, it occurred to me.” What is the practical _meaning_ of this moment? – For I want to say: _It came to me_ as if it occurred to me upon hearing this word,” – so the subjective element does not make any difference. The question is still the same: “What are the consequences of such a _communication_?”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#87b.4) <span class="fac">[87b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-87b4}

<span class="series-number">101</span> 04.11.1948

“I thought of him at this word.” Where lies the interest of this communication? What is the primitive reaction that corresponds to such words?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#87b.5) <span class="fac">[87b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-87b5}

<span class="series-number">102</span> “By the way…” “How did you suddenly come to think of him?” – “You said… and that reminded me of him.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#87b.6) <span class="fac">[87b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-87b6}

<span class="series-number">103</span> When does one say, “I am writing to _this_ person”? How does it manifest itself? How does one know it oneself?! – Am I writing to _him_ while I am writing?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#87b.7) <span class="fac">[87b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-87b7}

<span class="series-number">104</span> It would almost be strange to say: “I was thinking of him while I was writing to him.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#87b.8) <span class="fac">[87b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-87b8}

<span class="series-number">105</span> “We were just talking about _him_, about this person I am now pointing to. How was the speech related to him? Did I not, through these very words, establish a connection with him?”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#88a.1) <span class="fac">[88a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-88a1}

<span class="series-number">106</span> “I knew who they had been talking about.” – How did I know? & What was _that_ state of mind, this ‘knowledge that the conversation was about _this_ person’?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#88a.2) <span class="fac">[88a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-88a2}

<span class="series-number">107</span> “Who were you talking about?” – “N.” – “My friend N.” – “The person in this photograph.” – “The one who is coming through the door now.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#88a.3) <span class="fac">[88a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-88a3}

<span class="series-number">108</span> God, if he had looked into our minds, he would not have been able to see who we were talking about.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#88a.6) <span class="fac">[88a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-88a6}

<span class="series-number">109</span> In philosophy, one must distinguish between the sentences that express the inclination and those that provide the solution.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#88a.7) <span class="fac">[88a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-88a7}

<span class="series-number">110</span> Incurable illness is the rule, not the exception.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#88a.8+88b.1) <span class="fac">[88a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88a.webp),[88b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-88a888b1}

<span class="series-number">111</span> You refer with this statement to the time of speaking. It makes a difference whether you refer to this time or to that time. (The explanation of the word does not refer to any time.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#88b.3) <span class="fac">[88b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-88b3}

How does one begin to say that? (In what lies the importance of this question?)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#88b.4) <span class="fac">[88b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-88b4}

<span class="series-number">112</span> “Why did you look at me when I said that word; were you thinking of…?” So, there is a reaction at this moment, “I was thinking of…” explains the reaction.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#88b.5) <span class="fac">[88b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-88b5}

<span class="series-number">113</span> November 5, 1948

“With this word, both of us thought of him.” Let us assume that each of us said the same words to ourselves – & surely, that is all it can mean. But would these very words not also just be a _seed_? They must belong to a language & to a context in order to truly be the expression of the thought of him.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#88b.6+89a.1) <span class="fac">[88b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88b.webp),[89a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-88b689a1}

<span class="series-number">114</span> It does happen that a word spoken aloud stands in the middle of an unexpressed train of thought. And this could be reported. Just as one can generally report that one has thought this & that at that time, without speaking. Whatever the interest of _this_ report is, it must also be the interest of that report; & thus also: one has thought of ‥‥ when saying the word ‥‥.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#89a.2) <span class="fac">[89a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-89a2}

<span class="series-number">115</span> “At that time, I thought: I wonder if he will come.” “You looked at it so doubtfully; what did you think?” – “I thought: I wonder if he will come.” – – “Did you say these words, or similar ones, in your mind at the time?” – “No. I was strangely thinking of Piccolomini, of the scene in which…”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#89a.3) <span class="fac">[89a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-89a3}

<span class="series-number">116</span> Speaking in the imagination is, in an important way, not comparable to speaking, but our language-games are similar to both. (Tennis with a ball & tennis without a ball.) And also, some mental image plays in them the role of a real picture, which can also stand in the _context_ of sentences & explanations.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#89a.4) <span class="fac">[89a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-89a4}

<span class="series-number">117</span> That is: our language-game refers to a mental image approximately as it refers to a spoken sentence. For this is also just a sequence of sounds and does not immediately refer to anything.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#89a.5+89b.1) <span class="fac">[89a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89a.webp),[89b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-89a589b1}

<span class="series-number">118</span> Now the question arises: If a word is used in a specific context, can one nevertheless, in one’s thought, establish a _different_ connection – if I don’t do this, nothing abnormal happens – does my thinking then follow along with my speech?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#89b.3) <span class="fac">[89b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-89b3}

<span class="series-number">119</span> If my thinking once deviates from the path of speech, it normally follows the path.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#89b.4) <span class="fac">[89b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-89b4}

<span class="series-number">120</span> If everything proceeds in its normal course, no one thinks of something that accompanies the speech.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#89b.5) <span class="fac">[89b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-89b5}

<span class="series-number">121</span> 06.11.1948

Philosophy is not a description of linguistic usage, and yet one can learn it by constantly paying attention to all the utterances of language.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#89b.6) <span class="fac">[89b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-89b6}

<span class="series-number">122</span> Knowledge, belief, hope, fear (and others) are all such different concepts that a classification, an arrangement into categories, is of no use to us. But we want to recognize the differences and similarities among them.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#89b.7) <span class="fac">[89b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-89b7}

<span class="series-number">123</span> Compare: “I believe that when you spoke of ‘N.’, you meant…” and “When you spoke of N., I knew that you meant…” Does the latter involve a specific experience? And why, then, the former?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#90a.1) <span class="fac">[90a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-90a1}

<span class="series-number">124</span> The expression: “It occurred to me.” “While reading, our conversation of yesterday occurred to me.” Does what I am reading also occur to me while I am reading attentively?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#90a.2) <span class="fac">[90a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-90a2}

<span class="series-number">125</span> “No, when I said ‘bank,’ I thought for a moment of our garden bench.” – Would I also say that the money-bank occurred to me if everything had proceeded normally?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#90a.3) <span class="fac">[90a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-90a3}

<span class="series-number">126</span> Imagine someone, whenever they make an utterance, tells us (always) about what was going on in their mind at the time. (It is a habit.) Would we be interested in this under all circumstances?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#90a.4) <span class="fac">[90a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-90a4}

<span class="series-number">127</span> “I mean, when I said ‘bank,’ I of course meant the bank that you are supposed to go to.” Did an experience of meaning have to accompany the word? (Nonsense!) – But why then, if I – against the context – thought of our garden bench at the time?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#90a.5) <span class="fac">[90a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-90a5}

<span class="series-number">128</span> “I have already remembered three times today that I have to write to N.” What importance does what happened at the time have?! – But on the other hand, what importance, what interest does the report itself have? It allows certain conclusions.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#90a.6+90b.1) <span class="fac">[90a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90a.webp),[90b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-90a690b1}

<span class="series-number">129</span> “It hadn’t completely slipped my mind; I’ve remembered it three times today.” – “Yes, I know: you got worked up about the word ‥‥ once.” – His state of mind is being examined, & it has certain consequences. Others, for example, would say: “It was _completely_ forgotten; I no longer thought about it.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#90b.2) <span class="fac">[90b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-90b2}

<span class="series-number">130</span> 07.11.1948

I pursued the word in _this_ direction. (So it is as if one were to indicate the tangent to a curve at this point.) But that is again only an image. (As if one were to describe tennis without a ball by means of tennis with a ball.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#90b.3) <span class="fac">[90b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-90b3}

<span class="series-number">131</span> The language-game “I mean (or meant) _that_” (a subsequent explanation of a word) is quite different from the language-game “I was thinking of ‥‥”. This is related to: “It reminded me of ‥‥”.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#90b.4) <span class="fac">[90b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-90b4}

<span class="series-number">132</span> Here, during the time of believing, remembering, or recalling, a characteristic _reaction_ is possible.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#90b.5) <span class="fac">[90b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-90b5}

<span class="series-number">133</span> What is the primitive reaction with which the language-game begins? Which can then be expressed in words. How does it come about that people use these words?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#90b.6) <span class="fac">[90b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-90b6}

<span class="series-number">134</span> “You said the word as if something else suddenly occurred to you.” One does not learn this reaction. The primitive reaction could also have been a word-reaction.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#90b.7+91a.1) <span class="fac">[90b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90b.webp),[91a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-90b791a1}

<span class="series-number">135</span> Suppose I am talking to someone about Dr. N. In the middle of the conversation, I say, “When I said the name ‘N,’ I was just thinking about Dr. N.” The other person would not understand me. If I had said, “I meant ‘N’ to refer to Dr. N., who…,” the answer might have been: “Of course. Who else could you have meant!” If I had said, “When I said the name ‘N,’ I now see Dr. N. before me,” that might not have been relevant.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#91a.2) <span class="fac">[91a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-91a2}

<span class="series-number">136</span> “When asked about the word, I thought of…” – If one were to answer the question “What was going on in your mind at the time?” with _nothing_, would that utterance be invalid? – He could have answered, “I forgot,” & it only _seemed_ to him as if he had ever known.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#91a.3) <span class="fac">[91a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-91a3}

<span class="series-number">137</span> Is “I wanted to give you to understand by this sign…” comparable to: “When I used to open my mouth, I wanted to say…?” That is, is that sentence the expression of a past intention?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#91a.4) <span class="fac">[91a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-91a4}

<span class="series-number">138</span> 08.11.1948

“I wanted to give you to understand by this sign that you should…” “This does not express a rule of signs (no agreement); but the purpose of that sign in my action.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#91a.6) <span class="fac">[91a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-91a6}

<span class="series-number">139</span> [→ ] “_This_ number is the correct continuation of this sequence.” By means of this expression, I can get someone to call this & that the “correct continuation” in the future. What ‘this & that’ is, I can only show by means of examples. – I.e., I can teach him to continue a sequence (basic sequence), to use an expression for the law of the sequence, rather as a substrate for the use of algebraic rules, or something similar.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#91b.1) <span class="fac">[91b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-91b1}

<span class="series-number">140</span> I can now, however, use the word “equal” when teaching the basic series, which he perhaps already knows in other contexts, i.e., in another, albeit related, meaning: it may turn out that he learns to form the basic series more easily if I say to him “You must always do that, always add that,” i.e., if I state a rule; but it does not yet function here as a rule, there is not yet any algebra.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#91b.2) <span class="fac">[91b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-91b2}

<span class="series-number">141</span> If there were a verb with the meaning: to believe falsely, it would not have a first person of the present indicative.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#91b.3) <span class="fac">[91b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-91b3}

<span class="series-number">142</span> “I believe he will come, but he certainly will not come.” If I say this to someone, it is the communication that he will not come, and that I am nevertheless permeated by the opposite, and will therefore behave accordingly to this belief. But, of course, I do not do that just by telling the other person that he will not come.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#91b.4) <span class="fac">[91b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-91b4}

<span class="series-number">143</span> “I certainly believe he will come, but he certainly will not come. Why tell someone that one believes something? In order to convince him of what one believes, or only to prepare him for one’s behavior in this matter?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#91b.5) <span class="fac">[91b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-91b5}

<span class="series-number">144</span> Consider: “He won’t come, but I will behave as if I believed he would come.” I could say this to someone, but in reality, I might be pathologically compelled to do this, so that it wouldn’t actually be pretending.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#92a.1) <span class="fac">[92a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/92a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-92a1}

One could express it like this: It’s not the case, but I _must_ believe it.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#92a.2) <span class="fac">[92a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/92a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-92a2}

<span class="series-number">145</span> I said that the sentence “I believe that it is so, & it is not so” could be true; namely, if I really believe it falsely; which is presumably possible. But what makes the sentence true? How can another person see that it is true? How does he know that I believe it? Not from my behavior; because that is contradictory.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#92a.3) <span class="fac">[92a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/92a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-92a3}

<span class="series-number">146</span> November 9, 1948. If I say: “Look! in this picture, this figure is contained.” – am I making a geometrical remark? – Isn’t ‘this picture’ what _this_ is the exact copy of? which could be described with the words ‥‥? Would it therefore make sense to say that it contains that figure _now_? or _has_ contained it? – My remark is therefore timeless, & one can call it “geometrical”.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#92a.4) <span class="fac">[92a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/92a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-92a4}

<span class="series-number">147</span> But what are the consequences for perceiving such a state of affairs? Imagine someone looks at a trick picture and finds the hidden figure. He then imagines that the picture has changed in such a way that the figure is now in it. He might say something like, “This figure is now here.” Or, conversely, the picture may have changed imperceptibly, & he only _believes_ that he has discovered something in it that has always been there.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#92a.5+92b.1) <span class="fac">[92a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/92a.webp),[92b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/92b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-92a592b1}

<span class="series-number">148</span> “Actually, one should refer to one’s own visual impression in the words ‘I see this,’ then one would really be pointing to what one sees.” A result of the intersection of different language-games. (Similarly: “‘this’ is the actual name.”)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#92b.2) <span class="fac">[92b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/92b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-92b2}

<span class="series-number">149</span> “I see now that these faces are not quite the same.” (Timeless sentence.) Of course, the eyes are also there for that! Imagine someone wants to say: “Yes, this is a perception by means of the sense of sight; but it does not describe my visual impressions.” What would these be? Well, I looked from one face to the other to compare them, and in doing so, I received a lot of visual impressions; or a continuously changing visual impression, something that one would like to represent with a film. But could we, in order to simplify the matter, not pick out two of all these, and couldn’t two visual impressions do this in the extreme case? And could these two now represent what I had noticed, namely the difference? Don’t we have a completely different game here?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#92b.3) <span class="fac">[92b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/92b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-92b3}

<span class="series-number">150</span> It is not accidental that I use so many interrogative sentences in this book.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#92b.4+93a.1) <span class="fac">[92b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/92b.webp),[93a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/93a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-92b493a1}

<span class="series-number">151</span> Should one now say here that the visual **impression**, the sense-datum, the visual object is something different? This concept does not seem to fit very well here. If one were to imagine seeing similarity or difference as something image-like, one would perhaps imagine it as _emphasized_ in an image; just as, in order to show someone where the difference between two pictures lies, one can strongly emphasize the difference. But whoever sees the emphasized features in both does not yet notice the difference between the pictures. One must look at the game as a whole, then one will see the difference.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#93a.2) <span class="fac">[93a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/93a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-93a2}

<span class="series-number">152</span> “I see that the two are similar” can be used both temporally & atemporally, depending on how ‘the two’ are defined. But do I therefore see something different each time? “I see” is always temporal, but “The two are similar” can be atemporal.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#93a.3) <span class="fac">[93a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/93a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-93a3}

<span class="series-number">153</span> But is it always clear in practical use whether the sentence is meant to be temporal or timeless? – It concerns two brothers; I meet them & then say “Yes, I see that they are similar.” Did I mean: these two people, M & N, are similar now. (Perhaps they weren’t before, etc.) – Or: I notice that these two human appearances, which can, for example, be captured in a picture, are similar. – If I had made the utterance & had been asked which I meant, could I definitely answer it?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#93a.4) <span class="fac">[93a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/93a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-93a4}

<span class="series-number">154</span> 10.11.1948

“You shouldn't draw his face” could mean: You shouldn't draw the face of this person, however it looks – or: You shouldn't draw these facial features that happen to be his now. Each time, it depends on something different. And the prohibition, understood in one way or another, has different constructions.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#93b.1) <span class="fac">[93b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/93b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-93b1}

<span class="series-number">155</span> Even if I say “There is a similarity between these (two) faces,” it could matter in different ways. For example, it could mean: There is a similarity between _this_ type of face & _this_ type of face, where the two types are characterized by descriptions. It may not be the faces _of these people_, or these face **shapes**, that are of interest, wherever I encounter them. The difference I have in mind is naturally that between the sense: These two lines have a similar shape – & the sense: Circle, ellipse, parabola & hyperbola have a similarity with one another.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#93b.2) <span class="fac">[93b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/93b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-93b2}

<span class="series-number">156</span> The difference is between external & internal similarity.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#93b.3) <span class="fac">[93b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/93b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-93b3}

<span class="series-number">157</span> If I now say of two faces that they are similar, does it make sense to ask “Do you mean the external, or the internal similarity”?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#93b.4) <span class="fac">[93b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/93b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-93b4}

<span class="series-number">158</span> “Are you interested in whether you can notice a similarity in these seemingly quite different _forms_?” “Do you want to say that there is something in common between these _forms_ – or between these _people_?” – But where is the difference? – Are you interested in the _forms_, or in the _people_? If in the forms, then you will perhaps trace them exactly, study the similarity of the lines & forget the people completely. If a discussion arises about this, it will be a geometric one, a discussion about types of lines.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#93b.5+94a.1) <span class="fac">[93b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/93b.webp),[94a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/94a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-93b594a1}

<span class="series-number">159</span> Suppose I were to trace the outlines of faces in order to explain similarity to someone, & he said, “Yes, these lines have a similarity; I can see that; but these faces don’t look like that….” Then I could reply: “Perhaps you’re right, but that’s not what matters to me. I wanted to say that a _such_ form & a _such_ form, however different they may look, ‥‥․” Here, I was concerned with a geometrical question. But if I had replied, “You’re right; I was mistaken,” then I would have been concerned with the similarity of _these people_.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#94a.2) <span class="fac">[94a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/94a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-94a2}

<span class="series-number">160</span> “These are siblings, but quite dissimilar” – I can see a similarity in them. What am I interested in here?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#94a.3) <span class="fac">[94a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/94a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-94a3}

<span class="series-number">161</span> Imagine there is an aesthetic rule according to which there must be a similarity between faces in a painting. I now point to two people & say to one “Take these as models for your picture; they have a similarity.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#94a.6) <span class="fac">[94a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/94a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-94a6}

<span class="series-number">162</span> But when I utter it on a specific occasion, is it always clear whether I wanted to allow the substitution or not? Must I have thought about it?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#94a.5) <span class="fac">[94a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/94a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-94a5}

<span class="series-number">163</span> It may be that I am interested in the fact that I see something in common in these lines that apparently have nothing in common. So, in my analytical gaze.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#94a.7) <span class="fac">[94a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/94a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-94a7}

<span class="series-number">164</span> 12.11.1948

“I see different things in a much more important sense than I see the same thing.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#94a.8+94b.1) <span class="fac">[94a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/94a.webp),[94b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/94b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-94a894b1}

<span class="series-number">165</span> A picture story. In one of the pictures, ducks appear; in another, rabbits; but one of the duck heads is drawn exactly like one of the rabbit heads. Someone looks at the pictures & it doesn't occur to him. When he describes them, he describes this form without hesitation, sometimes as one thing, sometimes as the other. Only when we draw his attention to it does amazement follow.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#94b.2) <span class="fac">[94b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/94b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-94b2}

<span class="series-number">166</span> He saw the aspects & yet not the change of aspect.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#94b.3) <span class="fac">[94b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/94b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-94b3}

<span class="series-number">167</span> When copying the two pictures, did he redraw the head differently each time? Not that I know! So, he saw them exactly the same both times.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#94b.4) <span class="fac">[94b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/94b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-94b4}

<span class="series-number">168</span> But did he imagine the same thing in both cases? – As far as I understand this question – _no_.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#94b.5) <span class="fac">[94b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/94b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-94b5}

<span class="series-number">169</span> In the change of aspect, one becomes _conscious_ of the aspect.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#94b.6) <span class="fac">[94b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/94b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-94b6}

<span class="series-number">170</span> Was it correct to say “He saw the two aspects, but not the change of aspect”? Shouldn’t I have said “He interpreted the picture in two ways, but did not see the change of aspect”? For him, the picture was initially like any picture of a duck: and when he saw an aspect here, he saw it in every picture, and also in every object. Then, did I examine every picture to see if it could not be seen in another way? – So, I will say: he did not _see_ the aspect; he interpreted the picture in this way or that way.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#95a.1) <span class="fac">[95a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/95a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-95a1}

<span class="series-number">171</span> The utterance of the experience of seeing the aspect is: “Now it is a …”.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#95a.2) <span class="fac">[95a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/95a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-95a2}

<span class="series-number">172</span> What is the philosophical importance of this phenomenon? Is it so much stranger than everyday experiences of seeing? Does it shed an unexpected light on them? – In his description, the problems concerning the concept of seeing become acute.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#95a.4) <span class="fac">[95a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/95a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-95a4}

<span class="series-number">173</span> And one can ask: When someone says “Now it is _a_ duck – – – now it is a rabbit!” – what happened first? He had not yet experienced the change, and yet he already says “_Now_ it is …”. Nothing _happened_; but he was already playing that game. You would have to look for something that distinguishes the experience of seeing when uttering the words “Now it is a duck” from the experience of seeing when uttering the words “That is a duck” (if someone knows nothing of aspects). And of course, nothing can be found. Because what should I say? – _when_ does the experience that interests me take place when uttering the words “Now … now …”? (Do we have _two_ extraordinary experiences here, or _three_?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#95a.5) <span class="fac">[95a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/95a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-95a5}

<span class="series-number">174</span> What is strange is actually the amazement; the question “How is it possible!” The expression of this is something like: “_The same_ – and _yet_ not the same”.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#95a.6+95b.1) <span class="fac">[95a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/95a.webp),[95b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/95b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-95a695b1}

<span class="series-number">175</span> The paradoxical manifests itself in something like laughter. But one could also imagine someone who would not laugh here; to whom nothing would seem paradoxical. And that person would also experience the change of aspect. He would now address the picture _so_, now _so_: and that would be all.
