### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#95b.2) <span class="fac">[95b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/95b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-95b2}

<span class="series-number">176</span> And what does he do? He now says, as an utterance of experience, what is otherwise his report of perception. (The great similarity to the experience of meaning.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#95b.3) <span class="fac">[95b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/95b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-95b3}

<span class="series-number">177</span> In what lies the similarity of seeing an aspect with thinking? That this seeing does not have the consequences of perception; that it is similar to imagining something.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#95b.4) <span class="fac">[95b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/95b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-95b4}

<span class="series-number">178</span> Suppose there were a sign language in which a duck head is a certain message, a rabbit head another. Someone who uses this code draws, unintentionally, a duck head in such a way that it can also be seen as a rabbit head. The recipient of the message gives it the wrong interpretation: this will be shown in his actions. But if he realizes that it can be seen _so_ and _so_, then he will not, depending on the aspect he is currently seeing, act differently.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#95b.5) <span class="fac">[95b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/95b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-95b5}

<span class="series-number">179</span> “Is it thinking? is it seeing?” – Does this not actually mean “Is it _interpreting_? is it seeing?” And interpreting is thinking; and it often causes a change of aspect. Can I say: Seeing the aspect is _related_ to interpreting? – The inclination was to say “It is as if I _see_ an _interpretation_”. No, the expression of this seeing _is_ related to the expression of interpreting.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#96a.1) <span class="fac">[96a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/96a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-96a1}

<span class="series-number">180</span> 13.11.1948

[→ ] Two uses of the report “I see …”. The one language-game: The observer reports what he sees from his post. – The other: The same objects are looked at by several people; one says: “I see a similarity between them”. In the first language-game, the report could, for example, have been “I see two people who are similar to each other, like father and son”. That is a much less complete description than, for example, that provided by an accurate drawing. But someone could give this more complete description and still not notice the similarity. And another could see the drawing and discover the similarity in it.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#96a.2) <span class="fac">[96a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/96a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-96a2}

<span class="series-number">181</span> There is a game: guessing thoughts. One variant of this would be: I speak a sentence in a language that A understands, but B does not understand. B must guess what I said. Another variant: I write down a sentence that the other person cannot see. He must guess it; or guess what it is about.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#96a.3) <span class="fac">[96a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/96a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-96a3}

<span class="series-number">182</span> To guess the intention: I write on a piece of paper, which the other person cannot see, that when the clock strikes, I will raise my left arm. The other person should guess what I will do at that time.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#96a.4+96b.1) <span class="fac">[96a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/96a.webp),[96b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/96b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-96a496b1}

<span class="series-number">183</span> “Only I can _know_ what I will do.” But I might be wrong; and can’t the other person predict it correctly? But usually the other person doesn’t _know_, and I often do. Likewise, the other person doesn’t know who I am writing to—if he doesn’t see it, or hear it from me; but I can say. When asked about the motives for my action, one usually asks _me_, not someone else. Likewise, when asked if I am in pain. That is part of the language-game.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#96b.2) <span class="fac">[96b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/96b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-96b2}

<span class="series-number">184</span> But would it be correct to say that my pains are hidden?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#96b.3) <span class="fac">[96b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/96b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-96b3}

<span class="series-number">185</span> For example, is the future hidden?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#96b.4) <span class="fac">[96b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/96b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-96b4}

<span class="series-number">186</span> “Nothing is so well hidden as the events of the future. One _cannot_ know them. Only what is happening now can be known.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#96b.5) <span class="fac">[96b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/96b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-96b5}

<span class="series-number">187</span> One can, of course, not be mistaken about immediate _experience_: but not because it is so certain. The language-game does not permit the senseless utterance, – although not the ‘false’ one.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#96b.6) <span class="fac">[96b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/96b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-96b6}

<span class="series-number">188</span> “The future cannot be known” is a grammatical observation about the concept ‘to know’. It says something similar to: “That is not knowing.” And now one could ask: Why should someone be tempted to draw _this_ line of demarcation? And the answer is: Because of the uncertainty of predictions.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#96b.7) <span class="fac">[96b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/96b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-96b7}

<span class="series-number">189</span> “The future cannot be known? – What about solar and lunar eclipses?” – One can’t really know them.” – “Know? – how, for _example_?”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#96b.8) <span class="fac">[96b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/96b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-96b8}

<span class="series-number">190</span> 14.11.1948

[→ ] If a lion could speak, we could not understand him.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#97a.1) <span class="fac">[97a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/97a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-97a1}

<span class="series-number">191</span> Even if someone expressed everything ‘that is in his inner being’, we would not necessarily understand him.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#97a.2) <span class="fac">[97a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/97a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-97a2}

<span class="series-number">192</span> He becomes angry when we see no reason for it; what excites us leaves him calm. – Is the essential difference that we cannot predict his reactions? – Could it not be that we know them after some experience, but cannot cope with them?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#97a.3) <span class="fac">[97a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/97a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-97a3}

<span class="series-number">193</span> He behaves like someone for whom complex processes of _thinking_ are taking place; & if I could only understand them, I would understand him. Let us consider this case; & now he expresses his thoughts to himself, & in a certain sense I understand his action. That is to say; I see the chains of thought & know how they lead to the actions.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#97a.4) <span class="fac">[97a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/97a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-97a4}

<span class="series-number">194</span> He may have ceased to be a mystery to me.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#97a.5) <span class="fac">[97a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/97a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-97a5}

<span class="series-number">195</span> Think of the mysteriousness of the dream. Such a mystery does not necessarily have to have a solution. It intrigues us. It is as if there were a mystery here. This could, after all, be a primitive _reaction_.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#97a.6) <span class="fac">[97a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/97a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-97a6}

<span class="series-number">196</span> It is as if there were an enigma here; but it does not have to be an enigma. (“All forms are similar, and none is like the other, and thus the chorus suggests a secret law.”)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#97a.7+97b.1) <span class="fac">[97a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/97a.webp),[97b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/97b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-97a797b1}

<span class="series-number">197</span> I don’t know what is going on in him. I could not supplement his thoughts.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#97b.2) <span class="fac">[97b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/97b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-97b2}

<span class="series-number">198</span> He is incomprehensible to me, which means: I cannot interact with him in _the same way_ as I interact with others.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#97b.4) <span class="fac">[97b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/97b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-97b4}

<span class="series-number">199</span> 15.11.1948

Whoever is suspicious of a mathematical result will suspect the _calculation_. But isn’t this just a method? Whoever is suspicious of the immediate expression of experience and does not think that the other person is lying will say that he does not know what he is saying, that he is dreaming, or not in his right mind.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#97b.5) <span class="fac">[97b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/97b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-97b5}

<span class="series-number">200</span> 16.11.1948

[→ ] But how do I know what I would do _if_…? Perhaps, if I went out into the street and found everything different than I have _ever_ seen, I would cheerfully go along with it. I would behave completely differently than I have ever behaved. And yet there is something important in my remark.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#97b.6) <span class="fac">[97b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/97b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-97b6}

<span class="series-number">201</span> Even among people who all had the same facial features, we could not find each other.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#97b.7) <span class="fac">[97b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/97b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-97b7}

<span class="series-number">202</span> A people: with a ruling class, whose individuals (except for sexual characteristics) all look the same, and an oppressed class that has our variability of shape and facial features.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#98a.1) <span class="fac">[98a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/98a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-98a1}

<span class="series-number">203</span> 17.11.1948

A tribe that does not know the concept of feigned pain. When someone among them expresses pain, they are pitied. They are unfamiliar with the distrustful attitude toward the expression of pain. The traveler who comes to them from us often thinks that a complaint is exaggerated, that it is false, and only serves to elicit pity; the natives do not seem to think so. (In their language, there is an expression that corresponds, at least approximately, to our “having pain.”) A missionary teaches the children our language; he also trains them, and through him, they learn to distinguish between genuine and feigned expressions of pain. Because he distrusts the expression of pain and rejects it, and he teaches the children to be distrustful. – They learn our expression “having pain,” also “feigning pain,” – has a new concept of pain been taught to them? I certainly would not say that they only now know what pain is. Because that would mean that they never had pain before.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#98b.2+99a.1) <span class="fac">[98b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/98b.webp),[99a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/99a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-98b299a1}

A tribe that doesn't know the concept of feigned pain. When someone among them expresses pain, they are cared for and pitied. They don't know a distrustful attitude toward the expression of pain. The traveler who comes to them from us often thinks that a complaint is exaggerated, that it is false & only calculated to evoke pity; the natives don't seem to think so. In their language, there is an expression that, at least to some extent, corresponds to ours: “to have pain”. – A missionary teaches these people our language; he also trains them, & they learn from him to distinguish between genuine & feigned expressions of pain. Because _he_ distrusts some expressions & doesn't accept them, & he teaches the people this attitude. They learn our expression “to have pain”, also “to feign pain” – have they received a new concept of pain? I certainly won't say that they only now know what pain is. Because that would mean that they never had pain before. But they had to receive new training for the use of our words. This (training) was similar, but not the same as the old one.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#98a.2) <span class="fac">[98a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/98a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-98a2}

<span class="series-number">204</span> Had these people overlooked something, and did the teacher draw their attention to it?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#99a.2) <span class="fac">[99a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/99a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-99a2}

<span class="series-number">205</span> And how could the difference not come to their consciousness when they complained with pain at one time and without pain at another? Should I say: they always thought it was the same? – Certainly not. Or: no difference occurred to them? – But why not: it was not important to them?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#98a.3+98b.1) <span class="fac">[98a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/98a.webp),[98b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/98b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-98a398b1}

And how could the difference not come to their consciousness when they complained with pain at one time and without pain at another? But did the difference have to be as important to them as it is to us? (Some people tell untrue stories in company; others know that they are untrue, but they accept them as if they were true. They take no notice of the difference.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#99a.3) <span class="fac">[99a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/99a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-99a3}

<span class="series-number">206</span> [→ ] November 18, 1948 “If a concept relates to a specific life pattern, then there must be some indeterminacy in it.” I think of it like this: we would have a regularly repeating pattern on a strip, & _on_ this pattern, as a base, an irregular drawing or painting that we describe in relation to the pattern, because this relationship is what is important to us. If the pattern were to run: a b c a b c a b c, etc., then I would, for example, have a specific concept for the fact that something red falls on a _c_ & something green on the next _b_. If anomalies now occur in the pattern, I will probably have doubts about which judgment to make. But could there not be something provided for in my instruction? Or do I _simply assume_ that the specific pattern was a prerequisite in the training that teaches us the concept & was never described itself?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#99a.4) <span class="fac">[99a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/99a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-99a4}

<span class="series-number">207</span> [→ ] If colors played a different role in the world of humans than in our world, what would be the consequences for the concepts of color? This is actually a question of natural science, and I do not want to ask such a question. Rather: what consequences would seem plausible to us? What consequences would not surprise us?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#99b.1) <span class="fac">[99b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/99b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-99b1}

<span class="series-number">208</span> If colors played a different role in the world of humans than in ours, what kind of color concepts, different from ours, would not surprise us? Consider different cases. The question is not yet properly formulated; but what is its purpose? –

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#99b.2) <span class="fac">[99b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/99b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-99b2}

<span class="series-number">209</span> Other concepts than ours are so difficult to imagine because certain very general facts of nature are not conscious to us, and it does not occur to us to imagine them differently than they are. But if we do this, then it is no longer difficult for us to find ourselves in other concepts.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#99b.4) <span class="fac">[99b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/99b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-99b4}

<span class="series-number">210</span> Our concept of pure future – “It will happen” – as opposed to “It wants to happen” & “It should happen.” Must every people have this concept, which, as it were, conceives of time spatially?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#100a.3) <span class="fac">[100a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/100a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-100a3}

<span class="series-number">211</span> If a pattern of life is the basis for the use of a word, then there must be an indeterminacy in it. For the pattern of life is not an exact regularity.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#100a.4) <span class="fac">[100a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/100a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-100a4}

<span class="series-number">212</span> For someone who only counts on his fingers, for whom 5 is the hand, 10 is the whole person, and who then counts the people again on his fingers, etc., the decimal system will not be an arbitrary number system. It is not a method of counting for him, but the counting itself.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#100a.5) <span class="fac">[100a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/100a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-100a5}

<span class="series-number">213</span> Six pure colors. Must it seem that way to us? – Brown is not one of them. But what does that tell us at all? Where do we need something like this? When we describe things according to their colors? – Yes, if we do it in a general way, for example. “Brown is not one of them” can be the expression of an instinctive rejection of a color combination.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#100a.6+100b.1) <span class="fac">[100a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/100a.webp),[100b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/100b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-100a6100b1}

<span class="series-number">214</span> “The _light_ is white. Colors are already a shadow.” – But is everything really ‘light’ white? Doesn’t the lamp give off light? – Where does this first sentence come from, which sounds so obvious? And why does it sound obvious?) – We always call the brightest thing white. If one of two colors is brighter, then only it can be the white one. And brightness & light are equated here.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#100b.2) <span class="fac">[100b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/100b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-100b2}

<span class="series-number">215</span> It seems to us that there is a concept of color mixing that is above all the physical methods of color mixing. So that we can say of such a method: it represents the ‘pure’ color mixing most closely, for example.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#100b.3) <span class="fac">[100b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/100b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-100b3}

<span class="series-number">216</span> We judge whether, according to our concept, the two colors a & b really _should_ give the color c.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#100b.4) <span class="fac">[100b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/100b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-100b4}

<span class="series-number">217</span> How did we come to this concept? That is actually irrelevant.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#100b.5) <span class="fac">[100b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/100b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-100b5}

<span class="series-number">218</span> “Several shadows together give light” – this idea could already seem like a hellish distortion of the truth.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#100b.6) <span class="fac">[100b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/100b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-100b6}

<span class="series-number">219</span> Could one also perceive all colors as mixtures of white & black? – If, for example, the white & black pigment gave red, green, etc., under certain circumstances, perhaps. One might say: “The light brings out the red from the black.” (So one imagines the color hidden in the black.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#100b.7+101a.1) <span class="fac">[100b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/100b.webp),[101a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/101a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-100b7101a1}

<span class="series-number">220</span> Red & green are the same. I imagine that there is only _one_ shade of red & one of green. In nature, the two always blend into each other (as in autumn in certain leaves). They are found everywhere together, one being a variation of the other. Their difference plays no other role than that of light & dark. But do people not see the difference?! Of course. But they might have a word “leaf color” which is used somewhat analogously to our color names & which denotes red _or_ green, & two distinguishing words, “sharp” & “dull,” analogous to our “light” & “dark,” which differentiate red from green. And now the question is: which of their concepts is similar to our concept of color, their concept of ‘leaf color’ or their concept of ‘sharp leaf color’ (i.e., red), for example? (If it is a matter of dyeing or painting an object, they might say that they want it to be leaf-colored. If asked whether it should be sharp or dull, they might answer that it is all the same to them.) Or would these people then be colorblind? Well, if we teach them our language, they prove to be able to see colors.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#101a.2) <span class="fac">[101a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/101a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-101a2}

<span class="series-number">221</span> The difference between red & green does not have the _importance_ for them that it has for us.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#101a.4+101b.1) <span class="fac">[101a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/101a.webp),[101b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/101b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-101a4101b1}

<span class="series-number">222</span> If we introduce them to a greater variety of colors, they might perceive our system as the only natural one, i.e., they might adopt it & abandon the other one without difficulty. Perhaps not.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#101b.2) <span class="fac">[101b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/101b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-101b2}

<span class="series-number">223</span> A painting in which the light side of the bodies is always painted green, the shadows red.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#101b.3) <span class="fac">[101b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/101b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-101b3}

<span class="series-number">224</span> Could we imagine that people have a concept of pretense that is different from ours? – But would it then be the concept of pretense? – Well, it could be a concept related to ours.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#101b.4) <span class="fac">[101b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/101b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-101b4}

<span class="series-number">225</span> But are there not more essential & less essential features of (such) a concept? I.e.: If one changes _this_, one will still call it “pretense,” – if one changes _this_, not anymore. And _behavior_ here means an attitude.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#101b.5) <span class="fac">[101b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/101b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-101b5}

<span class="series-number">226</span> People whose faces immediately betray their feelings to others hide it when they want to pretend.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#101b.6) <span class="fac">[101b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/101b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-101b6}

<span class="series-number">227</span> People do not say that one cannot look into the interior, into the heart, but that one cannot read the features if they are covered.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#101b.7) <span class="fac">[101b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/101b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-101b7}

<span class="series-number">228</span> “One cannot see into his heart.” The question is: Can _he_? (_That_ determines the concept.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#101b.8+102a.1) <span class="fac">[101b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/101b.webp),[102a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/102a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-101b8102a1}

<span class="series-number">229</span> “One cannot look into a person’s heart.” However, the underlying assumption is that the person themselves can. – Is this something that experience teaches us? Yes & no – I would answer.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#101b.8+102a.1.2) <span class="fac">[101b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/101b.webp),[102a\[1\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/102a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-101b8102a12}

<span class="series-number">230</span> And there must be a reason for that.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#102a.2) <span class="fac">[102a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/102a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-102a2}

<span class="series-number">231</span> “He could tell me about himself what I would not otherwise know.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#102a.3) <span class="fac">[102a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/102a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-102a3}

<span class="series-number">232</span> This is certain: He can predict movements of his body that I cannot predict. And if I predict his actions, then I do so in a different way.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#102a.4) <span class="fac">[102a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/102a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-102a4}

<span class="series-number">233</span> And is _that_ an empirical fact? or: What is _it_ that I am talking about here? For example, I cannot move his arm arbitrarily, as I can move my own. But what is meant by this is not entirely easy to explain.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#102a.5) <span class="fac">[102a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/102a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-102a5}

<span class="series-number">234</span> I cannot know what he is planning in his heart. But suppose he always made written plans; how important would they be? If, for example, he never followed them. –

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#102a.6) <span class="fac">[102a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/102a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-102a6}

<span class="series-number">235</span> Perhaps one would say: Then they are not really plans. But they would not be plans even if they were _in_ him, & seeing into him would not be of any use to me.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#102a.7) <span class="fac">[102a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/102a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-102a7}

<span class="series-number">236</span> 20.11.1948

“Don’t you see that he is in pain!” – “Pain _there_? Why?” He would not understand what it means that the other _has_ pain.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#102a.9+102b.1) <span class="fac">[102a\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/102a.webp),[102b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/102b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-102a9102b1}

<span class="series-number">237</span> What if one had been taught in youth that plants feel pain; later, however, he no longer believes it. – How would this transition take place? He throws away the idea, as if it were a shell that no longer fits.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#102b.2) <span class="fac">[102b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/102b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-102b2}

<span class="series-number">238</span> How would one act who does not ‘believe’ that the other person feels pain? One can imagine that. He treats him as something inanimate, or as most people treat the most unhumanlike animals. (Jellyfish, for example.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#102b.3) <span class="fac">[102b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/102b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-102b3}

<span class="series-number">239</span> We all know the doctor’s question, “Is he in pain?” Also, the uncertainty as to whether a person under anesthesia, who groans, is actually feeling pain; but the philosophical question of whether the other (person) feels (something) is of a completely different kind; it is not the doubt applied to each individual in a specific case. [The point of this sentence has not come across.]

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#102b.4) <span class="fac">[102b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/102b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-102b4}

<span class="series-number">240</span> Do we encounter this doubt in ordinary life? No. But perhaps something similar: indifference to the other person’s expression of pain.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#102b.5) <span class="fac">[102b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/102b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-102b5}

<span class="series-number">241</span> Are the fictitious cases with which I try to grapple not like calculation problems? (And how would you solve _this_ equation? & how this one?)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#103a.2) <span class="fac">[103a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/103a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-103a2}

<span class="series-number">242</span> November 21, 1948

Believing that this person or that person is not in pain because he does not express it, or because he is only pretending, or because he is under anesthesia, has different _reasons_ than the belief that an amoeba does not feel pain, and also different reasons than those of the fictitious inhuman person who sees the expressions of pain in his surroundings as phenomena in inanimate things. Would this person even say that he _believes_ that they do not feel pain? – Perhaps. But would he mean the same thing as the doctor, for example, who reassures me about the patient’s condition? The utterance – however he may have learned it – is in a different context for him; even if some of its consequences are similar.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#103a.3) <span class="fac">[103a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/103a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-103a3}

<span class="series-number">243</span> “The _uncertainty_ as to whether the other person is in pain” – does it lie in the fact that he _is_ him and I am me? (But ask yourself: “Can he know? He has no point of comparison.”) No; _here_ I am being misled by a picture. The uncertainty is from case to case, and the fluctuation of the concept. But this is our game – we play it with an _elastic_ tool.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#103b.1) <span class="fac">[103b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/103b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-103b1}

<span class="series-number">244</span> Could there not be people who have never encountered the situation of feeling this uncertainty?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#103b.2) <span class="fac">[103b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/103b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-103b2}

<span class="series-number">245</span> “So, I should be uncertain because he is him and I am me? What on earth do you mean?” they would say.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#103b.3) <span class="fac">[103b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/103b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-103b3}

<span class="series-number">246</span> And could people play with a rigid concept? – Then it would be different from ours in a strange way. Because where all concepts are elastic, in the changes of life, a rigid concept would seem odd.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#103b.4) <span class="fac">[103b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/103b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-103b4}

<span class="series-number">247</span> Must not every concept of behaviour also be somewhat vague in order to be able to serve, in a rough way, in the game with such concepts?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#103b.5) <span class="fac">[103b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/103b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-103b5}

<span class="series-number">248</span> There could be someone who would be in serious, hopeless doubt about the other person. But how would he act? (Like a mentally disturbed person.) He might say: Sometimes I feel that the other person and I are the same, and sometimes that we are not. And accordingly, he would sometimes show compassion, sometimes not, sometimes even the doubt.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#103b.6+104a.1) <span class="fac">[103b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/103b.webp),[104a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/104a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-103b6104a1}

<span class="series-number">249</span> The behaviour of a person is unpredictable, not calculable. Suppose it were. I would have made the calculation and now I would observe his actions (like the movements of complicated machines). If that were the case, would it be possible for him to observe them with empathy? Would it be impossible for him to say, “One cannot know what is going on in them”? For example, if he says to himself, “That’s just how people are. I am exactly the same.” It would be possible that he would then look at his _calculation_ with new eyes.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#104a.4) <span class="fac">[104a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/104a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-104a4}

<span class="series-number">250</span> Why do we only _play_ this game! – But what is one asking about here? About its surroundings, not about its causes.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#104a.5) <span class="fac">[104a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/104a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-104a5}

<span class="series-number">251</span> “Where I am certain, he is uncertain.” If that were also the case in a calculation –.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#104a.6) <span class="fac">[104a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/104a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-104a6}

<span class="series-number">252</span> November 22, 1948

[→ ] “Could he not be pretending?” – But could he not only _imagine_ that he is pretending? (Wouldn’t this be conceivable? & it is the conceivability that matters here, not the probability.) Pretending is only a special case; only under special circumstances can we interpret behaviour as pretending.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#104a.7+104b.1) <span class="fac">[104a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/104a.webp),[104b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/104b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-104a7104b1}

<span class="series-number">253</span> The concept of ‘pretending’ is connected to the events and situations of pretending. So, can not all behaviour, under all circumstances, be pretending?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#104b.3) <span class="fac">[104b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/104b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-104b3}

<span class="series-number">254</span> But isn’t the concept precisely of such a kind that one can construct a further environment for every kind of behaviour, etc., in which even this would be a behaviour of pretending? Isn’t, for example, every detective story based on this?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#104b.4) <span class="fac">[104b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/104b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-104b4}

<span class="series-number">255</span> One could also say: the concept of pretending is connected to a _practical_ problem. And the blurred boundary of the concept does not change anything about that.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#104b.5) <span class="fac">[104b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/104b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-104b5}

<span class="series-number">256</span> Already recognizing the philosophical problem as a logical one is progress; it brings with it the right attitude & the method.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#104b.6) <span class="fac">[104b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/104b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-104b6}

<span class="series-number">257</span> “Theoretically, it could be pretending”: What does that mean?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#104b.7) <span class="fac">[104b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/104b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-104b7}

<span class="series-number">258</span> It must mean: the _concept_ of pretending allows it.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#104b.8+105a.1) <span class="fac">[104b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/104b.webp),[105a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/105a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-104b8105a1}

<span class="series-number">259</span> And _that_ means: If I now learn this & that & the other, I might say that it was pretending. (Euclidean geometry.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#105a.3) <span class="fac">[105a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/105a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-105a3}

But where does it say that one would say that; or how do I conclude it?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#105a.5) <span class="fac">[105a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/105a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-105a5}

‘To the extent that this concept is _defined_, it also allows _that_.’

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#105a.2) <span class="fac">[105a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/105a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-105a2}

<span class="series-number">260</span> But here we are creating a false picture of our concept.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#105a.4) <span class="fac">[105a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/105a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-105a4}

<span class="series-number">261</span> The concept of ‘pretending’ serves practical purposes.

### [Ms-167](/ms-167/#13v.3) <span class="fac">[13v\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-167/13v.webp)</span> {#ms-167-13v3}

<span class="series-number">262</span> (The motive, the reason, etc., belong to ‘pretending’.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#105a.7) <span class="fac">[105a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/105a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-105a7}

<span class="series-number">263</span> A drama, for example, shows you what cases of pretending look like.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#105a.8) <span class="fac">[105a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/105a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-105a8}

<span class="series-number">264</span> One could now imagine the typical appearances of pretending in a varied way. The dramas of these other people would then unfold quite differently. And we would not understand them at all. What is completely unmotivated for us would seem natural to them.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#105a.9) <span class="fac">[105a\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/105a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-105a9}

<span class="series-number">265</span> (For example, the way Orestes behaves towards the king, by pointing to his sword, etc., might seem completely nonsensical to people.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#105a.10+105b.1) <span class="fac">[105a\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/105a.webp),[105b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/105b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-105a10105b1}

<span class="series-number">266</span> A spectacle involving these people would be incomprehensible to us. (And is Greek tragedy understandable to us?) And what does ‘understanding’ mean here?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#105b.2) <span class="fac">[105b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/105b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-105b2}

<span class="series-number">267</span> A sharper concept would not be the same concept. That is to say: the sharper concept would not have the _value_ of the vague one for us. Precisely because we would not understand people who are completely certain of their cause where we cannot be.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#105b.3) <span class="fac">[105b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/105b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-105b3}

<span class="series-number">268</span> November 23, 1948

Couldn’t someone, in order to show that he understands what ‘pretending’ is, invent stories in which pretending occurs? In order to develop the concept of pretending, he invents ever more complicated stories. For example, what looks like a confession is only another form of pretending; what looks like pretending is only a façade to conceal the real pretending; etc. etc. etc. The concept is thus embedded in a kind of stories.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#105b.4+106a.1) <span class="fac">[105b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/105b.webp),[106a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/106a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-105b4106a1}

<span class="series-number">269</span> And the stories are constructed according to the principle that _everything_ can be pretending. This naturally includes the fact that in every story something is characterized as the true underlying cause. And how is the true underlying cause to be characterized as such? Perhaps in the form of monologues. These must not be audible, otherwise they could be part of the deception. – But couldn’t someone hold mental monologues, just because they give him a certain appearance that he wants to use for deception? – So, is the _intention_ the underlying cause? And how can that emerge in the story?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#106a.2) <span class="fac">[106a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/106a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-106a2}

<span class="series-number">270</span> The concept of pretending serves practical problems. That is to say: if the one who is hatching dark plans does nothing but good & wonderful things until he commits the dark deed, then even that will only be ‘theoretically’ pretending; because it no longer looks like pretending & the conclusions that one would normally draw from dark machinations do not apply here.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#106a.4) <span class="fac">[106a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/106a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-106a4}

<span class="series-number">271</span> And what have I achieved with all this? In the explanation of the _concept_, I have put the use in place of the picture.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#106a.5) <span class="fac">[106a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/106a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-106a5}

<span class="series-number">272</span> “The word W. has two meanings” means: it has two kinds of use. What does this sentence tell one? Under what circumstances is it used? Someone knows the word “bank” only in _one_ sense; I tell him: it has another. (Namely‥‥‥) He already knows every use of the word, but he suddenly becomes puzzled, he doesn't know what's what, & I explain to him: “The word has two uses: ‥‥․”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#106a.6) <span class="fac">[106a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/106a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-106a6}

<span class="series-number">273</span> In this concept of meaning, there is some indeterminacy.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#106b.1) <span class="fac">[106b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/106b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-106b1}

<span class="series-number">274</span> One doesn't say, for example, that “go” & “goes” have different meanings. They mean exactly the same thing, one would tell him; namely _that_ – & then one would demonstrate the act of walking to him.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#106b.2) <span class="fac">[106b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/106b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-106b2}

<span class="series-number">275</span> You come to a tribe, they have a language; in this language you hear a word (a sound) – – does it have _one_ meaning, or several? How will you decide?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#106b.3) <span class="fac">[106b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/106b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-106b3}

<span class="series-number">276</span> Sometimes the decision will certainly be quite easy & clear. [But always?]

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#106b.4) <span class="fac">[106b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/106b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-106b4}

<span class="series-number">277</span> I don't give an inch. “He has no hair on his head.” Does “hair” have the same meaning in both sentences? – And does “a little bit” mean a small bite? – “In one case, one is still conscious of the old meaning, in the other, not.” And this sentence does not refer to a consciousness when uttering the word, but rather to an explanation that one would give, or not give, if… That is, to connections that would, or would not, be made.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#106b.5) <span class="fac">[106b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/106b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-106b5}

<span class="series-number">278</span> What is the correct translation of an English pun into German? Perhaps a completely different pun.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#107a.1) <span class="fac">[107a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/107a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-107a1}

<span class="series-number">279</span> 24.11.1948

What do you achieve with the decision that the word has only one meaning, or not only one? You can learn its use without deciding that (without thinking about it).

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#107a.2) <span class="fac">[107a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/107a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-107a2}

<span class="series-number">280</span> If you say it has two meanings, then you must separate them by an explanation. (This can have various purposes.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#107a.3) <span class="fac">[107a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/107a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-107a3}

<span class="series-number">281</span> But the distinction may be obvious, or not.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#107a.4) <span class="fac">[107a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/107a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-107a4}

<span class="series-number">282</span> It may already be made when one is first learning to speak, or only by someone who researches the grammar of the language.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#107a.5) <span class="fac">[107a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/107a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-107a5}

<span class="series-number">283</span> (You must start here from the living language.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#107a.6) <span class="fac">[107a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/107a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-107a6}

<span class="series-number">284</span> The differentiation of ways of use has various purposes.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#107a.7+107b.1) <span class="fac">[107a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/107a.webp),[107b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/107b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-107a7107b1}

<span class="series-number">285</span> I look at the language & say “different words are used in quite different ways”. But also: “These have similar uses.” Yes: “These (here) have the same.” And further: “This word has two quite different uses.” But also: “It has two different, & yet similar uses.” – And so far I describe what I notice. (That is, there is no problem yet.) (So far I am still quite naive.) In each meaning, there is always an explanation of the meaning. And the explanations can differ in their nature and also be similar in various ways. [An explanation of “go” and of “gone.”] The differences can be more primitive, and less primitive.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#107b.2) <span class="fac">[107b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/107b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-107b2}

<span class="series-number">286</span> You come to a new situation when you consider _several_ languages & compare them with each other.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#107b.3) <span class="fac">[107b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/107b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-107b3}

<span class="series-number">287</span> The explanation of some word uses will seem simple, concise, original to us; another: artificial, arbitrary, pointless.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#107b.4) <span class="fac">[107b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/107b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-107b4}

<span class="series-number">288</span> “We _need_ a word to designate this object, this tool; but why a word that designates this one every Monday, that one every Tuesday, etc.?” Does this word have _one_ meaning, or seven?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#107b.5) <span class="fac">[107b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/107b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-107b5}

<span class="series-number">289</span> Not every _use_, you want to say, is a meaning.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#107b.6) <span class="fac">[107b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/107b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-107b6}

<span class="series-number">290</span> Does this word have _one_ function in our lives, or does it have seven functions? There is a model for one function. And what is related to this is called that. (An unclear concept.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#107b.7) <span class="fac">[107b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/107b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-107b7}

<span class="series-number">291</span> Meaning, function, purpose, use – related concepts.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#107b.8+108a.1) <span class="fac">[107b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/107b.webp),[108a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/108a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-107b8108a1}

<span class="series-number">292</span> Imagine the hypothesis: a person never remembers a dream properly, he forgets the thought of the dream immediately upon waking & only retains the images in his memory that accompanied it. The story is lost & only the illustrations remain.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#108a.2) <span class="fac">[108a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/108a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-108a2}

<span class="series-number">293</span> Imagine this: in a story, we replace every tenth word with the word “table.” Now, in a language, a word has the use that the word “table” has in that story. How could we describe the use of such a fluctuating word? Or what would it mean to “teach someone the use of this word”?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#108a.3) <span class="fac">[108a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/108a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-108a3}

<span class="series-number">294</span> What am I getting at? That the description of a word use is the description of a system, or of systems. But I have no definition of what a system is.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#108a.4) <span class="fac">[108a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/108a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-108a4}

<span class="series-number">295</span> I am dealing with people who use a fluctuating word in their language.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#108a.5) <span class="fac">[108a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/108a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-108a5}

<span class="series-number">296</span> If they had _only_ vague words, then it simply wouldn’t be language.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#108a.6) <span class="fac">[108a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/108a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-108a6}

<span class="series-number">297</span> I imagine a person who, in a completely naive way (without any philosophical preconceptions), looks at the varieties of word use & describes them for themselves. For example, they might classify the word that means something different every day of the week as a normal noun, & it would not occur to them to ask “Does this have _one_ function, or several?”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#108a.7) <span class="fac">[108a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/108a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-108a7}

<span class="series-number">298</span> The question does not even occur to him: “Do ‘non’ and ‘ne’ have the same meaning?”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#108b.1) <span class="fac">[108b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/108b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-108b1}

<span class="series-number">299</span> 25.11.1948

But now he also compares his language with the primitive language that is learned by someone who comes as a stranger among people who do not understand him. This person learns individual important words through demonstrations of various kinds. Every word is accompanied by a showing, a demonstration (a scene). The meaning of negation is also, of course, demonstrated. (Whether in the command “Don’t do that!” or in the communication.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#108b.2) <span class="fac">[108b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/108b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-108b2}

<span class="series-number">300</span> In this language, the exact endings of the words will not matter. (Or: this language has no inflection.) The demonstrations distinguish the use of one word from the use of another, but they do not, for example, distinguish “goes” from “go.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#108b.3) <span class="fac">[108b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/108b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-108b3}

<span class="series-number">301</span> And now we could introduce a concept of ‘meaning’ into our description of language, such that two words have the same meaning if the same demonstration explains them in that primitive language.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#108b.4) <span class="fac">[108b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/108b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-108b4}

<span class="series-number">302</span> One can therefore ask: If a stranger comes to the people who say “non” and “ne,” at what stage will the difference be taught to him? Certainly not at first; he will learn a negation that does not recognize the difference.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#109a.1) <span class="fac">[109a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/109a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-109a1}

<span class="series-number">303</span> Suppose I said, ‘Meaning’ is the primitive function of a word – would that be true?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#109a.2) <span class="fac">[109a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/109a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-109a2}

<span class="series-number">304</span> And of course, this concept is extremely vague.

_[→ ]_

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#111a.2) <span class="fac">[111a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-111a2}

28.11.1948

[→ ] But is, for example, the primitive function of negation in the account and of defense in the command (“Don’t do that!”) the same? – What one will call the same function, and what not, will depend on human nature. As will, of course, also: what is necessity, and what is not.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#109a.3) <span class="fac">[109a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/109a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-109a3}

<span class="series-number">305</span> 26.11.1948

The words “the rose is red” are senseless if the word “is” has the meaning of “is equal to.” – Does this mean: if you utter that sentence & at the same time _mean_ “is” as a sign of equality, then its sense collapses?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#109a.4) <span class="fac">[109a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/109a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-109a4}

<span class="series-number">306</span> We take a sentence & explain to someone the meaning of each of its words; he learns to use them, & therefore also that sentence. If, instead of the sentence, we had chosen a nonsensical string of words, he would not learn to use _it_. And if one explains the word “is” as a sign of identity, then he does not learn to use the sequence of words “the rose is red”.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#109a.5) <span class="fac">[109a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/109a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-109a5}

<span class="series-number">307</span> And yet it is true that if one thinks of “equal” when hearing the word “is,” the sense of that sentence falls apart in the mind. Similar to when someone, upon hearing “Hey, hey!”, thinks of two eggs. One could tell someone: If you want to say the exclamation “Hey, hey!” expressively, you must not think of an egg at the same time!

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#109b.1) <span class="fac">[109b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/109b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-109b1}

<span class="series-number">308</span> _---_

What makes my _image_ of him an image of _him_? If I say “I am now imagining him as he ‥‥,” then nothing more is meant than his portrait. But can I not come to realize that I had a completely wrong image of him? Is my question not like this: “What makes this sentence one that is about _him_?” “That we are talking about him.” – And what makes our conversation one about _him_?” – Certain transitions that we have made, or would make.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#109b.2) <span class="fac">[109b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/109b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-109b2}

<span class="series-number">309</span> 27.11.1948

What makes this _image_ his portrait? – It is designated as such in the catalog.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#109b.3) <span class="fac">[109b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/109b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-109b3}

<span class="series-number">310</span> Suppose, instead of imagining something, I sketched it on a piece of paper. So, I am talking about N. & my pencil is sketching a figure on the paper. One could then ask me, “Does that depict N.?” And it might depict him, whether it resembles him or not. Is it correct to say: It is similar with the image. Certainly; insofar as one can sometimes draw what one has imagined.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#109b.4) <span class="fac">[109b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/109b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-109b4}

<span class="series-number">311</span> The question “What makes this an image _of_ him?” normally doesn’t arise when I imagine something. And if I draw what I imagine, & someone asks “What makes this picture _his_ picture?”, then I could answer: “My image”.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#109b.5+110a.1) <span class="fac">[109b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/109b.webp),[110a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/110a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-109b5110a1}

<span class="series-number">312</span> “What makes the remark I just made a remark about him?”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#110a.2) <span class="fac">[110a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/110a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-110a2}

<span class="series-number">313</span> What can be said about that? Nothing that is simultaneous with it. If you want to know who he meant, ask him!

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#110a.3) <span class="fac">[110a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/110a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-110a3}

<span class="series-number">314</span> “What makes my imagination of him…?” Is there something here that I could investigate to determine whether it was my imagination of him?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#110a.4) <span class="fac">[110a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/110a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-110a4}

<span class="series-number">315</span> For when I say, “I now vividly imagine him as he…,” the same question applies to this sentence and to the mental image.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#110a.5) <span class="fac">[110a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/110a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-110a5}

<span class="series-number">316</span> On the other hand, I might have a face in mind, I might even be able to draw it, and not know to whom it belongs, where I saw it.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#110a.6) <span class="fac">[110a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/110a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-110a6}

<span class="series-number">317</span> What makes my image of him an image of _him_? Not the similarity of the picture. The same question applies to the utterance “I now vividly imagine him,” _how about **from the** image_? What makes my utterance an utterance about _him_? Nothing that is in it, or simultaneous with it (“behind it”). If you want to know who he meant, ask him!

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#110a.7+110b.1) <span class="fac">[110a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/110a.webp),[110b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/110b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-110a7110b1}

<span class="series-number">318</span> If, however, someone were to draw while imagining, or instead of imagining, even if only with a finger in the air. (One could call that a “motor image”.) One could ask him, “Whom does that depict?” And his answer would be decisive. It would communicate an intention to us.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#110a.7+110b.1.2) <span class="fac">[110a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/110a.webp),[110b\[1\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/110b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-110a7110b12}

<span class="series-number">319</span> The line I drew was like a description.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#110b.2) <span class="fac">[110b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/110b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-110b2}

<span class="series-number">320</span> _---_

One must consciously remember that a face with a soulful expression can be _painted_ in order to believe that mere colors and shapes can have such an effect on us.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#110b.3) <span class="fac">[110b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/110b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-110b3}

<span class="series-number">321</span> “I believe that he is suffering.” – Do I also believe that he is not a machine? I could only use that word in these two contexts with reluctance. (Or is it like this: “I believe that he is suffering; I am certain that he is not a machine”? Nonsense!) (That would be philosophical nonsense.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#110b.4) <span class="fac">[110b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/110b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-110b4}

<span class="series-number">322</span> Suppose I say about a friend, “He is not an automaton” – What kind of communication would that be? For whom would it be? For a _person_ who sees him under normal circumstances? What _could_ it communicate to him?! (At most, that he always behaves like a human being and not sometimes like a machine.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#110b.5) <span class="fac">[110b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/110b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-110b5}

<span class="series-number">323</span> “I believe that he is not an automaton” does not immediately make sense.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#110b.6) <span class="fac">[110b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/110b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-110b6}

<span class="series-number">324</span> My attitude towards him is the attitude towards the mind. I do not have the opinion that he has a mind.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#110b.7+111a.1) <span class="fac">[110b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/110b.webp),[111a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-110b7111a1}

<span class="series-number">325</span> Of course, an image forces itself upon us, of something incorporeal that animates the face (like trembling air). One must consciously remember that a face with a soulful expression can be painted in order to believe that colors and shapes alone can have such an effect on us.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#111a.3) <span class="fac">[111a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-111a3}

<span class="series-number">326</span> The concept of ‘meaning’ will serve to distinguish what one might call the capricious formations of language from the essential ones, those inherent in the nature of their purpose.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#111a.4) <span class="fac">[111a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-111a4}

<span class="series-number">327</span> The concept of ‘meaning’ will introduce a new perspective into the description of word use.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#111a.5) <span class="fac">[111a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-111a5}

<span class="series-number">328</span> Example: A verb that means “to _write_” in the first person, “to _love_” in the second person, & “to _eat_” in the third person.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#111a.6) <span class="fac">[111a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-111a6}

<span class="series-number">329</span> What is capricious is decided by human nature.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#111a.7) <span class="fac">[111a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-111a7}

<span class="series-number">330</span> But the nature of one who already knows a language, or of one who does not yet know it (e.g., a one-year-old child)?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#111a.8+111b.1) <span class="fac">[111a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111a.webp),[111b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-111a8111b1}

<span class="series-number">331</span> Is it capricious, or not, that a word has a different meaning on each day of the week? or in the first person, something different than in the second?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#111b.3) <span class="fac">[111b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-111b3}

<span class="series-number">332</span> ‘Meaning’ is a primitive concept. Its form includes: “The word means _that_”; i.e., the explanation of meaning through demonstration. This works well under certain circumstances & with certain words. However, difficulties arise when one extends the concept to other words.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#111b.4) <span class="fac">[111b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-111b4}

<span class="series-number">333</span> The explanation of a word is not an analysis of what goes on in me (or is supposed to go on) when I utter it.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#111b.5) <span class="fac">[111b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-111b5}

<span class="series-number">334</span> “Two soldiers stand on each two meters.” “He sat in the bank on a bench.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#111b.6+112a.1) <span class="fac">[111b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111b.webp),[112a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-111b6112a1}

<span class="series-number">335</span> “For this word in our language, I will assign two meanings; one I explain _like this_: ‥‥‥, the other _like this_: ‥‥․” I could also have said: This word in our language has two meanings: ‥‥‥” Here, one could not ask: “But are these really two meanings?” – Or perhaps – if that means: “Isn’t this distinction completely arbitrary, completely pointless?”

“Why do you distinguish them, what is the point of this distinction?”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#112a.2) <span class="fac">[112a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-112a2}

<span class="series-number">336</span> “I cannot see its purpose.” But what does the explanation of a purpose look like? I cannot give a general answer to that.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#112a.3) <span class="fac">[112a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-112a3}

<span class="series-number">337</span> You set yourself tasks & then solve them, like a mathematician. The task: non & ne.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#112a.4) <span class="fac">[112a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-112a4}

<span class="series-number">338</span> The one who describes word uses naively will also describe those of “non” & those of “ne,” & he can also make the observation that they are almost the same. – But that is not all: Can he not say that the two words are only different in very specific language-games, & otherwise have the same use?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#112a.5) <span class="fac">[112a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-112a5}

<span class="series-number">339</span> Must he not be able to say that in a certain language-game, one word can be replaced by another?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#112a.6) <span class="fac">[112a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-112a6}

<span class="series-number">340</span> If the language-game, the activity, e.g., building a house (as in No. 2), fixes the use of a word, then the concept of use is elastic with that of the activity. But this lies in the _nature_ of language.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#112a.7+112b.1) <span class="fac">[112a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112a.webp),[112b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-112a7112b1}

<span class="series-number">341</span> Let us imagine _this_ use of “non” & “ne”: The two words are used like our “not”; on the same occasion, one or the other is used, they behave in this respect just like synonymous words; only in the rare case of doubling is a distinction made. I will therefore be tempted to distinguish the word use ‘as _a whole_’ from a partial use. Yes, here the partial use may seem more important than the ‘whole’.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#112b.2) <span class="fac">[112b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-112b2}

<span class="series-number">342</span> So I say: “The use here & here is the same. In all these cases, one can substitute one for the other.” But what does that actually mean?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#112b.3) <span class="fac">[112b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-112b3}

<span class="series-number">343</span> The naive describer – does he know the concept of ‘being able to replace one word with another’? – He certainly knows the one concerning the mixed _use_ of two words.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#112b.4) <span class="fac">[112b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-112b4}

<span class="series-number">344</span> Or also like this: The traveler, who travels through the land where “non” & “ne” are used & tries to translate the language into his own, will have no reason to translate each into a separate word in his language – until he comes to a case of double negation (then _he_ may find an equivalent in his language).

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#112b.5) <span class="fac">[112b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-112b5}

<span class="series-number">345</span> The traveler could also say: “As far as I can see, the use is the same.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#112b.6) <span class="fac">[112b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-112b6}

<span class="series-number">346</span> “‘ne’ & ‘non’” has exactly the same meaning in all these cases.” One could say this, for example, if in their language the words are _treated_ like synonyms in these cases. (And we know what that looks like.) – But it could also be that the language does not treat them like synonyms, does not ‘_mix_’ them, & they would still be synonyms for us.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#113a.4) <span class="fac">[113a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-113a4}

<span class="series-number">347</span> _---_

The greatest difficulty in these investigations is finding a way of representation for vagueness.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#113a.5) <span class="fac">[113a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-113a5}

<span class="series-number">348</span> One can speak of the function of the word in the sentence, in the language-game, in language. But “function” means in each of these cases _Technique_. It therefore refers to a _general_ explanation & training.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#113a.6+113b.1) <span class="fac">[113a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113a.webp),[113b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-113a6113b1}

<span class="series-number">349</span> Whoever teaches someone a sign for negation trains them in a certain way. (The double negation need not appear at all in the training.) Now, however, he can use it once, or hear it & perceive it in this way or that. This perception need not be connected with his earlier training, although one might imagine it that way. Should I say that the training has taught him the meaning of the double negation? I don't have to say that. And if it has taught me to use two words in the same way as a negation, certainly not, nor to discriminate between them in the case of doubling. I certainly did not learn this distinction through the training. But I did learn a meaning through it, & therefore, the _same_ meaning.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#113b.2) <span class="fac">[113b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-113b2}

<span class="series-number">350</span> One can distinguish between different kinds of training in a training session. And thus, one can distinguish between different kinds of use in a use of words.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#113b.3) <span class="fac">[113b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-113b3}

<span class="series-number">351</span> _---_

November 30, 1948. So psychology is concerned with behaviour; not with the mental? What does the psychologist report? – What does he observe? Not the behaviour of people & in particular their utterances? But these do not report on their behaviour.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#113b.4+114a.1) <span class="fac">[113b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113b.webp),[114a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-113b4114a1}

<span class="series-number">352</span> The doctor asks, “How is he doing?” The nurse says, “He is groaning.” A report about behaviour. But does the question of whether the groaning is genuine even need to arise? Couldn’t it be that this question simply doesn’t exist? Couldn’t one, for example, conclude: “If he is groaning, then we must give him another pain-relieving medication”? Couldn’t the report about behaviour in this world of thought simply be _used_ as a report about the psychological, _as_ such? Couldn’t it be used for this purpose, & isn’t it precisely the purpose that matters?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#114a.2) <span class="fac">[114a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-114a2}

<span class="series-number">353</span> “But these then involve a tacit assumption.” Then the technique of the use of our words is always a tacit assumption.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#114a.3) <span class="fac">[114a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-114a3}

<span class="series-number">354</span> “In doing so, we always make an assumption; if it is not correct, then, of course, everything is different.” Let us say, for example, when we send someone to do some shopping. Is the assumption that he is a human being & that the shop is not a mirage? Assumptions have an end.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#114a.4) <span class="fac">[114a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-114a4}

<span class="series-number">355</span> But couldn't what is not an ‘assumption’ here be something else in another case? Isn’t an assumption simply where there is a _doubt_? And doubt can be completely absent; & it can be present to the slightest degree & to the greatest extent.

_---_

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#114a.5) <span class="fac">[114a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-114a5}

<span class="series-number">356</span> Imagine someone saying, “I’m scared, I’m scared all the time,” – but what he means is that he can play chess. He gives a capability the expression of an experience.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#114a.6) <span class="fac">[114a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-114a6}

Even if he could do this only when & for as long as he feels it, feeling would not be the ability.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#114a.7+114b.1) <span class="fac">[114a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114a.webp),[114b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-114a7114b1}

<span class="series-number">357</span> 01.12.1948

How does one compare the behaviour of anger, joy, hope, expectation, belief, love, understanding? – Depict an angry person! That is easy. A joyful one – that would depend: what kind of joy? The joy of reunion, or the joy of listening to music…? – Hope? That would be difficult. Why? There are no gestures of hope. How does hope express itself, that he will return?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#114b.2) <span class="fac">[114b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-114b2}

<span class="series-number">358</span> It is easy to imagine an animal angry, fearful, sad, joyful, or frightened. But hopeful?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#114b.3) <span class="fac">[114b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-114b3}

<span class="series-number">359</span> Surely, hoping is a calm, joyful anticipation. (Although such an analysis has something rather unappealing about it.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#114b.4) <span class="fac">[114b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-114b4}
