### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#132a.5) <span class="fac">[132a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-132a5}

<span class="series-number">556</span> I engage with what I now notice, with what strikes me. In this respect, experiencing the change of aspect is also like an action.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#132a.6) <span class="fac">[132a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-132a6}

<span class="series-number">557</span> Does the exclamation “What was that?” express a particular visual experience?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#132a.7) <span class="fac">[132a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-132a7}

<span class="series-number">558</span> Could one not say: Yes and no?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#132a.8) <span class="fac">[132a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-132a8}

<span class="series-number">559</span> “I just saw a shadow flitting past.” Isn’t this the expression of the visual experience?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#132a.9) <span class="fac">[132a\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-132a9}

<span class="series-number">560</span> I see a ‘questionable’ shape.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#132a.10) <span class="fac">[132a\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-132a10}

<span class="series-number">561</span> But can one really say that the questionableness _&_ the shape is seen?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#132b.1) <span class="fac">[132b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-132b1}

<span class="series-number">562</span> Question: _What speaks in favor of it_? Well, that the description that one gives of the phenomenon is also modeled by its problematic nature.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#132b.2) <span class="fac">[132b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-132b2}

<span class="series-number">563</span> What is the criterion of the experience of seeing? What should the criterion be? The representation of ‘what is seen’.

_---_

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#132b.3) <span class="fac">[132b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-132b3}

<span class="series-number">564</span> Can I now, when the aspect changes, separate a visual experience from a thought experience? – If you separate them, then the change of aspect seems to be lost.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#132b.4) <span class="fac">[132b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-132b4}

<span class="series-number">565</span> I believe one could also put it this way: The essential thing about the change of aspect is a sense of _wonder_. And wonder is thinking.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#132b.5) <span class="fac">[132b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-132b5}

<span class="series-number">566</span> But isn’t that just _my_ understanding of the change of aspect?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#132b.6) <span class="fac">[132b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-132b6}

<span class="series-number">567</span> _What_ is illuminated? The aspect of it, for example. And the thought lay in the fact that one could only express it in this way.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#132b.7) <span class="fac">[132b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-132b7}

<span class="series-number">568</span> Something passing by could surprise me, so to speak, _physically_ surprise me, & yet I don’t _think_ about it. That is, I could, for example, continue in a train of thought, even though I flinched.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#132b.8+133a.1) <span class="fac">[132b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132b.webp),[133a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-132b8133a1}

<span class="series-number">569</span> Now, however, consider the aspects of the rotating drum. When they change, it is as if the _motion_ has changed. One does not necessarily know here whether the mode of motion or the aspect has changed. Here, therefore, one does not have the experience of the change of aspect in the same sense.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#133a.2) <span class="fac">[133a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-133a2}

<span class="series-number">570</span> Imagine two lights, blue & red, alternating in front of my eyes. It is my task to press a button when the blue light appears, and another button when the other light appears. Surely, one could do this completely mechanically. – And now imagine this game being played with the two aspects of the black-and-white cross. Is it impossible that there could be an equally mechanical, thoughtless reaction?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#133a.3) <span class="fac">[133a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-133a3}

<span class="series-number">571</span> If now I recognize this person in the crowd, after perhaps having looked in their direction for some time, – is it seeing? Is it thinking? The expression of the experience is “Look, there is the ‥‥!” – but it could, of course, also be a sketch. The fact that I recognize this person might also be expressed in the sketch & in the sketching. (But the sudden recognition is not expressed in it.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#133b.3) <span class="fac">[133b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-133b3}

The same expression, which previously reported what had happened, is now an exclamation of recognition.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#133a.4+133b.1) <span class="fac">[133a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133a.webp),[133b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-133a4133b1}

<span class="series-number">572</span> Suppose a child suddenly recognizes a person. Let it be the first time that it suddenly recognizes someone. – It is as if their eyes have suddenly opened. One could, for example, ask: If it suddenly recognizes N.N., – could it have the same visual experience, but without recognizing him? It could, for example, _mistake_ him.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#133b.2) <span class="fac">[133b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-133b2}

<span class="series-number">573</span> Imagine someone asked: “Am I doing this with my eyes?”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#133b.4) <span class="fac">[133b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-133b4}

<span class="series-number">574</span> A hare runs across the path. One person does not know him & says: “Something strange has just rushed past” & now describes the phenomenon. Another person exclaims “A hare!” & cannot describe it so precisely. And why do I still want to say that the one who recognizes him sees him differently than the one who does not recognize him?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#133b.5) <span class="fac">[133b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-133b5}

<span class="series-number">575</span> If one sees a smile that one does not recognize as a smile, does not understand it, is it seen differently than when it is understood? For example, one might imitate it differently.

_(Understanding of the modes of church music.)_

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#133b.6) <span class="fac">[133b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-133b6}

<span class="series-number">576</span> _What is to be said_ that he sees it differently?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#133b.7) <span class="fac">[133b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-133b7}

<span class="series-number">577</span> “When one knows what it is, it looks different.” – Why?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#133b.8) <span class="fac">[133b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-133b8}

<span class="series-number">578</span> What if someone does not know the thing rushing past, but immediately understands it? Does he then see it like the one who knows it?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#133b.9) <span class="fac">[133b\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-133b9}

<span class="series-number">579</span> It is a question of defining concepts.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#133b.10) <span class="fac">[133b\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-133b10}

<span class="series-number">580</span> I mention these types of aspects in order to show what kind of variety we are dealing with here.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#133b.11+134a.1) <span class="fac">[133b\[11\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp),[134a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-133b11134a1}

<span class="series-number">581</span> There is a large number of interrelated phenomena & possible concepts here.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#134a.2) <span class="fac">[134a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-134a2}

<span class="series-number">582</span> Sometimes the conceptual is predominant in the aspect. That is: Sometimes the expression of the aspect experience is only possible through a conceptual explanation. And this | can again be of a very different kind.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#134a.3) <span class="fac">[134a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-134a3}

<span class="series-number">583</span> 25.12.1948

The different types of aspects.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#134a.4) <span class="fac">[134a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-134a4}

<span class="series-number">584</span> Hearing a melody & the movements with which one perceives it in a certain way, or hears it.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#134a.5) <span class="fac">[134a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-134a5}

<span class="series-number">585</span> Why does it seem so difficult to separate _doing_ & experiencing here?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#134a.6) <span class="fac">[134a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-134a6}

<span class="series-number">586</span> It is as if doing & impression did not proceed side by side, but the doing shaped the impression.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#134a.7) <span class="fac">[134a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-134a7}

<span class="series-number">587</span> I hear it differently, I can now play it differently. That is, reproduce it differently.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#134a.8) <span class="fac">[134a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-134a8}

<span class="series-number">588</span> There are many types of aspectual experience. They all have in common the expression: “I see it now as _that_”; or “I see it now _like this_”; or “Now it is _that_, – now _this_”: or “I hear it now as _that_; “I hear it now as …”. The _explanation_, however, of this “that” & “_like this_” is, in different cases, quite different.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#134a.9+134b.1) <span class="fac">[134a\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp),[134b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-134a9134b1}

<span class="series-number">589</span> But what if I suddenly become aware of a lion outdoors? I assume I only see a part of its head, but I immediately recognize it & shout “A lion!”. The strongest thing in me is the fear. – And now I ask again: What was the visual impression like? Was _it_ of a different kind than the one I receive in the zoo? (Apart from the fact that this one is much more complete. –)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#134b.2) <span class="fac">[134b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-134b2}

<span class="series-number">590</span> (I cannot yet rise above the mass of phenomena.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#134b.3) <span class="fac">[134b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-134b3}

<span class="series-number">591</span> It is _difficult_ to see here that it is about definitions. The _concept_ forces itself upon you. (You must not forget that.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#134b.5) <span class="fac">[134b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-134b5}

<span class="series-number">592</span> 26.12.1948

The visual impression seems to organize itself into this form.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#134b.6) <span class="fac">[134b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-134b6}

<span class="series-number">593</span> That is, it actually means: it changed, & it did not change.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#134b.8) <span class="fac">[134b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-134b8}

<span class="series-number">594</span> When I suddenly recognized it, my visual impression seemed to suddenly become _that_.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#134b.9) <span class="fac">[134b\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-134b9}

<span class="series-number">595</span> Was it understanding? Was it seeing?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#134b.10) <span class="fac">[134b\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-134b10}

<span class="series-number">596</span> What, if anything, justifies me in speaking of seeing here?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#134b.11+135a.1) <span class="fac">[134b\[11\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp),[135a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-134b11135a1}

<span class="series-number">597</span> Imagine, someone told me: “It was as if my visual impression suddenly organized itself into this face & its surroundings.” I would understand him. I would grasp why he expresses himself this way. That is, I would also be inclined to use the image.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#135a.2) <span class="fac">[135a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-135a2}

<span class="series-number">598</span> This _figure_

![](../graphics/137,135a-1.svg)

is the reversal of

![](../graphics/137,135a-2.svg)

& this:

![](../graphics/137,135a-3.svg)

[must be written in mirror writing] the reversal of

this:

![](../graphics/137,135a-4.svg)<span class="post-block-punct">.</span>

One is inclined to say that one sees the inverted word differently than the upright one. This is easy to copy, that one is difficult.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#135a.3) <span class="fac">[135a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-135a3}

<span class="series-number">599</span> The figure a) 🖵 is the reversal of the figure b) 🖵, as the figure c 🖵 is of d)🖵. But there is still another difference between my impression of c & d – I would like to say – than between a & b, (d, for example, looks neat, c messy. Cf. Lewis Carroll ‘Looking glass’) d is easy to copy, c difficult.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#135a.5) <span class="fac">[135a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-135a5}

<span class="series-number">600</span> What formerly fell apart in the visual impression now belongs together.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#135b.2) <span class="fac">[135b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-135b2}

<span class="series-number">601</span> 29.12.1948

How would this explanation be: “I can see something as _that_, of which it can be a _picture_”? – But is this an explanation, or a pleonasm? –

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#135b.3) <span class="fac">[135b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-135b3}

<span class="series-number">602</span> It says that the aspects in the change of aspect are those that the figure might, under certain circumstances, have in a picture in a _static_ way.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#135b.4) <span class="fac">[135b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-135b4}

<span class="series-number">603</span> A triangle can indeed be in a picture, hanging in another, representing something overturned in a third. – So that I, the observer, do not say “This can also represent something overturned”, but “This jug has fallen over & lies in shards” … That is how we react to the picture.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#135b.5) <span class="fac">[135b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-135b5}

<span class="series-number">604</span> Could I say what a picture must be like so that one reacts to it in that way? No. There are, for example, styles of painting that do not communicate anything to me in this direct way, but do to someone else. I believe that habit & education have something to do with it.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#135b.6) <span class="fac">[135b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-135b6}

<span class="series-number">605</span> 30.12.1948

Consider now, as an example, the aspects of the triangle. The triangle

![](../graphics/137,135b.svg)

can be seen as a triangular hole, as a body, as a geometric drawing; standing on its base, hanging from its point; as a mountain, as a wedge, as an arrow or pointer; as an overturned body that should (e.g.) stand on the shorter leg, as half a parallelogram, and other things.

_---_

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#135b.7+136a.1) <span class="fac">[135b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135b.webp),[136a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-135b7136a1}

<span class="series-number">606</span> What does it mean that I see the sphere floating in the picture? Does it already lie in the fact that I describe the picture in this way? That this description is the most obvious, most natural one for me? No; it could be for various reasons. It could, for example, simply be the conventional one.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#136a.2) <span class="fac">[136a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-136a2}

<span class="series-number">607</span> What, then, is the _expression_ for the fact that I understand the _image_ not merely in the sense of ‘e.g.’ like this (know what it is supposed to represent), but see it like this? Such an expression is: “The sphere appears to float,” “One sees it floating,” or also in a particular tone of voice, “It is floating!” This, then, is the expression of the attitude. But not used as such.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#136a.3) <span class="fac">[136a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-136a3}

<span class="series-number">608</span> We are not asking here what the causes are & what produces this impression in a particular case.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#136a.4) <span class="fac">[136a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-136a4}

<span class="series-number">609</span> And _is_ it a different impression? – “I do see something _different_ when I see the sphere floating, compared to when I simply see it lying there.” – This actually means: This expression is justified! (Because, taken literally, it is merely a repetition.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#136a.5) <span class="fac">[136a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-136a5}

<span class="series-number">610</span> (And yet, my impression is also not that of a real, floating sphere. Compare different kinds of ‘spatial’ seeing; the spatial quality of ordinary photography & that of what one sees through a stereoscope.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#136a.6+136b.1) <span class="fac">[136a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136a.webp),[136b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-136a6136b1}

<span class="series-number">611</span> “And is it really a different impression?” In order to answer this, I would like to ask myself whether something different actually exists within me. But how can I convince myself of this – I _describe_ what I see differently.

_---_

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#136b.2) <span class="fac">[136b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-136b2}

<span class="series-number">612</span> We can bring about the change in aspect, and it can also occur against our will. It can follow our will as our gaze does.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#136b.4) <span class="fac">[136b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-136b4}

<span class="series-number">613</span> When one is riding in a bus at night & it turns a corner, & one looks at the front wall of the bus (which does not move relative to the passenger), one believes that one _sees_ it making the turn. One naturally feels that the vehicle is making the movement, & perhaps one also sees some indication of it in the outer darkness, which one still sees out of the corner of one’s eye, even if unconsciously. But one believes that one sees the front wall describing the curve & _at the same time_ naturally that it is not changing relative to oneself.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#136b.5+137a.1) <span class="fac">[136b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136b.webp),[137a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-136b5137a1}

<span class="series-number">614</span> 01.01.1949

(Rhees) When someone describes his current mood, for example, saying that it is like a gray cloud, – is he not observing it, even if it is perhaps modified by this observation? & does what I said about ‘descriptions’ in general apply to this description?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#137a.2) <span class="fac">[137a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-137a2}

<span class="series-number">615</span> Don’t I look within myself & say: “What is the right word for this feeling, this mood? – And is it clear that it is not, for example, intensified by my looking? Can’t I _indulge_ in a mood? And can’t self-observation be part of indulging?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#137a.3) <span class="fac">[137a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-137a3}

<span class="series-number">616</span> Is this similar to the fact that I evoke a bodily pain (in whatever way) in myself and then try to describe its character accurately.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#137a.5) <span class="fac">[137a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-137a5}

<span class="series-number">617</span> Imagine I say in such a case: “Yes, this pain is like a blazing flame.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#137a.6+137b.1) <span class="fac">[137a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137a.webp),[137b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-137a6137b1}

<span class="series-number">618</span> In what way & in what sense do I observe the pain? (For it seems to me that there is no difference whether one observes one’s sadness or one’s own pain.) I put myself in the position of feeling it. But _which_ one? The one _of that_ kind, or the one that is brought about _in this_ way? Do I say, “I would like to create this same burning pain again, in order to see _how_ it is”? What is the point of observing it if I can identify it in this way? Well, one could say: “If I only feel this same pain again & again, I will finally find the right word or even the right colored image (perhaps that of a flame) for it.” & now I can simplify the case. He does not need to intentionally bring about the pain; rather, let there be a persistent pain (in the head, or stomach) & he thinks about the right description of his feeling.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#137b.2) <span class="fac">[137b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-137b2}

<span class="series-number">619</span> What I want to say is that when I look, I don’t observe the _visual impression_, but rather what is being looked at.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#137b.3) <span class="fac">[137b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-137b3}

<span class="series-number">620</span> So, if I look at my sorrow in some sense, then I am not observing the impression that I receive.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#137b.4) <span class="fac">[137b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-137b4}

<span class="series-number">621</span> But imagine, I stare at an object & ask myself “What kind of red am I seeing there?” I am not interested in the color of the object; rather, I am simply looking for a name for my current impression of it. Can I say: To think about an impression is not the same as ‘to observe it’?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#137b.5+138a.1) <span class="fac">[137b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137b.webp),[138a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-137b5138a1}

<span class="series-number">622</span> 01/02/1949

What does the person communicating to us when they say “Now I see it as …?” That is: what are the consequences of this communication, what kind of use can it have? It could have various consequences. For example, someone who sees the duck-rabbit as a duck will not be able to describe the expression of the rabbit's face. Spatial image in descriptive geometry. Whoever sees the cube schema _exactly_ will not be able to perform various drawing operations with it. [Not quite correct.]

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#138a.2) <span class="fac">[138a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-138a2}

<span class="series-number">623</span> Related to the game “This could be …”.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#138a.3) <span class="fac">[138a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-138a3}

<span class="series-number">624</span> What do you communicate to me with the words …? What can I do with this utterance? What are its consequences?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#138a.4) <span class="fac">[138a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-138a4}

<span class="series-number">625</span> Some drawings are always seen, flat as a figure in the plane, others sometimes, or always spatially.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#138a.5) <span class="fac">[138a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-138a5}

<span class="series-number">626</span> One might now say: The visual impression of the spatially seen drawings is spatial; for the cube scheme, for example, it is a cube. (Because the description of the impression is the description of a cube.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#138a.6) <span class="fac">[138a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-138a6}

<span class="series-number">627</span> “Now I always see it as ….” I previously saw this incorrectly in the picture as …; but not anymore. I now always see it as it is intended. – How does this manifest itself?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#138a.7) <span class="fac">[138a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-138a7}

<span class="series-number">628</span> And it is then remarkable that our impression is something flat for some drawings, and something spatial for others. One wonders: “Where can this end?” [The image of a runner.]

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#138a.8) <span class="fac">[138a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-138a8}

<span class="series-number">629</span> 01/03/1949

“What does this color remind me of?” – Whoever looks at an object & asks himself this, – is he observing the facial **impression**? []

_---_

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#138a.9) <span class="fac">[138a\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-138a9}

<span class="series-number">630</span> What does someone communicate to me when they say “Now I see it as …”? What are the consequences of this communication? What can I do with it?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#138a.10+138b.1) <span class="fac">[138a\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp),[138b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-138a10138b1}

<span class="series-number">631</span> People often associate colors with vowels. It could be that for some, a vowel, when pronounced repeatedly, changes its color. The vowel _a_ would be ‘now blue – now red’. “Now I see it as …” might no longer mean anything more than “a is red” (combined with physiological observations, this change could also become important to us).

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#138b.2) <span class="fac">[138b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-138b2}

<span class="series-number">632</span> When I ask about the use, about the interest, of that communication, it occurs to me how often in aesthetic considerations it is said “You must see it _this_ way, that is how it is intended,” “If you see it _this_ way, you will see where the mistake lies,” “You must hear these measures as an introduction,” “You must listen for this key,” “You must phrase the theme _this_ way” (and this can refer to both listening and playing).

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#138b.3) <span class="fac">[138b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-138b3}

<span class="series-number">633</span> The figure

![](../graphics/137,138b-1.svg)

is intended to represent a convex step, and is used to demonstrate certain spatial processes. I draw the line _a_ through the midpoints

![](../graphics/137,138b-2.svg)

of the two surfaces. – If someone only sees the figure spatially for a moment, and perhaps even then sometimes as a concave step, then it might be difficult for them to follow the demonstration. (As it is for someone in descriptive geometry who is unable to see the projections spatially.) (The role of perception in mathematics.) And if the flat aspect alternates with a spatial one for them, then it is no different than if I were showing them alternately something flat and one of two models during the demonstration.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#139a.1) <span class="fac">[139a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-139a1}

<span class="series-number">634</span> But the uses in aesthetics and in descriptive geometry are fundamentally different. Is it not essential in aesthetics that the picture, the piece of music, etc., can change its aspect for me? – because for that depiction of spatial processes, it is of course not.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#139a.2) <span class="fac">[139a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-139a2}

<span class="series-number">635</span> “If I see it _this_ way, it fits, but if I see it _this_ way, then it doesn’t.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#139a.3) <span class="fac">[139a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-139a3}

<span class="series-number">636</span> Game: “It could also be …”.

_---_

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#139a.4) <span class="fac">[139a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-139a4}

<span class="series-number">637</span> “That is not _seeing_!” – “That is _seeing_!” – Both must be justified conceptually.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#139a.5) <span class="fac">[139a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-139a5}

<span class="series-number">638</span> The question is: _In what way_ is it seeing?

_---_

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#139a.6) <span class="fac">[139a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-139a6}

<span class="series-number">639</span> “Do you always _see_ this leaf as _green_, as long as you perceive it spatially, & when asked what color it is, would you answer “green” without lying?” Does this question have a clear sense? One possible answer might be: “Well, when I look at the leaf, I don’t keep saying to myself ‘Oh, how _green!_’”.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#139a.7) <span class="fac">[139a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-139a7}

<span class="series-number">640</span> 04.01.1949

What is the expression of the fact that I see this picture as a picture of bare trees? That I don’t just _know_ that it depicts such, that I don’t _read_ it like a blueprint? – I treat it differently. (Child & doll)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#139a.8+139b.1) <span class="fac">[139a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139a.webp),[139b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-139a8139b1}

<span class="series-number">641</span> If I see an animal in a picture pierced by an arrow, do I only _know_ that the arrowhead is connected to the feathers, or do I _see_ it? – I _behave_ towards these parts as I would towards an arrow, i.e., I don’t just say, as if it were a drawing of a machine that I am deciphering, “These two parts belong together; a rod goes through here”; but if asked, “What did you see in the picture?” I immediately answer: “An animal pierced by an arrow.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#139b.2) <span class="fac">[139b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-139b2}

<span class="series-number">642</span> “The phenomenon initially amazes one, but a physiological explanation for it will certainly be found.” – Our problem is not causal but conceptual.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#139b.3) <span class="fac">[139b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-139b3}

The question is: _In what way_ is it seeing?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#139b.4) <span class="fac">[139b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-139b4}

<span class="series-number">643</span> I often see a contour continue when it is interrupted in the drawing.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#139b.5) <span class="fac">[139b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-139b5}

<span class="series-number">644</span> I see that in the picture, the arrow penetrates the animal. It has hit it in the neck and still protrudes at the nape of the neck. Imagine the picture as a silhouette. – Do you _see_ the arrow, or do you only _know_ that these two parts depict (are supposed to depict) a part of an arrow?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#139b.6) <span class="fac">[139b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-139b6}

<span class="series-number">645</span> Compare Köhler’s figure of the mutually penetrating hexagons.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#139b.7) <span class="fac">[139b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-139b7}

<span class="series-number">646</span> That is seeing! _In what way_ is it seeing?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#139b.8) <span class="fac">[139b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-139b8}

<span class="series-number">647</span> The description I would give of the figure if I approached it naively is undoubtedly _that_. If the picture were shown to me for a short time & I were to describe it, _that_ would be the description; if I were to draw it afterward, I would certainly draw two identical, mutually intersecting hexagons, & in _this_ respect I would not err in the copy, even if otherwise much of it might be incorrect.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#140a.1) <span class="fac">[140a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-140a1}

<span class="series-number">648</span> Is it knowledge or seeing? – What if it were _merely_ knowledge? In what cases would I say that it is merely knowledge? When I read a blueprint, for example.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#140a.2) <span class="fac">[140a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-140a2}

<span class="series-number">649</span> What does it mean when, looking at a drawing in descriptive geometry, I say: “I know that it continues here, but I can’t _see_ it”? Does it simply mean that I lack the familiarity of ‘knowing’? Well, this familiarity is certainly one of our criteria. The criterion is a certain _kind_ of knowing. (Certain gestures, for example, that indicate the spatial relationships. Fine nuances of behaviour.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#140a.3) <span class="fac">[140a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-140a3}

<span class="series-number">650</span> You must think about the role that pictures (as opposed to technical drawings) play in our lives. And this role is by no means uniform.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#140a.4) <span class="fac">[140a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-140a4}

<span class="series-number">651</span> I would expect something different from someone who sees the drawing as… than from someone who only knows what it is supposed to depict.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#140a.5) <span class="fac">[140a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-140a5}

<span class="series-number">652</span> [Remark about the _third_ person.]

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#140a.6) <span class="fac">[140a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-140a6}

<span class="series-number">653</span> One sometimes hangs sayings on the wall. But not theorems of geometry. Our relationship to these two.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#140a.7) <span class="fac">[140a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-140a7}

<span class="series-number">654</span> “If I see it _this_ way, then it fits with the rest.” This is a very specific language-game with the expression “to see something this way.” And the criterion of ‘seeing it _this_ way’ is different here than in the case of descriptive geometry.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#140b.3) <span class="fac">[140b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-140b3}

<span class="series-number">655</span> What is the criterion for him seeing it _this_ way, if, for example, he says, “If I see it _this_ way, then it fits with _this_”? – That he can, for example, make or suggest certain changes to the picture, building, etc., that have a certain effect on the viewer.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#140b.4) <span class="fac">[140b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-140b4}

<span class="series-number">656</span> 05.01.1949

_---_

How, if one were to say: The recounting of a dream is an odd form of memory disturbance; it combines a lot of memories from the previous day, from earlier days, yes, even from childhood, & makes of them a memory of an event during the time of sleep. We all know of cases in which we mix the memories of several days into _one_.

_---_

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#140b.5) <span class="fac">[140b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-140b5}

<span class="series-number">657</span> When would I call it merely _knowing_? – If one treated the picture as if it were a technical drawing. One would **read** out what it depicts from it. (Subtle nuances of behaviour.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#140b.6) <span class="fac">[140b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-140b6}

<span class="series-number">658</span> 06.01.1949

I immediately recognize the 6 corners as such. Now I look at them & ask myself: “Am I really seeing them _as_ hexagons?” – and that: the whole time, during which I see them? – And I would like to answer: I don't think I think of them as hexagons the whole time.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#141a.1) <span class="fac">[141a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-141a1}

<span class="series-number">659</span> The first thing that strikes the eye in this picture is: they are hexagons.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#141a.2) <span class="fac">[141a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-141a2}

<span class="series-number">660</span> Someone tells me: “I immediately saw it as two hexagons. Yes, that was _all_ that I saw in it.” But how do I understand that? I think he would have answered the question “What do you see?” immediately with “Two hexagons.” He would also not have treated this answer as one of many possible answers. It is in this respect the same as the answer “An animal” – if I had shown him the picture of such an animal; or “A face” – if I had shown him the figure

![](../graphics/137,141a.svg)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#141a.3) <span class="fac">[141a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-141a3}

<span class="series-number">661</span> I _recognize_ it immediately as a face, I am ready to treat it as such.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#141a.5) <span class="fac">[141a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-141a5}

<span class="series-number">662</span> It could also have been that I saw the picture as something else first, & then said to myself “Ah, it’s 2 hexagons!” But this did not happen. So the aspect would have changed. And does this now prove that I actually saw it in a particular aspect? (Well, as you like!)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#141a.6) <span class="fac">[141a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-141a6}

<span class="series-number">663</span> “Is it a _real_ experience of seeing?” The question is: In what respect is it one?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#141a.7) <span class="fac">[141a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-141a7}

<span class="series-number">664</span> [It is difficult to see ‥‥․] [The eye “See how it looks!”]

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#141b.1) <span class="fac">[141b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-141b1}

<span class="series-number">665</span> “For me it is an animal, pierced by an arrow.” I treat it as that, this is my _attitude_ towards the figure. This is one of its meanings: to call it seeing.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#141b.2) <span class="fac">[141b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-141b2}

<span class="series-number">666</span> But can I also say in the same sense: “These are two hexagons for me”? Not in the same sense, but in a similar one. –

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#141b.3) <span class="fac">[141b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-141b3}

<span class="series-number">667</span> So I see it, in this sense, therefore only as long _as_ I have this _attitude_ towards it? One could say that.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#141b.4) <span class="fac">[141b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-141b4}

<span class="series-number">668</span> “This feature of the picture struck me.”

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#141b.5) <span class="fac">[141b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-141b5}

<span class="series-number">669</span> The best description I can give of what was shown to me in a fleeting moment is _this_: ‥‥‥ “The impression was that of a standing animal.” So, a quite specific description emerged. – Was that _seeing_, or was it a thought? How am I supposed to decide?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#141b.6) <span class="fac">[141b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-141b6}

<span class="series-number">670</span> But do I only see the picture in this aspect as long as I have this attitude towards it? – One can say that.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#141b.7) <span class="fac">[141b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-141b7}

<span class="series-number">671</span> But couldn't one also say: “I always see it as that, if I never see it as something else”?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#141b.8+142a.1) <span class="fac">[141b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141b.webp),[142a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-141b8142a1}

<span class="series-number">672</span> “He sees it spatially & is therefore so familiar with the drawing, as if he were operating in the spatial model.” But isn't his particular manipulation in the drawing precisely the criterion for the fact that he sees it spatially? (Because what else do I know about his impression?)

_---_

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#142a.2) <span class="fac">[142a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-142a2}

<span class="series-number">673</span> I don't just see it as an animal while I say this. A body also does not have its weight only during the time it is being weighed. (Definition of concepts.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#142a.3) <span class="fac">[142a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-142a3}

<span class="series-number">674</span> It is a lion for me. How long is it a lion for me?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#142a.4) <span class="fac">[142a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-142a4}

<span class="series-number">675</span> But wait! Do I ever actually say of the ordinary picture (of a lion) that I see it as a lion? I have never heard that. [→ ]

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#142a.5) <span class="fac">[142a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-142a5}

<span class="series-number">676</span> And yet I have talked about such seeing here!

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#142a.6) <span class="fac">[142a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-142a6}

<span class="series-number">677</span> 07.01.1949

I could say of a picture by Picasso that I do _not_ see it as people. Or of some: I could not have seen it as what it represents for a long time, but I do now. This is similar to: I was not able to hear this as a unit for a long time, but now I hear it as such. Previously it seemed to me like a lot of short pieces that kept breaking off – now I hear it as an organism. (Bruckner.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#142a.7) <span class="fac">[142a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-142a7}

<span class="series-number">678</span> Would you understand if I said, “We _regard_ the photograph, the picture on our wall, as the people & other things, as the things that are depicted on them”?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#142a.8+142b.1) <span class="fac">[142a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142a.webp),[142b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-142a8142b1}

<span class="series-number">679</span> This doesn't have to be the case. We could easily imagine people who do not have this relationship to the pictures. (People, for example, who would be repelled by our photographs because a face without color is eerie & ugly.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#142b.2) <span class="fac">[142b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-142b2}

<span class="series-number">680</span> We do not say of a conventional picture of a person, “I see that as a person.” “I see it as a…” goes together with… [→] (“Goes together” in the language-game.)

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#142b.3) <span class="fac">[142b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-142b3}

<span class="series-number">681</span> If I now say “We regard a portrait as a human being,” – when & for how long do we do this? Always, whenever we see it at all (& not see it as something _other_)? I could affirm this, & in doing so, I would define the concept of regarding. – The question is whether a _different_ concept becomes important to me besides this one: a concept of seeing it in this way, which only takes place while I am occupied with the picture as ‥‥ _occupied_.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#142b.4) <span class="fac">[142b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-142b4}

<span class="series-number">682</span> The concept of noticing. I can say that I sometimes notice the similarity of this picture with… and so on; but I do not say that I sometimes notice that this photograph is a face. I could say: A picture does not always _live_ for me while I see it.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#142b.5) <span class="fac">[142b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-142b5}

<span class="series-number">683</span> But the question now is: Is this “living” a “seeing,” or: on what grounds could I call it a “seeing”? What does this concept have in common with a concept of seeing?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#142b.6) <span class="fac">[142b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-142b6}

<span class="series-number">684</span> Now, we do not say that we ‘see’ the conventional picture of a lion, for example, _as_ a lion:

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#142b.7+143a.1+143a.2+143a.3) <span class="fac">[142b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142b.webp),[143a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143a.webp),[143a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143a.webp),[143a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-142b7143a1143a2143a3}

<span class="series-number">685</span> “Your picture smiles at me from the wall.” It does not have to do this every time I see it. And this expression is, after all, a justification for the expression that I see it differently then.

I am not striving for any kind of completeness with all these examples. No inventory of all psychological concepts. I only want to enable my reader to help himself in conceptual uncertainties.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#143a.7) <span class="fac">[143a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143a.webp)</span> {#ms-137-143a7}

<span class="series-number">687</span> The child says, “Now it is a house.” – this can also be said in the game in which the box is a house, in various ways & in various situations. Someone comes into the room while the game is in progress; they are told, “Now it is a house.” This does not mean: “_Now_ it has become a house for me,” not the lighting up of the aspect. For it to be so, the tone & situation must be of a particular kind, & it is again about subtle differences in behaviour.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#143a.8+143b.1) <span class="fac">[143a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143a.webp),[143b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-143a8143b1}

<span class="series-number">688</span> 09.01.1949

‘Subtle nuances of behaviour’ – If my understanding of a subject is expressed in that I whistle it correctly, that is an example of these subtle nuances.

But if “Now it is a house” does not express the _lighting up_ of the aspect, can it report the stable aspect?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#143b.2) <span class="fac">[143b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-143b2}

<span class="series-number">689</span> “He completely forgets that it is a box; it is actually a house for him.” (There are certain signs of this.) If you were to say this about someone, would it also be correct to say that he _sees_ it as a house?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#143b.3) <span class="fac">[143b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-143b3}

<span class="series-number">690</span> And whoever could play like this & in a particular situation exclaims with special expression, “Now it is a house!,” would express the lighting up of the aspect.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#143b.4) <span class="fac">[143b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-143b4}

<span class="series-number">691</span> But the expression of the voice & gesture is the same as if the object had changed & had finally _become_ that & this.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#143b.5) <span class="fac">[143b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-143b5}

<span class="series-number">692</span> I would like to say that what lights up here only remains as long as a certain engagement with the object lasts. (“Look how it looks!”) (The noticing of the family resemblance of this face to an absent person.) – “I would like to say” – & _is_ it so? – Ask yourself: “How long do I notice something?” – How long is it _new_ to me?

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#143b.6) <span class="fac">[143b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-143b6}

<span class="series-number">693</span> Would it be correct to say that in the change of aspect of the rotating drum, the perception of the _constancy_ of the object is missing? Because one can really doubt here whether the mode of movement has not changed.

### [Ms-137](/ms-137/#143b.7) <span class="fac">[143b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143b.webp)</span> {#ms-137-143b7}

<span class="series-number">694</span> You must bear in mind that the description of the changing aspects is in every case of a different kind.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#1a.2) <span class="fac">[1a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/1a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-1a2}

<span class="series-number">695</span> I want to call the aspects of ‘black cross’, ‘white cross’ the main aspects of the double cross, for short. Likewise, let us speak of the two main aspects of the stage. There is a fundamental difference between them & the aspect of the triangle, for example, as an inverted triangle.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#1a.3) <span class="fac">[1a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/1a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-1a3}

<span class="series-number">696</span> The difference lies in the description for the communication of the aspect.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#1a.4) <span class="fac">[1a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/1a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-1a4}

<span class="series-number">697</span> The aspect experiences have in common the form of expression: “I see it now as _this_” or “I see it now like this” or “Now it is _this_ – now _this_” or “I hear it now as …; before I heard it as …”. But the explanation of this ‘_this_’ & ‘_like this_’ is of a very different kind in different cases.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#1a.5) <span class="fac">[1a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/1a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-1a5}

<span class="series-number">698</span> Seeing the triangle as half a parallelogram requires imagination; seeing the main aspects of the double cross does not.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#1a.6) <span class="fac">[1a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/1a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-1a6}

<span class="series-number">699</span> These seem to be of a more fundamental nature than that.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#1a.7) <span class="fac">[1a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/1a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-1a7}

<span class="series-number">700</span> Only someone who has the shape of those animals can ‘see the H. & E. aspect’; the main aspects of the double cross could manifest in the primitive reactions of a child who cannot yet speak.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#1b.1) <span class="fac">[1b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/1b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-1b1}

<span class="series-number">701</span> – – – These two aspects _A_ (I want to call them the aspects _A_) could, for example, be communicated simply by the observer alternately pointing to a free-standing white cross & a free-standing black cross. Yes, one could imagine that this would be a primitive reaction of a child who cannot yet speak. In the communication of the aspects A, a part of the double cross figure is therefore pointed out. The H. & E. aspect could not be described in an analogous way.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#1b.2) <span class="fac">[1b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/1b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-1b2}

<span class="series-number">702</span> Only of the one will we say that he sees the H. & E. aspects, who has the shape of those two animals. Analogous conditions do not exist for the aspects A.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#1b.3) <span class="fac">[1b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/1b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-1b3}

<span class="series-number">703</span> Someone can take the H. & E. head for the image of a rabbit, the double cross for the image of a black cross, but not the mere triangular figure for the image of something inverted. Imagination is needed to see this aspect of the triangle.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#1b.4) <span class="fac">[1b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/1b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-1b4}

<span class="series-number">704</span> Whoever is deceived by the cube schema, so that he _takes it for_ a cube, primarily sees it as this cube, whether he later tries to see it differently, & whether he succeeds in doing so. (Comparison with the double cross.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#1b.5+2a.1) <span class="fac">[1b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/1b.webp),[2a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/2a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-1b52a1}

<span class="series-number">705</span> The aspects A are not essentially spatial aspects. A black cross on a white background is not necessarily a black cross that lies on a white surface. One could teach him this concept by only showing him black crosses painted on paper; provided that the environment of these crosses changes & the cross is the _important_ thing in what is perceived: If, for example, it is copied, then always, or primarily, the cross is copied, etc.. The aspects A are not related in the same way to a _deception_ as the spatial aspects of the cube schema.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#2a.2) <span class="fac">[2a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/2a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-2a2}

<span class="series-number">706</span> January 16, 1949

From any script – this one, for example <math display="inline"><mtext>𝖄</mtext></math> – I can imagine that it is a perfectly correctly written letter of some foreign alphabet. – Or, it is a poorly written letter; & in a way that is one of several different ways: e.g. hastily, or typically childish – clumsy, or bureaucratically ornate. It could deviate from the correctly written in various ways. – & depending on the fiction with which I surround it, I can see it in different aspects. – Here there is a close kinship with the experience of the meaning of an isolated word.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#2b.2) <span class="fac">[2b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/2b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-2b2}

<span class="series-number">707</span> “I noticed the similarity between him & his father for perhaps 5 minutes.” One can say this if his face changes & only resembled his father’s for those 5 minutes. But it can also mean: the similarity to his father only struck me for a few minutes, after which I forgot it.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#2b.3) <span class="fac">[2b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/2b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-2b3}

<span class="series-number">708</span> “It no longer strikes me” – but what happens when it strikes me? – Well, I look at the face, with an expression of astonishment, perhaps, in my mien, maybe also in words. – But is that the striking of the similarity? No; these are the appearances of the striking, but these _are_ ‘what happens’. ‘Striking’ is a different concept. –

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#2b.4) <span class="fac">[2b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/2b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-2b4}

<span class="series-number">709</span> ‘Thinking’ & ‘speaking in the imagination’ (I don’t say: “talking to oneself”) are different concepts.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#2b.5) <span class="fac">[2b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/2b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-2b5}

<span class="series-number">710</span> Is the striking looking & thinking? No. Many concepts _intersect_ here.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#2b.6) <span class="fac">[2b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/2b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-2b6}

<span class="series-number">711</span> “If one did not _experience_ the meaning of words, how could one laugh at puns?” [Hairdresser & sculptor.] – One laughs at such jokes: & in that sense, one could say that one experiences the meaning.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#3a.1) <span class="fac">[3a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/3a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-3a1}

<span class="series-number">712</span> Just think of the words that lovers speak to one another! They are ‘charged’ with feelings. And they are certainly not – as technical terms are – to be replaced by any other sounds according to an agreement. Isn’t this because they are _gestures_? And a gesture does not have to be innate; it is learned, but precisely _assimilated_. – But isn’t that a myth?! – No. For the characteristics of assimilation are precisely that I use _this_ word & prefer to use none rather than one that is imposed; & similar reactions.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#3a.2) <span class="fac">[3a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/3a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-3a2}

<span class="series-number">713</span> A word has, for example, become the carrier of a _tone_ for us; & I cannot, on command, utter another word in the same felt tone.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#3b.1) <span class="fac">[3b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/3b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-3b1}

<span class="series-number">714</span> The similarity (for example) strikes me, – & the striking ceases. It only struck me for a few minutes, then no longer.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#3b.2) <span class="fac">[3b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/3b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-3b2}

<span class="series-number">715</span> What happened then? First, I looked at the face with a peculiar expression in my own, & if someone had asked me, “Why are you looking at him so intently?”, I would have answered, “Because he looks _so_ much like his father.” Perhaps he is speaking to me & I don’t even pay attention to what he is saying, because I am only thinking about this similarity. – That is approximately what occurs to me in answer to the question, what happened.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#3b.3) <span class="fac">[3b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/3b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-3b3}

<span class="series-number">716</span> But in this answer, there is a heterogeneous element: “& if someone had asked me …”. That is not something that ‘happened’ when the similarity struck me. – Yes, my distraction is also not of the same kind as my facial expression. – So only my mien, gestures, perhaps words that I say to myself or others, remain.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#3b.4) <span class="fac">[3b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/3b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-3b4}

<span class="series-number">717</span> The striking is related to thinking.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#3b.5+4a.1) <span class="fac">[3b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/3b.webp),[4a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/4a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-3b54a1}

<span class="series-number">718</span> What happened there? – Whose face do I remember? My own _facial expression_ comes to mind; I could imitate it. If someone who knows me had seen my face, they would have said, “Something about his face has struck you.” – Words also come to mind that I might utter aloud, or to myself, on such an occasion. And that is all. And is that what it means to be struck by something? No.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#4a.3) <span class="fac">[4a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/4a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-4a3}

<span class="series-number">719</span> To notice, to pay attention, to be attentive.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#4a.4) <span class="fac">[4a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/4a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-4a4}

<span class="series-number">720</span> 19.01.1949

“Do _you_ see this leaf as always _green_, as long as you see it, & does its color not change for you?” Does this question have a clear sense? An answer to it might be: “Well, I don’t keep saying to myself all the time ‘Oh, how _green_!’”.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#4a.5) <span class="fac">[4a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/4a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-4a5}

<span class="series-number">721</span> “Are you _conscious_ of its color all the time?” First, I would like to say: “Certainly not!” But when & for how long am I conscious of it? It seems I cannot say anything sensible about that; I do not know what criteria should be applied. Should I say: “Only as long as I _think_ of it”?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#4a.6) <span class="fac">[4a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/4a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-4a6}

<span class="series-number">722</span> Someone tells me: “I looked at the flower, but I was thinking about something else & was not conscious of its color.” Do I understand that? – I can imagine a meaningful connection to it; it would continue something like: “Then suddenly I _saw_ it & realized that it was the one that…”.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#4a.7+4b.1) <span class="fac">[4a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/4a.webp),[4b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/4b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-4a74b1}

<span class="series-number">723</span> But what about this answer: “If I had turned away at that time and someone had asked me what color it was, I wouldn’t have known”? “He looked at him without seeing him.” That happens. But what is the criterion for that? There are just different cases.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#4b.2) <span class="fac">[4b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/4b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-4b2}

<span class="series-number">724</span> “I’m looking more at the shape now than at the color.” Don’t let yourself be confused by such turns of phrase. Above all, don’t think, “What might be happening in the eye or in the brain?”

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#4b.5) <span class="fac">[4b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/4b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-4b5}

<span class="series-number">725</span> “A thought echoing in seeing” – one might say.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#4b.6) <span class="fac">[4b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/4b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-4b6}

<span class="series-number">726</span> “The word has an atmosphere” – a figurative expression; but perfectly understandable in certain contexts. For example: The word “Sabel” has a different atmosphere than the word “Säbel”. They have the same _meaning_, insofar as both are names for the same type of objects. But what should one say here? Do they have different meanings?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#5a.1) <span class="fac">[5a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/5a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-5a1}

<span class="series-number">727</span> Should I now distinguish this many types of meaning? I do _not_ want to do that. Such a classification can be useful for a specific practical purpose. Because for such a purpose, one – out of the countless possible classifications – would be better than another.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#5a.2) <span class="fac">[5a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/5a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-5a2}

<span class="series-number">728</span> The botanist classifies plants. But to show the infinite variety of plants and the diversity of the fine transitions, no classification is needed.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#5a.3) <span class="fac">[5a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/5a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-5a3}

<span class="series-number">729</span> I saw the face clearly (in front of me) as before, – but I no longer noticed the similarity to that other face.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#5a.4) <span class="fac">[5a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/5a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-5a4}

<span class="series-number">730</span> It could also be that one similarity faded for me and another came to my consciousness.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#5a.5) <span class="fac">[5a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/5a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-5a5}

<span class="series-number">731</span> Let’s suppose – as a hypothetical construct – that certain memories become more or less vivid while I look at his face, & that _this_ is responsible for the change of aspect. Should I still say that I _see_ now this, now that?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#5a.6) <span class="fac">[5a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/5a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-5a6}

<span class="series-number">732</span> _Is_ the noticing of similarity a seeing, or is it not? How am I to decide? They are unequal here, but related concepts.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#5a.7) <span class="fac">[5a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/5a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-5a7}

<span class="series-number">733</span> 20.01.1949

One perceives an internal relation through noticing the aspect, & yet it is related to _imagining_.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#5a.8+5b.1) <span class="fac">[5a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/5a.webp),[5b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/5b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-5a85b1}

<span class="series-number">734</span> 21.01.1949

Only someone who has learned & mastered that, and _can_ do it, has any reason to say that he has experienced something.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#5b.2) <span class="fac">[5b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/5b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-5b2}

<span class="series-number">735</span> – – –Does one now perceive the timidity, or does one not? With the concept of ‘timid,’ one can describe what is visually perceived, just as with the concepts of ‘major’ or ‘minor,’ one can describe what is heard.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#5b.4) <span class="fac">[5b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/5b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-5b4}

<span class="series-number">736</span> How could one see that the facial expression is mean, fearful, cruel, if one did not know that this is an _expression_, not the anatomy of the essence?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#5b.5) <span class="fac">[5b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/5b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-5b5}

But does that not simply mean that I could not apply _these_ concepts, which do not only relate to the visual, to the description of what is _seen_? Could I not still have a _purely_ visual concept, let's say, of a frightened face? (I could then use a different word.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#5b.6) <span class="fac">[5b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/5b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-5b6}

<span class="series-number">737</span> I must already _know_ quite a lot in order to be able to describe a text as “childish”. But can I also say: “in order to be able to _see_ it as ‘childish’”?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#5b.7) <span class="fac">[5b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/5b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-5b7}

“Childish” can describe a script, i.e., what I see, but ‘childish’ is not a purely visual concept.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#6a.1) <span class="fac">[6a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/6a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-6a1}

<span class="series-number">738</span> Is it now correct to say: “We _could_ have a purely visual concept, which coincides entirely with the visual part of the concept ‘common’ (e.g.)”?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#6a.2) <span class="fac">[6a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/6a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-6a2}

<span class="series-number">739</span> Such a concept would then truly be comparable to the concepts ‘major’ and ‘minor’, which also have an emotional value, but which can also only be used to describe the structure of what is perceived.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#6a.3) <span class="fac">[6a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/6a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-6a3}

<span class="series-number">740</span> ‘Major’ & ‘minor’ is therefore compared here with ‘oblique’ & ‘right-angled’, for example.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#6a.4) <span class="fac">[6a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/6a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-6a4}

<span class="series-number">741</span> But wouldn't it also be correct to say that whoever does not have our concept of ‘timid’, ‘childish’, ‘mean’, could not experience the writing, the facial expression, in the same way as we do, even if he has a concept that is always applicable wherever ‘timid’ is, for example? Couldn't I say: The two of them _see_ the same thing, but _experience_ it differently? Just as they can both hear something, but experience it differently.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#6a.5) <span class="fac">[6a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/6a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-6a5}

<span class="series-number">742</span> Just think of the expression “I heard a _mournful_ melody”! And now the question: “Does he _hear_ the lament?”

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#6a.6) <span class="fac">[6a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/6a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-6a6}

<span class="series-number">743</span> And if I were to answer: “No, he doesn’t hear it; he only experiences it” – what would that accomplish? One cannot even specify a sensory organ for this ‘experience’. Some might answer: “Of course I hear it!” – Others: “I don’t actually _hear_ it.” – However, distinctions in concepts can be identified.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#6b.1) <span class="fac">[6b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/6b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-6b1}

<span class="series-number">744</span> (One can draw a boundary for a concept. But where does the idea of ‘experiencing’ the common, fearful, etc. come from?) (Now,) we _react_ differently to the hesitant facial expression than to the one who does not recognize it as hesitant (in the _full_ sense of the word). – Now, I do _not_ want to say that we _sense_ this reaction in the muscles & joints. – No, we have here a modified **experience** concept.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#6b.2) <span class="fac">[6b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/6b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-6b2}

<span class="series-number">745</span> But what exactly is _something akin to sensation_ here?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#6b.3) <span class="fac">[6b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/6b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-6b3}

<span class="series-number">746</span> “You must _experience_ the sadness of this face.” (When looking at a picture.) – Whoever experiences it often mirrors the face with their own. They are impressed. The picture evokes this effect in them. Most likely, I could compare this ‘experience’ to the sensation of pain, which also has a characteristic expression in facial expressions & gestures. And yet, it is also related to seeing, because it (?) – – –

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#6b.4) <span class="fac">[6b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/6b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-6b4}

<span class="series-number">747</span> What is the expression, the criterion, of this sensation? For instance, how, with what _expression_, will someone hum the melody. Also perhaps, with what facial expression. Or: what he will say about it. That is probably the particular description that he gives of it.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#6b.5+7a.1) <span class="fac">[6b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/6b.webp),[7a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/7a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-6b57a1}

<span class="series-number">748</span> The truth is this: ‘lamenting’ is a concept that is not purely acoustic. However, I can use it to describe something that is purely acoustic (“The steam whistle emits a lamenting tone”). The word “lamenting” could also lose all its non-acoustic relationships and become a purely acoustic designation (like the words “to travel” and “travailler” originally had a relationship to what was painful, which they then lost).

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#7a.2) <span class="fac">[7a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/7a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-7a2}

<span class="series-number">749</span> One could now raise an objection to the designation “_purely_ acoustic.” Who says what “purely” acoustic is? – Well, “purely acoustic” is a description if, according to it, one can reproduce what was heard & all other relationships are left out of the game.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#7a.3) <span class="fac">[7a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/7a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-7a3}

<span class="series-number">750</span> I can describe a chair by using the concept “Louis XIV style,” and in contrast to that, a description that, for example, through drawings, etc., notes the shape, color, etc., without reference to a historical period, a king, etc.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#7a.4) <span class="fac">[7a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/7a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-7a4}

<span class="series-number">751</span> One might ask: “Do _you_ see the style of Ludwig XIV when you look at the armchair?”

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#7a.5) <span class="fac">[7a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/7a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-7a5}
