<span class="series-number">752</span> It is difficult to understand and present conceptual slopes.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#7a.6) <span class="fac">[7a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/7a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-7a6}

<span class="series-number">753</span> 22.01.1949

One can answer the question “What does a chair in the Louis XIV style look like?” – or the question, “What does a lamenting melody sound like?” – Show me such chairs, sing me such a melody!

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#7a.7) <span class="fac">[7a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/7a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-7a7}

<span class="series-number">754</span> The adjective “sad,” when applied to the stick figure, e.g., characterizes the grouping of lines within the oval. (Major, minor). When applied to a person, it has a different, albeit related, meaning. (That is, however, _not_ to say that the facial expression is _similar_ to the feeling of sadness!)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#7a.8+7b.1) <span class="fac">[7a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/7a.webp),[7b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/7b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-7a87b1}

<span class="series-number">755</span> Consider this as well: I can only see red & green, but I cannot hear them; however, the sadness, insofar as I can see it in his face, I can also hear it in his _voice_.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#7b.2) <span class="fac">[7b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/7b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-7b2}

<span class="series-number">756</span> Untangling many knots is the task of the philosopher.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#7b.3) <span class="fac">[7b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/7b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-7b3}

<span class="series-number">757</span> This face is outrageous, this face disgusts me, this smell is repulsive. Does repulsiveness belong to the sensation of smell? How does one decide? One could, for example, ask: “Can two people have the _same_ sensation of smell, but one find it repulsive, the other not? – & what would be the criterion of sameness? – One could, for example, compare them with the same smells. – But there _is_ no accepted criterion here. Do I _see_ the outrageousness? Yes & no. Both can be justified.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#7b.4) <span class="fac">[7b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/7b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-7b4}

<span class="series-number">758</span> One doesn’t need any _knowledge_ to find a smell disgusting.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#7b.5) <span class="fac">[7b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/7b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-7b5}

<span class="series-number">759</span> “Do you see that when you draw these lines, the face becomes sad?” Into which category does this sentence belong? How is it used? I once said that it is similar to a geometrical one. But one could also argue that it is a psychological one, i.e., an empirical proposition (roughly comparable to: if you mix these substances, a yellow color results).

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#7b.6) <span class="fac">[7b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/7b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-7b6}

<span class="series-number">760</span> One might say to a child: “Do you see that when you put these two stones together, a circle results?”

![](../graphics/138,7b.svg)<span class="post-block-punct">.</span>

Is he learning an empirical proposition? (Here I am deliberately speaking of the child, not the adult.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#8a.1) <span class="fac">[8a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/8a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-8a1}

<span class="series-number">761</span> (Could the sentence not also fall ‘between several games’?)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#8a.3) <span class="fac">[8a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/8a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-8a3}

<span class="series-number">762</span> That sentence need not be a geometrical one. Its purpose could be to establish that the face, with _these_ lines, makes a sad impression on _me now_. But it could also play roughly the role of a geometrical (timeless) one.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#8a.5) <span class="fac">[8a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/8a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-8a5}

<span class="series-number">763</span> 23.01.1949

One could say that someone is blind to the _expression_ in a face. But does that mean that their sense of faces is lacking? But this is, of course, not simply a question of physiology. The physiological is here a symbol for the logical. Physiological distinctions symbolize logical distinctions here.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#8a.6) <span class="fac">[8a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/8a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-8a6}

<span class="series-number">764</span> ‘He has the eye of a painter,’ ‘the ear of a musician’.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#8a.7+8b.1) <span class="fac">[8a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/8a.webp),[8b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/8b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-8a78b1}

<span class="series-number">765</span> Is talking about the sensation of an expression as a kind of seeing simply a shift in concepts, like when one talks about the marriage of money? Is this merely a misunderstanding, or a gradual erosion of the concept of ‘seeing’?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#8b.2) <span class="fac">[8b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/8b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-8b2}

<span class="series-number">766</span> Someone who has only seen _one_ facial expression could not possess the concept of ‘facial expression’. ‘Facial expression’ only exists in the play of facial expressions. Someone who has only seen ‘sad’ faces could not _experience_ them as sad.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#8b.4) <span class="fac">[8b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/8b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-8b4}

<span class="series-number">767</span> But couldn’t he _see_ it, just like you & I? – But the word “sensation” is not entirely without ambiguity either. – What do I perceive with the sensation? Do I perceive, in addition to the so-called sadness of the facial features, also the sad mood of the person? Or do I _infer_ this from the face? Do I say: “His features & his behaviour were sad, so he was probably also sad”?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#8b.5) <span class="fac">[8b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/8b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-8b5}

<span class="series-number">768</span> I believe the question belongs here: Does ‘sad music’ make us _sad_? It seems, yes & no. We make, for example, a sad face, or a face that _reflects_ sadness.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#8b.6) <span class="fac">[8b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/8b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-8b6}

<span class="series-number">769</span> 24.01.1949

One sees sadness, insofar as one sees, for example, the sad facial expression, but one does not see the sad sound of his voice.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#8b.7) <span class="fac">[8b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/8b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-8b7}

<span class="series-number">770</span> One also sees crying. And does the one who sees it differently, the one who only observes the physiological phenomenon, see something different from the one who sees the expression of grief in it? – He _observes_ it differently.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#9a.2) <span class="fac">[9a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/9a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-9a2}

<span class="series-number">771</span> Yes, I would like to ask: Do I even have an _excuse_ for talking about a different ‘seeing’?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#9a.4) <span class="fac">[9a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/9a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-9a4}

<span class="series-number">772</span> Now, what would be the sign that he sees it differently? Only his statement about it. And of course: whoever observes differently also sees something different.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#9a.5) <span class="fac">[9a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/9a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-9a5}

<span class="series-number">773</span> Imagine, someone asks you quite dryly: “Why do you say he _sees_ it differently?” (What could you answer?) First, I would like to say, “He _looks_ at something different,” then perhaps “He will make different comparisons.” It may also be that the mere fact that the person is _not_ crying or complaining makes his face look sadder.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#9a.6) <span class="fac">[9a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/9a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-9a6}

<span class="series-number">774</span> I hear the melody very differently after I know the style of this master. I might have described it as cheerful before, but now I experience it as the expression of great suffering. I describe it differently now, put it in a completely different context.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#9a.7) <span class="fac">[9a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/9a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-9a7}

<span class="series-number">775</span> What does one perceive when one experiences the seriousness of a melody? – Nothing that can be explained by reproducing what was heard.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#9b.1) <span class="fac">[9b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/9b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-9b1}

<span class="series-number">776</span> 25.01.1949

How could I recognize the expression of the face if I did not know that it is an expression, not the anatomy of this being? How could I see sadness, seriousness, cruelty in the face without knowing that?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#9b.3+10a.1) <span class="fac">[9b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/9b.webp),[10a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/10a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-9b310a1}

<span class="series-number">777</span> 26.01.1949

Imagine a physiological explanation for this experience. Let it be this: when looking at the figure, the gaze traces the object again & again along a certain path. This path corresponds to a certain periodic movement of the eyeballs. It may be that such a movement automatically alternates with another. Certain movements are physiologically impossible; therefore, one cannot see the duck-rabbit as a picture of a rabbit’s head & a duck’s head behind it, or the cube schema as that of two prisms penetrating each other. And so on. – Let us assume that this is the explanation. – “Yes, now I know in what respect it is a seeing.” You have now introduced a _new_, a physiological criterion of seeing. And this may obscure the old problem, but it does not solve it. – The purpose of this remark, however, is to bring to your attention what happens when a physiological explanation is presented to us. Our problem hovers untouched above this explanation, & its nature is now all the more clear. It is the psychological concept that is creating the difficulty for us (here).

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#10a.2) <span class="fac">[10a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/10a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-10a2}

<span class="series-number">778</span> 27.01.1949

The question now arises: could there be people who cannot see something _as_ something? – Or: what would it be like if a person lacked this ability? What consequences would it have? Would this defect be comparable to that of color blindness, or to the lack of perfect pitch? We want to call it “aspect-blindness” – and now consider what this might mean. (A conceptual investigation.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#10a.3) <span class="fac">[10a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/10a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-10a3}

<span class="series-number">779</span> So is he not supposed to, for example, be able to see the cube schema as a cube? It would not follow from this that he could not recognize it as a representation (e.g., a technical drawing) of a cube. But it would not involve a transition from one aspect to another. Question: Could he, as we do for a cube, _hold_ it to be so? If not, then one would not call it _blindness_. He will have a different relationship to pictures in general than we do. (And deviations from the normal of _this_ kind are easy to imagine.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#10a.4+10b.1) <span class="fac">[10a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/10a.webp),[10b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/10b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-10a410b1}

<span class="series-number">780</span> Should he be blind to the similarity of two faces? But also to sameness, or approximate sameness? I wouldn’t say so. – We would call someone who could not recognize the sameness of shapes “mentally deficient,” not “blind.”

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#10b.2) <span class="fac">[10b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/10b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-10b2}

<span class="series-number">781</span> The aspect-blind person should not see the aspects change. But should he not recognize that the double cross contains a black cross? Should he therefore not be able to cope with the task “Show me among these figures those that contain a black cross”? No; he should only not say: “Now it is a black cross on a white background!”

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#10b.3) <span class="fac">[10b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/10b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-10b3}

<span class="series-number">782</span> One says that someone has ‘the eye of the painter,’ ‘the ear of the musician,’ but if someone does not have it, their defect is hardly a kind of blindness or deafness.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#10b.4) <span class="fac">[10b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/10b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-10b4}

<span class="series-number">783</span> One says that someone does not have ‘musical hearing,’ and ‘aspect-blindness’ is (roughly) comparable to this kind of hearing impairment.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#10b.5) <span class="fac">[10b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/10b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-10b5}

<span class="series-number">784</span> The importance of the concept of ‘aspect-blindness’ lies in the kinship between seeing an aspect and experiencing the meaning of a word. For we want to ask: “What is lacking in someone who does not _experience_ the meaning of a word?” – Someone who, for example, could not even pronounce the word ‘bank’ once with one meaning and then with another, or who would not think that if one says the word ten times in a row, it loses its meaning and becomes a mere sound.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#11a.3) <span class="fac">[11a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/11a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-11a3}

<span class="series-number">785</span> The statement “The word – – – was filled with its meaning” has a completely different use, completely different consequences, than “It had the meaning…”

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#11a.4) <span class="fac">[11a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/11a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-11a4}

<span class="series-number">786</span> January 29, 1949

“How does the chemist know that a Na atom is located at _this_ point in the structure?” Compare this with: “How does Mr. N know that a Na atom is located at _this_ point, etc.?” – The answer could be: “B told him.” The question “How does the chemist know…” is the typical _expression_ of the question about the _criterion_.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#11a.5+11b.1) <span class="fac">[11a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/11a.webp),[11b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/11b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-11a511b1}

<span class="series-number">787</span> Here, think of a particular type of illusion that sheds light on these things. – I go for a walk with an acquaintance in the vicinity of the city. In the conversation, it becomes apparent that I imagine the city to be on our right. I have not only _no_ conscious reason for this idea, but a very simple consideration has convinced me that the city is behind us. Asked _why_ I imagine the city in _this_ direction, I can initially give no answer. I had _no reason_ to believe that. Although there is no _reason_, I seem to see or sense certain psychological causes. Specifically, they are certain associations & memories, e.g., this: We are walking along a canal, and I once followed one that ran in the direction I suspected. I could investigate the causes of my conviction in a kind of psychoanalytic way.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#11b.3) <span class="fac">[11b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/11b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-11b3}

<span class="series-number">788</span> “But what kind of odd experience is that?” – It is certainly no odder than any other; it is just of a different kind than those experiences that we regard as the most fundamental, such as sensory impressions.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#11b.4) <span class="fac">[11b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/11b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-11b4}

<span class="series-number">789</span> How then should someone who feels that the city lies in this direction express this correctly? Is it, for example, correct to say that he feels it? Should he actually coin a new word for it? But how could one learn this word? The _primitive_ expression of the experience could not (indeed) contain it. His inclination might be to say “It is as if I know that the city lies there”. Now, the fact that he says this, or something similar, under these circumstances, is precisely the expression of this peculiar experience.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#11b.5) <span class="fac">[11b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/11b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-11b5}

<span class="series-number">790</span> The name, the image of the bearer.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#12a.1) <span class="fac">[12a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/12a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-12a1}

<span class="series-number">791</span> “It is as if I know that the city is there” – “It is as if the name Schubert fits Schubert’s works and his face.”

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#12a.2) <span class="fac">[12a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/12a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-12a2}

<span class="series-number">792</span> January 30, 1949

For mathematics, an investigation is possible that is quite analogous to the philosophical investigation of psychology. It is no more a _mathematical_ investigation than the other is a psychological one. In it, one does _not_ calculate; it is therefore not, for example, logistics. It could be worthy of the name of an investigation of the “foundations of mathematics.”

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#12a.4) <span class="fac">[12a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/12a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-12a4}

<span class="series-number">793</span> I say the word “yield” to myself & ‘mean’ it once as an imperative, once as an adjective. And now say “Yield!” & then “Do _not_ yield from your position!” Does the _same_ experience accompany the word “yield”?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#12a.5) <span class="fac">[12a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/12a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-12a5}

<span class="series-number">794</span> 31.01.1949

Whoever imagines something might express it in a primitive way: “It is as if I were seeing … before me.” – Can one now say that he calls “seeing” what is not actually seeing, but rather something only similar to it?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#12b.1) <span class="fac">[12b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/12b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-12b1}

<span class="series-number">795</span> Given the two words “fat” & “thin,” would you be more inclined to say that Wednesday is fat & Tuesday is thin, or Tuesday is fat & Wednesday is thin? (I decidedly lean toward the former.) Do “fat” & “thin” have a different meaning here than the usual one? They have a different use. So should I have actually used different words? But certainly not. I want to use these words (with the common meanings) _here_. Now, I say nothing about the causes of the phenomenon. They _could_, for example, be that when I was a child, I was taught by a fat teacher every Wednesday & by a thin teacher on Tuesdays. But that is a hypothesis. Whatever the explanation, that inclination exists.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#12b.2) <span class="fac">[12b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/12b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-12b2}

<span class="series-number">796</span> If you asked him, “What do you actually mean here by ‘fat’ & ‘thin’?” he could only explain it in the completely usual way. He could not point to Tuesday & Wednesday and make clear what he means by _them_.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#12b.4+13a.1) <span class="fac">[12b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/12b.webp),[13a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/13a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-12b413a1}

<span class="series-number">797</span> Could one speak here of the ‘primary’ & ‘secondary’ meaning of a word? – The explanation of the word is in both cases that of the primary meaning. Only for the one who knows the word in that meaning can it have this meaning. That is to say, the secondary use consists in the fact that the word, with _this_ primary use, is now used in this new environment.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#13a.2) <span class="fac">[13a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/13a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-13a2}

<span class="series-number">798</span> In that sense, one could want to call the secondary a ‘transferred’ meaning.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#13a.3) <span class="fac">[13a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/13a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-13a3}

<span class="series-number">799</span> But the relationship here is not like that between ‘cutting a thread’ & ‘cutting short one’s speech,’ because here one does _not_ have to use the figurative expression. And when one says ‘The vowel e is yellow,’ the word yellow is _not_ used figuratively.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#13a.4) <span class="fac">[13a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/13a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-13a4}

<span class="series-number">800</span> One only says of such children that they are playing train, if they know a real train. And the word train in the expression “playing train” is not used figuratively, or in a transferred sense.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#13a.5) <span class="fac">[13a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/13a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-13a5}

<span class="series-number">801</span> 01.02.1949

When someone says that he calculates in his head, does he not actually calculate, does he mean something else by calculating? One could not make it understandable to someone what one means by “mental arithmetic” if one had not first taught him the concept of calculation.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#13a.6) <span class="fac">[13a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/13a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-13a6}

<span class="series-number">802</span> Only by means of the concept of calculation (written, spoken calculation) can one make it understandable to someone what “mental arithmetic” means.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#13a.7) <span class="fac">[13a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/13a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-13a7}

<span class="series-number">803</span> I could not make understandable to someone the command to read something silently, nor the concept that he has read it silently, if I did not first teach him the concept of reading aloud. And this impossibility is a logical one.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#13b.1) <span class="fac">[13b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/13b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-13b1}

<span class="series-number">804</span> 02.02.1949

Only if someone has learned to calculate, either in writing or orally, can one, by means of this concept of calculation, make it understandable to him what mental arithmetic is.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#13b.6+14a.1) <span class="fac">[13b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/13b.webp),[14a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/14a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-13b614a1}

<span class="series-number">805</span> But think of the pictures that depict a face both from the front and in profile. One could say: “That’s not how a face looks!” But also: It is a misleading picture, unless you let your gaze wander in such a way that you no longer see it, in the ordinary sense, as _a_ picture, but as several pictures, each of which has its own application.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#14a.3) <span class="fac">[14a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/14a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-14a3}

<span class="series-number">806</span> The brain looks like a script that invites us to read it, and yet it is not a script. Imagine that people become more intelligent the more books they own – that is a fact, but it does not depend on what is in the books.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#14a.4) <span class="fac">[14a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/14a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-14a4}

<span class="series-number">807</span> Does the progress of science benefit philosophy? Certainly. The discovered realities make it easier for the philosopher to consider possibilities. I am well again, or almost well.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#14a.5) <span class="fac">[14a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/14a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-14a5}

<span class="series-number">808</span> 03.02.1949

“I saw him before me in those words.” Isn’t that an experience? And yet, the fact that I saw _him_ could not lie in the picture that I had in mind. Was there then a picture _and_ a thought; and was the picture an experience, but the thought not?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#14a.6) <span class="fac">[14a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/14a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-14a6}

<span class="series-number">809</span> One _‘experiences’_ the _expression_ of a thought.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#14a.7+14b.1) <span class="fac">[14a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/14a.webp),[14b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/14b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-14a714b1}

<span class="series-number">810</span> I cannot call the thought an experience, because otherwise I would have to say that this experience accompanies, for example, speaking.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#14b.2) <span class="fac">[14b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/14b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-14b2}

<span class="series-number">811</span> “But how did you know that it was him, the one you see before you?” – I didn’t know. I said it.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#14b.3) <span class="fac">[14b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/14b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-14b3}

<span class="series-number">812</span> If I say that I experience the expression of the thought, then here I must also understand by ‘expression’ the _imagined_ expression.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#14b.4) <span class="fac">[14b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/14b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-14b4}

<span class="series-number">813</span> 04.02.1949

The _purpose_ of a sign. – “If you want him to come, wave your hand _like this_.” “If you want me to stop, make _this_ sign.” – Can one, for example, speak of a ‘purpose’ of negation (of the word “not”)? One could only do so if every sentence in which one uses it had a purpose. – Nevertheless, one could speak of (the) purposes of the word “not”.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#14b.5) <span class="fac">[14b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/14b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-14b5}

<span class="series-number">814</span> And one could, for example, say: “non” and “ne” fulfill more or less the same purposes, and also: “_This_ word has hardly any purpose. You can easily do without it.”

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#14b.6) <span class="fac">[14b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/14b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-14b6}

<span class="series-number">815</span> Someone who, for example, constructs an artificial language (Esperanto, Basic English) will select its words according to certain principles, and from these principles one could then look at _our_ language. He could, for example, say: “I will not allow _two_ words, one for ‘go’, one for ‘walk’ because for all important purposes _one_ word is enough.” And thus also: “‘go’ and ‘walk’ have essentially the _same_ meaning.”

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#14b.7) <span class="fac">[14b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/14b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-14b7}

<span class="series-number">816</span> One can consider language from different points of view. And they are reflected in the respective concept of ‘meaning’.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#14b.8+15a.1) <span class="fac">[14b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/14b.webp),[15a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/15a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-14b815a1}

<span class="series-number">817</span> 05.02.1949

“I thought of _him_ while saying that.” What is it that I thought of _him_? How would what happened have changed if, instead of thinking of _this_ one, I had thought of another? Did I have to be able to specify a ‘seed’ that then grew into the verbal expression? No.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#15a.2) <span class="fac">[15a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/15a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-15a2}

<span class="series-number">818</span> “When you spoke of ‘a friend,’ who did you mean?” – “I meant…” What happened during your words that made them a reference to _this_ person? Nothing that made them so. Because even if the image of him, with all its details, had been before me while speaking (or whatever you want to put in place of this image), that could not have achieved more than if I had looked at him while saying the words, and looking at him does not mean meaning him. – There are _signs_ that I meant _him_, and a glance could be such a sign. A mental image is also nothing more than such a sign.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#15a.3) <span class="fac">[15a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/15a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-15a3}

<span class="series-number">819</span> Compare the question “What happened when you thought of him while saying this word?” with “What happened when you suddenly knew more?” –

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#15a.4) <span class="fac">[15a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/15a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-15a4}

<span class="series-number">820</span> Meaning is not a process that accompanies the words. Because no _‘process’_ could have the particular _consequences_ of meaning.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#15a.5) <span class="fac">[15a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/15a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-15a5}

<span class="series-number">821</span> If, with the words “my friend,” I meant him, did I have to think of him while uttering the words? Where is the difference? But there is a difference between “I meant him with that word” & “He occurred to me with that word.”

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#15a.6) <span class="fac">[15a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/15a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-15a6}

<span class="series-number">822</span> There are important accompanying processes of speaking that are often lacking in thoughtless speaking. But these are not the thinking.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#15b.2) <span class="fac">[15b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/15b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-15b2}

<span class="series-number">823</span> So, I thought of _this_ person – but not of all aspects of this person.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#15b.3) <span class="fac">[15b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/15b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-15b3}

<span class="series-number">824</span> The garden of this aunt was present in my mind. I saw a part of it in my imagination, but not, for example, that it belonged to this woman. There was something like a sign, which I then interpreted further _in this way_. Or _read_? No, it is not reading, but neither is it interpreting.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#15b.4) <span class="fac">[15b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/15b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-15b4}

<span class="series-number">825</span> “Now I know it!” What was going on there? – So, I didn’t _know_ it when I said I did? You are looking at it incorrectly. (What is the purpose of the signal?)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#15b.5) <span class="fac">[15b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/15b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-15b5}

<span class="series-number">826</span> And could one call this ‘knowing’ an accompaniment of the exclamation?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#15b.6) <span class="fac">[15b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/15b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-15b6}

<span class="series-number">827</span> (The seed could be a word or a mental image, or various other things.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#15b.7) <span class="fac">[15b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/15b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-15b7}

<span class="series-number">828</span> “The word is on the tip of my tongue.” What is happening in my consciousness? That is not what matters. Whatever was happening, I did not mean it with those words. What is more interesting is what was happening in my _behaviour_ at the same time. What I said, what images I used, how I looked – “The word is on the tip of my tongue” is a verbal expression of what is also expressed in quite a different way through characteristic behaviour. Ask again about the primitive reaction that underlies the utterance.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#15b.8+16a.1) <span class="fac">[15b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/15b.webp),[16a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/16a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-15b816a1}

<span class="series-number">829</span> 07.02.1949

The intention has no expression in facial expression, gesture, or voice, but the resolution does.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#16a.2) <span class="fac">[16a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/16a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-16a2}

<span class="series-number">830</span> Philosophers devise an _ideal_ use for many words, which then turns out to be useless.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#16a.3) <span class="fac">[16a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/16a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-16a3}

<span class="series-number">831</span> “I know …” usually means “I have convinced myself that …”. No one says that they have convinced themselves that they have two hands.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#16a.4) <span class="fac">[16a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/16a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-16a4}

<span class="series-number">832</span> I know how one convinces oneself that one has two coins in one’s pocket. But I cannot convince myself that I have two hands because I cannot doubt it.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#16a.5) <span class="fac">[16a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/16a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-16a5}

<span class="series-number">833</span> But what does it mean “to convince oneself of something”? To understand it, one must be shown simple language-games with this word. – How does one convince oneself, in language-game 8, that there are so many plates there? How does one convince oneself that 6 + 6 = 12? Etc.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#16a.6) <span class="fac">[16a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/16a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-16a6}

<span class="series-number">834</span> One says “I know …” where one can doubt, while philosophers say that one knows something precisely where there is no doubt & where, therefore, the words “I know” as an introduction to the statement are superfluous.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#16a.7) <span class="fac">[16a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/16a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-16a7}

<span class="series-number">835</span> It is here as with the syllogism “All men are mortal; Socrates is a man; etc.”, of which it is also not clear how, under what circumstances, it would be applied.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#16a.8) <span class="fac">[16a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/16a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-16a8}

<span class="series-number">836</span> 08.02.1949

How would one describe, for example, the visual impression of someone reading a printed page.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#16b.1) <span class="fac">[16b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/16b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-16b1}

<span class="series-number">837</span> “Yes, now I know what ‘braking’ is.” (He has, for example, felt an electric shock for the first time.) – If he feels the same thing another time, he will perhaps look for the same accompanying phenomena. Braking teaches him about the external world. – Does remembering teach us in the same way that this and that event has passed? – Then one would have to _relate_ it to past events. (Photograph & fashions) Whereas it is indeed the _criterion_ of the past.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#16b.2) <span class="fac">[16b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/16b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-16b2}

<span class="series-number">838</span> And how will you know in the future how remembering feels?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#16b.3) <span class="fac">[16b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/16b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-16b3}

<span class="series-number">839</span> How does he know that _this_ feeling is ‘remembering’? Compare “Yes, now I know what ‘braking’ is” (he has, for example, felt an electric shock for the first time). – Does he know that it is remembering because he recognizes the past with it? And how does he know what _past_ is? The human being learns the expression of the past by remembering.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#16b.4) <span class="fac">[16b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/16b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-16b4}

<span class="series-number">840</span> In contrast, one could speak of a feeling of “long, long ago,” because there is an expression in the voice & facial expression that is characteristic of stories from past days.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#16b.5+17a.1) <span class="fac">[16b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/16b.webp),[17a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/17a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-16b517a1}

<span class="series-number">841</span> February 9, 1949

James actually wants to say: “What a remarkable experience! The word is not yet there, & yet, in a sense, it is already there, or something is there that can only grow into this word.” – But this is not an experience at all; the words “It is on the tip of my tongue” do not express an experience, they only give it a strange interpretation.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#17a.2) <span class="fac">[17a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/17a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-17a2}

<span class="series-number">842</span> They express an experience no more than the words “Now I've got it!” – they are an expression that we use in _certain situations_, & they are surrounded by a behaviour of a special kind, also by some characteristic experiences. In particular, the _finding_ of the word often follows them. (Ask yourself: “What would it be like if people _never_ found the word they were looking for?”)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#17a.4+17b.1) <span class="fac">[17a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/17a.webp),[17b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/17b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-17a417b1}

<span class="series-number">843</span> There is here, as in many related cases, what one could call a _germinal experience_: an image, a sensation, that then gradually develops into a full explanation. And one would like to say that it is a _logical_ germ, something that had to develop in this way with _logical_ necessity. On some occasion, person N. comes to mind. How did it happen? – First, I saw an image, perhaps just gray hair – then I said the name N. (but it could also belong to many people) – but I say, I mean _that_ N., who… etc. – And furthermore, I did not _read_ the name from the mental image, & I also did not subsequently _interpret_ it in this way; for, to the question of whether I only knew or decided later to whom the gray hair & the name N. belonged, I would deny it & say that I knew it from the beginning. But knowing is not an experience. – “I knew it from the beginning” actually only means: I did not read the name from the image, because I did not, for example, think “Whose hair is this, who looks like this!” – nor did I say to myself “The name ‘N.’ should one day stand for _this_ person.” One could say that I became more and more explicit. But where does the idea of the logical germ come from? That is, actually: Where does the idea come from that “it was all already there at the beginning & contained in the first experience”? Does it not have a similar basis as James’s assertion that the thought is already complete at the beginning of the sentence? This treats intention as an experience.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#17b.2) <span class="fac">[17b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/17b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-17b2}

<span class="series-number">844</span> I proceed from explanation to explanation (further). But I only seem to be saying what was already there. Of course. For “It was not there from the beginning” would be wrong. “The thought was _not_ complete from the beginning” means: I only later discovered or decided what I wanted to say. And _that_ is what I do _not_ want to say.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#17b.3) <span class="fac">[17b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/17b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-17b3}

<span class="series-number">845</span> The idea that this experience is a seed arises, however, through a logical process. It becomes a seed _in a logical sense_. Through a logical interpretation.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#17b.4) <span class="fac">[17b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/17b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-17b4}

<span class="series-number">846</span> Could I not also say: That the gray hair first occurred to me, because the name is quite immaterial. It could just as easily have occurred to me at the beginning.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#17b.5) <span class="fac">[17b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/17b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-17b5}

<span class="series-number">847</span> I knew right away from the beginning who it was. “I did not know right away from the beginning” would mean: I only realized it later. That was certainly not the case.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#18a.1) <span class="fac">[18a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/18a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-18a1}

<span class="series-number">848</span> 10.02.1949

When (normally) I walk, go, eat, talk, look here & there, I am no less performing these _actions_ than when the face of an old friend ‘becomes _familiar_’ to me. But trying, intending, making up one’s mind are the acts of the **will**, that in which the will expresses itself to us; they are what we think of when we speak of the will.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#18a.2) <span class="fac">[18a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/18a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-18a2}

<span class="series-number">849</span> (Similarly, I think, one could say: A multiplication is not an experiment, because no experiment could have the special consequences of a multiplication.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#18a.4) <span class="fac">[18a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/18a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-18a4}

<span class="series-number">850</span> But doesn’t the word that _occurs_ to you come in a rather special way? Just pay attention! – Exact attention doesn’t help me at all. With it, I could only discover what is currently going on in me. And how can I listen for it at all when philosophizing? I would have to wait until a word occurs to me again (once). But the curious thing is (yes) that it seems as if I don’t (even) have to wait for such an opportunity. As if I could demonstrate the case to myself, even if it doesn’t really happen to me. And how? – I _play_ it. – But what can I learn in this way? What do I do next? – Gestures, facial expressions, a tone of voice. (This remark has very general application.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#18a.5+18b.1) <span class="fac">[18a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/18a.webp),[18b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/18b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-18a518b1}

<span class="series-number">851</span> 12.02.1949

– – – Interpreted as an _experience_, it certainly looks odd. (No different than ‘meaning’, interpreted as a process in speaking, or – as a cardinal number.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#18b.2) <span class="fac">[18b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/18b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-18b2}

<span class="series-number">852</span> 13.02.1949

Silent speech is not a half-hidden, difficult-to-_see_ phenomenon, & we must now try to see it more clearly & say something about it, as far as we _know_. – It is not hidden at all, but its concept is confusing.

We can call it an articulated process: for it takes place within a _time span_, can accompany another process, as it were a veiled face, as it sometimes seems. (The question of whether laryngeal movements, etc., always, or mostly, take place in silent speech, may be of great interest, but not to us.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#18b.3) <span class="fac">[18b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/18b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-18b3}

<span class="series-number">853</span> I should not say “silent speaking to myself,” because one can speak internally without speaking to oneself.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#18b.4) <span class="fac">[18b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/18b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-18b4}

<span class="series-number">854</span> Imagine _this_ game – I call it “Tennis without a ball”: The players move around on a tennis court just as in tennis, they also have rackets but no ball. Everyone reacts to the other’s stroke as if, or approximately as if, a ball had determined their reaction. (Maneuver) The one who has ‘an eye’ for the game must judge, if necessary, whether a ball went into the net, etc. etc. The game clearly has great similarity with tennis & is nevertheless **fundamentally** different.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#19a.1) <span class="fac">[19a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/19a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-19a1}

<span class="series-number">855</span> But there is a difference here: only the one who can _speak_ can speak in his imagination. Because speaking in imagination involves being able to communicate later _what_ I have said in silence. – In contrast, even someone who does not know the other tennis could (in theory) learn tennis without a ball.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#19a.2) <span class="fac">[19a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/19a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-19a2}

<span class="series-number">856</span> “But silent speaking is a certain activity that I have to learn!” Yes; but what is ‘doing’ and what is ‘learning’ here? Let the meaning of the words be taught to you by their use!

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#19a.3) <span class="fac">[19a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/19a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-19a3}

<span class="series-number">857</span> “So I don’t _really_ calculate when I calculate in my head?!” – Don’t you also distinguish mental calculation from perceptible calculation? And you cannot have that concept without having this one, and you can only learn that activity by learning this one. (Their concepts are so closely related and so far apart as the concepts of cardinal number and rational number.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#19a.4) <span class="fac">[19a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/19a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-19a4}

<span class="series-number">858</span> You could learn to calculate _in your head_ according to the metronome.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#19a.5) <span class="fac">[19a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/19a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-19a5}

<span class="series-number">859</span> Not every being that can express fear, joy, pain, can _feign_ it.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#19a.6) <span class="fac">[19a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/19a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-19a6}

<span class="series-number">860</span> It would be something like this: only in the _face_ can an eye smile, but only in the whole figure can it – – –

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#19a.7) <span class="fac">[19a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/19a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-19a7}

<span class="series-number">861</span> Only in a very specific environment can something be an expression of pain; but only in an even more specific one can it be a feigned expression of pain.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#19a.8+19b.1) <span class="fac">[19a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/19a.webp),[19b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/19b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-19a819b1}

<span class="series-number">862</span> Because feigning is a (specific) pattern in the tapestry of life. It recurs in infinite variations. A dog cannot feign pain because its life is too simple. It does not have the necessary joints for these movements.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#19b.2) <span class="fac">[19b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/19b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-19b2}

<span class="series-number">863</span> You can portray the hypocrite on the stage. So there is an _appearance_ of feigning; it is far more complicated than the appearance of suffering, for example. Otherwise, one could not expose hypocrisy.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#19b.3) <span class="fac">[19b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/19b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-19b3}

<span class="series-number">864</span> 14.02.1949

One could also think that people consciously calculate in their throats, just as they could, for example, calculate with their fingers. Do you want to say that it is an _illusion_ if they imagine that they _hear_ the speech inside, or just a trick of the language?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#19b.4) <span class="fac">[19b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/19b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-19b4}

<span class="series-number">865</span> The hypothesis that certain physiological processes take place during silent speech is of interest to us only insofar as it shows us a possible application of the statement “I said to myself in silence…”, namely, to infer the physiological process from the utterance.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#19b.5) <span class="fac">[19b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/19b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-19b5}

<span class="series-number">866</span> What must the child learn before it can feign? For example, the use of the words: “He thinks I am in pain, but I am not.”

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#19b.6) <span class="fac">[19b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/19b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-19b6}

<span class="series-number">867</span> The child has the experience that it will be treated kindly if, for example, it cries when in pain; it now cries in order to be treated that way. _That_ is not hypocrisy. It is merely a root of hypocrisy.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#20a.1) <span class="fac">[20a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/20a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-20a1}

<span class="series-number">868</span> A child must learn all sorts of things before it can feign.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#20a.2) <span class="fac">[20a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/20a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-20a2}

<span class="series-number">869</span> It must learn a complicated pattern of behaviour before it can feign or be sincere.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#20a.3) <span class="fac">[20a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/20a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-20a3}

<span class="series-number">870</span> A dog does not feign; but it is also not sincere.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#20a.4) <span class="fac">[20a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/20a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-20a4}

<span class="series-number">871</span> The child also learns to mime pain. It learns the game of pretending to be in pain.

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#20a.5) <span class="fac">[20a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/20a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-20a5}

<span class="series-number">872</span> “If the child knows what pain is, then it naturally also knows that it can feign it.”

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#20a.6) <span class="fac">[20a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/20a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-20a6}

<span class="series-number">873</span> 15.02.1949

“…And one day the child _believes_ something.” Why is that wrong? “One day it says ‘I believe …’” is correct. “Today, for the first time, it believed something.” Well, what’s wrong with that? – it simply happened today for the first time _that_ something occurred within it. – But how did it show itself? Well, today it said for the first time, “I believe she is in _pain_.” But that is not enough. I must therefore assume that it subsequently showed that it had not merely repeated the words. In short, that _utterance_ started a language-game, & it could _continue_ it. Today, it seemed, the language-game had begun to work for it. But how can a language-game suddenly begin to work for the child? God knows. – It begins one day to _do_ something. Imagine something analogous in learning a board game that the child sees being played every day.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#20a.7+20b.1) <span class="fac">[20a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/20a.webp),[20b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/20b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-20a720b1}

<span class="series-number">874</span> He is not only learning the use of the expression “to have pain” in all its tenses & persons, but also in connection with negation & the verbs of assertion. For: to believe, to doubt, etc., that the other has pain, are natural ways of our behaviour towards the other. (He learns “I believe that he has …”, “He believes that I have …”, etc. etc., but _not_ “I believe that I have …”). (Does the room have a hole there? No, it only seems to have one.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#20b.2) <span class="fac">[20b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/20b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-20b2}

<span class="series-number">875</span> Does the word ‘pain’ change its meaning in the process?

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#20b.3) <span class="fac">[20b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/20b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-20b3}

<span class="series-number">876</span> ‘Feigning’ does not make the concept of pain more difficult. It makes it more complicated. (Use of money.)

---

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#20b.4) <span class="fac">[20b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/20b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-20b4}

<span class="series-number">877</span> The _uncertainty_ as to whether the other person …, it is a (significant) feature of all these language-games. But this does not mean that everyone is in hopeless doubt about what the other person feels.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#20b.5) <span class="fac">[20b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/20b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-20b5}

<span class="series-number">878</span> The parts of a machine are elastic, yes, even flexible. But does that mean that there is actually no mechanism, because the machine parts behave as if they were made of butter? (And now imagine mechanisms, such as clockworks, made of materials that are far more flexible than ours, so that the movements become strangely irregular – would such a mechanism be unusable, or could it, in fact, be used? (And we don't have our concepts because they are _practical_. Or only some of them for this reason.))

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#20b.6+21a.1) <span class="fac">[20b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/20b.webp),[21a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/21a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-20b621a1}

<span class="series-number">879</span> Imagine introducing uncertainty into a game! This could happen in many ways. Imagine it like this: [tennis without a ball]. If you were to see people playing this game, would you say that this _is_ not a game? Well, compared to our games, it would be of a very different character. (It takes many kinds…)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#21a.2) <span class="fac">[21a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/21a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-21a2}

<span class="series-number">880</span> The fact that what someone says internally is hidden from me, unless he tells me, lies in the _concept_ of internal speech. Only, “hidden” is the wrong word here, because if it is hidden from me, then it should be obvious to him; he should _know_ it. But he doesn’t ‘know’ it, even though he has no doubt about it.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#21a.3) <span class="fac">[21a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/21a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-21a3}

<span class="series-number">881</span> “I know what I want, what I desire, what I believe, what I hope, what I see” etc. etc. (through all psychological verbs) is either philosophical nonsense, or it is _not_ an a priori judgment.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#21a.4) <span class="fac">[21a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/21a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-21a4}

<span class="series-number">882</span> “I know…” may mean “I do not doubt…” – but it does not mean that the words “I doubt” are here _senseless_, or that doubt is logically impossible.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#21a.5) <span class="fac">[21a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/21a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-21a5}

<span class="series-number">883</span> One says “I know…”, where one can convince oneself.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#21a.6+21b.1) <span class="fac">[21a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/21a.webp),[21b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/21b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-21a621b1}

<span class="series-number">884</span> One can imagine a case in which I might convince myself that I have two hands. Normally, however, I cannot _do that_. “But you only have to hold them in front of your eyes.” – If I could _now_ doubt that I have two hands, then I would also have no reason to trust my eyes. (Just as easily, I could then ask my friend.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#21b.2) <span class="fac">[21b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/21b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-21b2}

<span class="series-number">885</span> “_His_ pain is hidden from me,” that would be as if I said: “These sounds are hidden from my eye.”

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#21b.3) <span class="fac">[21b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/21b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-21b3}

<span class="series-number">886</span> The uncertainty in which all his behavior leaves me, is about what is in his mind. But does it always leave me uncertain?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#21b.4) <span class="fac">[21b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/21b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-21b4}

<span class="series-number">887</span> “It is certainly not always subjective uncertainty here, but rather _objective_ uncertainty.” (But what does that mean?)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#21b.5) <span class="fac">[21b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/21b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-21b5}

<span class="series-number">888</span> 16.02.1949

‘Objective uncertainty’ is an indeterminacy in the nature of the game, the permitted evidence.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#21b.6) <span class="fac">[21b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/21b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-21b6}

<span class="series-number">889</span> “What he says to himself inwardly is hidden from me,” which could, of course, also mean that one can usually not _guess_ it, nor (as would be possible) deduce it from his throat movements, for example.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#21b.7) <span class="fac">[21b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/21b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-21b7}

<span class="series-number">890</span> However, I will disregard uses of language such as “Only you can know what is going on inside you.” But if someone wanted to argue that one sometimes says “I must know whether I am in pain,” “Only you can know what you think,” etc., let them consider the occasions and purpose of such expressions. (“War is war” is also not an example of the law of identity.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#21b.8) <span class="fac">[21b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/21b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-21b8}

<span class="series-number">891</span> Am I any less certain that this man is in pain than I am that 2 x 2 = 4? – But does that make the former a _mathematical_ certainty? – “Mathematical certainty” is not a _psychological_ concept.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#22a.1) <span class="fac">[22a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/22a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-22a1}

<span class="series-number">892</span> 17.02.1949

The _kind_ of certainty is the kind of language-game.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#22a.2) <span class="fac">[22a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/22a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-22a2}

<span class="series-number">893</span> There are two different facts here: the one fact that I generally foresee my actions more certainly than the other person; the other, that my prediction is not based on the same evidence as that of the other person, and that it allows for different inferences.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#22a.3) <span class="fac">[22a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/22a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-22a3}

<span class="series-number">894</span> It is not important that I know about certain processes in my _mind_; one does not ask _me_ about my motives for that reason. Rather, it is because the _evidence_ & the consequences of the statement are of a different kind.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#22a.4) <span class="fac">[22a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/22a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-22a4}

<span class="series-number">895</span> “The physicist calculates because paper and ink are more reliable than his apparatus.”

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#22a.5) <span class="fac">[22a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/22a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-22a5}

<span class="series-number">896</span> Let us suppose there is a person who always correctly guesses what I think to myself. (How he does it is irrelevant.) But what is the _criterion_ for him guessing it correctly? Well, I am truth-loving & admit that he has guessed it correctly. – But could I not be mistaken, could my memory not deceive me? & can it not (at all) always, when I – without lying – express what I have thought to myself? – But that is how it seems; it does not seem to depend on whether I know ‘what has happened inside me.’ (I am making a hypothetical construction here.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#22a.6+22b.1) <span class="fac">[22a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/22a.webp),[22b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/22b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-22a622b1}

<span class="series-number">897</span> For the truthful confession that I thought this and that, the criteria are not the same as for the description of a past event.

And the importance of the truthful confession does not lie in the fact that it correctly represents any process with certainty. Rather, it lies in the special signs of ‘subjective truth’ and in the special consequences of the truthful confession. It lies rather in the special consequences that can be drawn from a confession, the truthfulness of which is guaranteed by the special criteria of truth.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#22b.2) <span class="fac">[22b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/22b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-22b2}

<span class="series-number">898</span> (Assuming that the dreams of people can give us important insights into the dreamer, then what gives the insight is the truthful dream narration. The question of whether the dreamer’s memory sometimes deceives him cannot even arise, unless we introduce a completely new criterion for the ‘correctness’ of the dream narration.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#22b.3+23a.1) <span class="fac">[22b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/22b.webp),[23a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/23a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-22b323a1}

<span class="series-number">899</span> 18.02.1949

The child that first learns a primitive verbal expression of its own pain, and then begins to talk (also) about a past pain – it may one day say: “When I have pain, the doctor comes.” Has the word “pain” changed its meaning in this process of learning? – Yes, its use has changed. But doesn’t the word in the primitive expression & in the sentence refer to the _same_ thing, namely the same feeling? Yes; but not using the same technique.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#23a.2) <span class="fac">[23a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/23a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-23a2}

<span class="series-number">900</span> I can utter or write a sentence that expresses an intention (in the first person). Let the sentence be: “I will raise my left arm in 2 minutes.” But there is a difference whether this is actually my intention, or whether I just write it down, as I am doing now, as an example.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#23a.3) <span class="fac">[23a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/23a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-23a3}

<span class="series-number">901</span> One infers not only from pain, but also from pretending, from behaviour.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#23a.4) <span class="fac">[23a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/23a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-23a4}

<span class="series-number">902</span> 19.02.1949

A form of guessing thoughts: One assembles a jigsaw puzzle, the other cannot see it, but he says from time to time: “Now he cannot find something,” “Now he is thinking ‘where have I seen such a piece before?’” “Now he is very satisfied,” “Now he is thinking ‘now I know where it belongs!’” “Now he is thinking ‘it doesn’t quite fit’” – and the other need not speak aloud or to himself.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#23a.5) <span class="fac">[23a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/23a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-23a5}

<span class="series-number">903</span> All of this is guessing at thoughts, & the fact that it doesn't actually happen doesn't make the thought any more obscure than the physical process that I don't perceive.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#23a.6+23b.1) <span class="fac">[23a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/23a.webp),[23b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/23b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-23a623b1}

<span class="series-number">904</span> One can imagine guessing someone’s intention, similar to guessing someone’s thought, but also guessing what someone will actually _do_. To say “Only he can _know_ what he intends” is nonsense. To say “Only he can know what he will do” is false. For the prediction that lies in the expression of the intention (e.g., “As soon as it strikes five, I will go home”) may not be accurate, & I can know what will actually happen.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#23b.2) <span class="fac">[23b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/23b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-23b2}

<span class="series-number">905</span> But two things are important. That in many cases he cannot predict my actions, in which he predicts them in the intention. And that the prediction of intention is not based on the same basis as the other's prediction.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#23b.3) <span class="fac">[23b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/23b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-23b3}

<span class="series-number">906</span> 20.02.1949

Of belief, of certainty, one might sometimes say that they are shades of thought: and indeed, they are often expressed in the _tone_ of speech. But do not think of them as ‘feelings’ that accompany our words.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#23b.4) <span class="fac">[23b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/23b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-23b4}

<span class="series-number">907</span> Would it be correct to say that the language-game of expressing the motive from the side of the ‘other’ is the same as that of expressing the cause, but not from the side of the one who confesses his motive?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#24a.1) <span class="fac">[24a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/24a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-24a1}

<span class="series-number">908</span> February 21, 1949

What is the difference between motive & cause? – How does one _discover_ the motive, & how does one discover the cause?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#24a.2) <span class="fac">[24a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/24a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-24a2}

<span class="series-number">909</span> The unspeakable difference of all these everyday language-games does not become conscious to us, because the external forms of our language make everything equal.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#24a.3) <span class="fac">[24a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/24a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-24a3}

<span class="series-number">910</span> 22.02.1949

One might think that people judge the weather; & one says, for instance, “It looks yellow in the west, that is a good sign. It will stay nice.” & acts accordingly. Another says, “No. It is gray in the north. I am convinced that it will rain” – & acts accordingly. A third has different criteria for his forecast, etc. etc. All these people can presumably be _certain_ of their assessment. And that certainty will be expressed in their actions. Yes, couldn’t they, instead of all the criteria, simply look at the sky & say: “I have the _definite_ impression that it will be …”?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#24a.4) <span class="fac">[24a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/24a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-24a4}

<span class="series-number">911</span> And now: Several people are looking at a sick person (or someone pretending to be sick); one has the impression that he is really sick, the other has the opposite impression; each says a) that he has the specific impression that…, & acts accordingly, b) he gives reasons for this impression, but these are only reasons for _him_. “What happens when one has the impression…?” – Nonsense! How about if people simply said: “I bet…, he is sick”, “I bet…, he is faking it”?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#24a.5+24b.1) <span class="fac">[24a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/24a.webp),[24b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/24b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-24a524b1}

<span class="series-number">912</span> If I now believe that someone is feigning pain, then I do not only believe that he does not have any. Here, there is a specific _suspicion_. I want to say: if people’s _natural_ attitude towards someone who expresses pain is _different_ – some are cool & indifferent, others are compassionate, etc. – then _that_ does not mean that one believes that the person is _feigning_.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#24b.2) <span class="fac">[24b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/24b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-24b2}

<span class="series-number">913</span> If now _someone_ says, “I believe he is feigning it,” what does he mean by that? Well, he _uses a word_ that is used in certain situations. He might continue the game by making conjectures about the other person’s future behaviour; but that does not have to happen. There is some behaviour and some conversation. A few sentences, back and forth; and a few actions. That can be all there is to it.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#24b.3+24b.5) <span class="fac">[24b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/24b.webp),[24b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/24b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-24b324b5}

[Only in the stream of life do words have meaning.]

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#24b.4) <span class="fac">[24b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/24b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-24b4}

<span class="series-number">914</span> It seems to me as if there is a chessboard somewhere, and chess pieces lying next to it. If a few people pass by, one of them might put two or three pieces on the board, and the other might do the same; one makes a move, followed by a counter-move; they make faces, or say things like “That was stupid!” “See!” etc., and then they leave it. All of this would be impossible if they could not _play chess_; but what is happening is a fragment, or a possible fragment, of a chess game.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#24b.6+25a.1) <span class="fac">[24b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/24b.webp),[25a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/25a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-24b625a1}

<span class="series-number">915</span> Compare now “the judgment of an expert” with those judgments about the weather. The former has value for someone other than the person making the judgment – the latter is only an expression of the person making the judgment’s position; although it may also affect others. The language-games are different.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#25a.2) <span class="fac">[25a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/25a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-25a2}

<span class="series-number">916</span> And of course, there are transitions here as well.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#25a.3) <span class="fac">[25a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/25a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-25a3}

<span class="series-number">917</span> One could ask: “With regard to a person’s feelings, is there an ‘expert’ judgment?” And the answer would be: There are also _here_ what one calls ‘people with better’ and ‘people with worse’ judgment.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#25a.4) <span class="fac">[25a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/25a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-25a4}

<span class="series-number">918</span> But there is, for example, no professional examination in the ability to judge people. (What if there were?)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#25a.5) <span class="fac">[25a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/25a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-25a5}

<span class="series-number">919</span> But how does it show that someone has the right judgment? That is _difficult_ to say. I could mention some things; but they would only be fragments of a description.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#25a.7) <span class="fac">[25a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/25a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-25a7}

<span class="series-number">920</span> One can also convince someone of a certain state of mind of the other person through _evidence_, and yet there is no professional study here.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#25a.8+25b.1) <span class="fac">[25a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/25a.webp),[25b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/25b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-25a825b1}

<span class="series-number">921</span> What if one can give _certain_ rules, but only a few, and those which one mostly learns through experience anyway, and if the most important thing remains _unquantifiable_?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#25b.2) <span class="fac">[25b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/25b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-25b2}

<span class="series-number">922</span> What does “_unquantifiable_ evidence” mean? (Let us be honest!)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#25b.3) <span class="fac">[25b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/25b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-25b3}

<span class="series-number">923</span> I tell someone that I have reasons for this assertion, or evidence for it, but that they are ‘unquantifiable’. Well, I have, for _example_, seen the look that one person gave the other. I say, “If _you_ had seen it, you would say the same.” [But there is still an ambiguity here.] I might perhaps show him this look another time, and then he will be convinced. That would be _one_ possibility. I _partly_ make predictions about behaviour (“They will get married, she will make him do it”), and partly I do not.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#25b.4) <span class="fac">[25b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/25b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-25b4}

<span class="series-number">924</span> The question is: What does the unquantifiable evidence _achieve_? By what right does one call it “evidence”? (Compare the case of the weather observers with that of the person who judges the suffering of another.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#25b.5+26a.1) <span class="fac">[25b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/25b.webp),[26a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/26a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-25b526a1}

<span class="series-number">925</span> 23.02.1949

An important fact here is that one learns certain things only through long experience & not through a course in school. For example, how does one develop expertise? Someone says, “This picture is not by so-and-so,” – thus making a statement that is not an aesthetic judgment, but which can perhaps be proven through documents. He may not be able to clearly justify his judgment. – How did he learn it? Could someone have taught it to him? Oh yes. – Not _in the same way_ that one teaches arithmetic. It required long _experience_. That is, the learner may have had to look at & compare a large number of pictures by different masters again & again. In the process, one could give him _hints_. That was the process of _learning_. Then, however, he looked at a picture & gave a judgment. In most cases, he could give reasons for his judgment, but _they_ were in most cases not convincing.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#26a.2) <span class="fac">[26a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/26a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-26a2}

<span class="series-number">926</span> Consider _learning_ – and the _result_ of learning.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#26a.3) <span class="fac">[26a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/26a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-26a3}

<span class="series-number">927</span> The _expert_ might not be able to make himself understood by a jury. That is, they would understand his statement but not his reasons. He can give the other expert the hints that this one understands.

---
