### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#26a.4) <span class="fac">[26a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/26a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-26a4}

<span class="series-number">928</span> But do I want to say that the certainty of mathematics rests on the reliability of ink & paper? _No_. (That would be a vicious circle.) I did not say _why_ there is no dispute among mathematicians, but only _that_ there is no dispute.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#26a.5) <span class="fac">[26a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/26a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-26a5}

<span class="series-number">929</span> It is true that one could not calculate with certain types of paper & ink if they were subject to certain strange changes, but the fact that they could change could again only be shown through memory & comparison with other means of calculation. And one cannot check these again against something else.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#26a.6+26b.1) <span class="fac">[26a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/26a.webp),[26b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/26b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-26a626b1}

<span class="series-number">930</span> Does it make sense to say that people generally agree about their color judgments? What if it were different? One person would say the flower is red, the other that it is blue, etc. – But on what grounds could one now call the words “red” & “blue” the color words of these people? Why should we say that they have the same meaning? We can say one thing or the other. The concept has now changed, & there are reasons to continue to call it the same, & counter-reasons.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#26b.2) <span class="fac">[26b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/26b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-26b2}

<span class="series-number">931</span> But what about this: “There is generally no dispute about color judgments”? There is ‘color blindness’ & ways to detect it. Is that statement not one about the _concept_ of color judgment?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#26b.3) <span class="fac">[26b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/26b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-26b3}

<span class="series-number">932</span> 24.02.1949

If there were no agreement in color judgments, how would people learn to use color words? On what grounds could we call the use they learn the use of ‘color names’? But there are, of course, transitions here.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#26b.4) <span class="fac">[26b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/26b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-26b4}

<span class="series-number">933</span> And this consideration must apply to mathematics: If there were no mathematical certainty, people would also not learn the same technique that we learn. It would be more or less different from ours, & in the extreme case, to the point of being unrecognizable.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#26b.5+27a.1) <span class="fac">[26b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/26b.webp),[27a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/27a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-26b527a1}

<span class="series-number">934</span> “Mathematical truth is, after all, independent of whether people recognize it or not!” – Certainly: “People believe that 2 x 2 = 4” & “2 x 2 = 4” do not have the same sense. This is a mathematical sentence; the other, if it has any sense at all, might mean that people have come to understand the mathematical sentence. The two have completely different _use_. – But what would it mean to say: “Even if all people believed that 2 x 2 = 5, it would _still_ be 4”? What would it look like if everyone believed that? Well, I can only say that it would be a different calculus. Would it be _false_? Is a coronation _false_? At most, it would be useless. And perhaps not even that.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#27a.2) <span class="fac">[27a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/27a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-27a2}

<span class="series-number">935</span> Mathematics is, of course, in one sense, a teaching, but also a doing. And a ‘wrong move’ can only occur as an exception; for if what we now call the rule were the rule, then the game in which it was a wrong move would stop.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#27a.5) <span class="fac">[27a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/27a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-27a5}

<span class="series-number">936</span> 26.02.1949

The ‘unpredictable evidence’ certainly includes tone, gaze, & gesture. Is it not really as if one were observing the workings of the nervous system here? Because I would presumably like my feigned gesture to be completely identical to the genuine one, but it simply does _not_ happen the same way.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#27a.6+27b.1) <span class="fac">[27a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/27a.webp),[27b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/27b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-27a627b1}

<span class="series-number">937</span> I can recognize the genuine look of love, distinguish it from the affected one. And yet I cannot describe it to another in any way. If we had a great painter here, it would be conceivable that he would depict the genuine and the feigned in pictures, or perhaps a depiction in a film could be imagined, on which a description in words might be based.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#27b.2) <span class="fac">[27b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/27b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-27b2}

<span class="series-number">938</span> Ask yourself: How does a person learn to ‘develop a feel’ for something? & how can this feel be used.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#27b.3) <span class="fac">[27b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/27b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-27b3}

<span class="series-number">939</span> February 27, 1949

On what grounds can one say that a child must learn certain things before it can feign? (– – – Before it can make a calculation error.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#27b.4) <span class="fac">[27b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/27b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-27b4}

<span class="series-number">940</span> Someone says of their child, “Today, it feigned for the first time.” One can easily imagine that. But not if they say, “Today, it was sincere for the first time” – even though one could not say that a newborn is sincere. And yet, one can again say, “My child is now decidedly sincere.”

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#27b.5) <span class="fac">[27b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/27b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-27b5}

<span class="series-number">941</span> If one now asks “What must it learn in order to be able to be sincere?” – one might receive an answer such as: “It must have come to _sehen_ that insincerity is bad” – or some other answer that describes the child’s inner self, the inner requirements.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#27b.6+28a.1) <span class="fac">[27b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/27b.webp),[28a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/28a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-27b628a1}

<span class="series-number">942</span> A newborn child cannot be spiteful, friendly, grateful. Gratitude exists only in a complicated pattern of behaviour. If a figure consists of only three lines, it can be neither a regular nor an irregular hexagon.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#28a.2) <span class="fac">[28a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/28a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-28a2}

<span class="series-number">943</span> We certainly normally only say of someone that they are sincere if they can speak. And even if it does not follow from this that the concept ‘sincere’ is _inapplicable_ where there is no language, it does follow that this concept cannot be applied there without some difficulty.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#28a.3) <span class="fac">[28a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/28a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-28a3}

<span class="series-number">944</span> The adult can, of course, without speaking a word, feign or be sincere through facial expressions, gestures & unarticulated sounds.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#28b.2) <span class="fac">[28b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/28b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-28b2}

<span class="series-number">945</span> Imagine a newborn child that could not, of course, speak, but that had the facial expressions & gestures of adults!

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#28b.3) <span class="fac">[28b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/28b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-28b3}

<span class="series-number">946</span> Only in a complex _game of expression_ does hypocrisy and its opposite exist. (Just as one only has a false or correct _move_ in a _game_.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#28b.4) <span class="fac">[28b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/28b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-28b4}

<span class="series-number">947</span> And if the **expression**-game develops, then of course one can say that a mind, an _inner_ self, develops. But now the inner is no longer the cause of the expression. (Just as little as mathematical thinking generates calculation; the driving force is the calculation itself. And this is a remark about concepts.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#28b.5) <span class="fac">[28b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/28b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-28b5}

<span class="series-number">948</span> A triple counterpoint exists only in a very specific musical context.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#28b.6) <span class="fac">[28b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/28b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-28b6}

<span class="series-number">949</span> Imagine someone concealing their intention by hiding a written plan.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#28b.7) <span class="fac">[28b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/28b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-28b7}

<span class="series-number">950</span> 2/28/1949

‘Pain is the important thing – lament is the unimportant thing’ – Well, I want him to take note of my pain, not of the sounds of lament. And how does he take note of my pain?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#28b.8) <span class="fac">[28b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/28b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-28b8}

<span class="series-number">951</span> It seems: here is an inner life, to which the external only allows for uncertain inferences. It is a picture, and what justifies the picture is obvious. The apparent certainty of the first person, the uncertainty of the third.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#29a.1) <span class="fac">[29a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/29a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-29a1}

<span class="series-number">952</span> ‘Sufficient evidence gradually merges into insufficient evidence, without having sharp boundaries.’ The boundaries are blurred. And yet there is evidence.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#29a.2) <span class="fac">[29a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/29a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-29a2}

<span class="series-number">953</span> The assessment of cases is _fluctuating_, like the natural stance towards others.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#29a.4) <span class="fac">[29a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/29a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-29a4}

<span class="series-number">954</span> 3/1/1949

The soulful expression in music. It cannot be described in terms of degrees of intensity and tempo. Just as a soulful facial expression cannot be described by spatial measurements. Yes, it cannot even be explained by a paradigm, because the same piece can be played in countless ways with genuine expression.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#29a.5) <span class="fac">[29a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/29a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-29a5}

<span class="series-number">955</span> And what would the opposite look like? – One could, for example, determine sadness with the same certainty as one determines a sore throat. – But what would that be as a concept of sadness? The one we have?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#29a.6) <span class="fac">[29a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/29a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-29a6}

<span class="series-number">956</span> Why not? Whoever makes _this_ face on a particular occasion, holds themselves _this_ way, etc., we can predict all of that with certainty, which we (in the world as it is now) expect from a truly sad person.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#29b.2) <span class="fac">[29b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/29b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-29b2}

<span class="series-number">957</span> What is our ignorance about the mental states and processes in another person? Because it is made up of several things. We cannot read from their outward behavior what they say to themselves. We often cannot understand what they say. We cannot guess their intentions. We often do not know what mood they are in.

The ignorances are of different _kinds_; and if one thinks about them being resolved, then they would be resolved in different _ways_.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#29b.3) <span class="fac">[29b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/29b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-29b3}

<span class="series-number">958</span> What does it mean, for example, to know the other person’s mood with certainty? Well, one thinks that one could only read it from their face. – But also their intention?! Why then not just as easily from their hands or clothes? – But one could imagine a way to find out the intention. One asks them about their intention and can reliably recognize if they are lying and perhaps also what is going through their head. But if the intention is, as it were, only present as a disposition in this moment, if it is not thought? – Here, it would perhaps be necessary that I had observed him _before_!

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#29b.4+30a.1) <span class="fac">[29b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/29b.webp),[30a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/30a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-29b430a1}

<span class="series-number">959</span> “The inner life is hidden from me” – is that not just as vague as the concept of ‘inner life’? (Because consider: the inner life is feelings and thoughts and images and mood and intention, etc.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#30a.2) <span class="fac">[30a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/30a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-30a2}

<span class="series-number">960</span> You don’t, after all, guess at his _intention_, his sensations, his thoughts, or his mood in the same way.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#30a.3) <span class="fac">[30a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/30a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-30a3}

<span class="series-number">961</span> 03.03.1949

I also cannot anticipate his actions any more than I can anticipate my own, & I have different means of forming my intentions than he does, of guessing them. Even if I have no positive intention, I can have negative intentions; I do not know what I will do, but I have already decided that I do not want to do this & that _not_.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#30a.4) <span class="fac">[30a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/30a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-30a4}

<span class="series-number">962</span> When, in the late hours, one remembers a dream from the previous night that one had never thought of while awake, that is a strange kind of remembering.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#30a.5) <span class="fac">[30a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/30a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-30a5}

<span class="series-number">963</span> The contrast to my uncertainty regarding what is going on in him is not _his_ certainty. For I _can_ also be certain of the feelings of another, but that does not make them mine.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#30a.6) <span class="fac">[30a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/30a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-30a6}

<span class="series-number">964</span> “I can only guess the feelings of another” – does that really make sense when one sees him, for example, with severe injuries and in terrible pain?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#30a.8+30b.1) <span class="fac">[30a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/30a.webp),[30b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/30b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-30a830b1}

<span class="series-number">965</span> March 16, 1949

Is the dream a hallucination? – The memory of the dream is like the memory of a hallucination, or rather: like the memory of a real experience. That is, one sometimes wants to say, for example: “I just saw this and that,” as if one had really just seen it.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#31a.4) <span class="fac">[31a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/31a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-31a4}

<span class="series-number">966</span> Consider, for example, the description of _‘occasions’_. Is it really clear that one has to understand the description of the ‘occasion for sorrow’? After all, the occasions for sorrow are interwoven with 1000 other patterns. Is it clear that one must be able to learn the technique of using the designation for this type of pattern? That one can extract it from the other patterns, as we do?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#31a.5+31b.1) <span class="fac">[31a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/31a.webp),[31b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/31b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-31a531b1}

<span class="series-number">967</span> But there are simple and more complicated cases here, and this is important for the concept. Someone burns himself and cries out; only in very rare circumstances would one call his behavior “pretending.” Yes, here a doctor could tell us that it could only be pretending under these and these circumstances.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#31b.2) <span class="fac">[31b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/31b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-31b2}

<span class="series-number">968</span> 20.03.1949

The description of the use of words. The word is uttered – in what context? We must therefore find something _characteristic_ in these individual instances, a certain _regularity_. – Now, one does not learn the use of words with the help of rules. How could one give someone a rule for when he is to say that he is in pain! – On the other hand, there is an _approximate_ regularity in the use that a person actually makes of the word.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#31b.3) <span class="fac">[31b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/31b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-31b3}

<span class="series-number">969</span> I want to say: it is not predetermined that there is such a thing as ‘a _general_ description of the use of a word’. And if there is indeed such a thing, it is not predetermined how _precise_ such a description must be.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#31b.4) <span class="fac">[31b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/31b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-31b4}

<span class="series-number">970</span> Under what circumstances (under what _external_ circumstances) does one call something a manifestation of pain? (Because this is indeed an important question if one says that a true manifestation of pain corresponds to something _inner_.)

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#31b.5) <span class="fac">[31b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/31b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-31b5}

<span class="series-number">971</span> And can I now describe these circumstances? – and why not? I could give examples, that is clear. How can I learn to describe the circumstances? Was I taught this? Or what would I have to observe for this?

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#32a.1) <span class="fac">[32a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/32a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-32a1}

<span class="series-number">972</span> And the same applies to the external signs of ‘pretending’.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#32a.2) <span class="fac">[32a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/32a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-32a2}

<span class="series-number">973</span> And if I now think of a list of such circumstances, for whom would it be of interest? – Yes, individual insights are probably of interest. But would a list that _aspired to_ completeness be interesting? Could it be of practical use? – That’s not how this game works.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#32a.3) <span class="fac">[32a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/32a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-32a3}

<span class="series-number">974</span> March 22, 1949

There is nothing hidden here, & if I assumed that something was hidden, then knowledge of this hidden thing would have no interest. But I can conceal my thoughts from him by hiding a diary. And here I am hiding something whose knowledge might be of interest to him.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#32a.4) <span class="fac">[32a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/32a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-32a4}

<span class="series-number">975</span> To say that my thoughts are inaccessible to him because they take place in the inner recesses of my mind is a pleonasm.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#32a.5) <span class="fac">[32a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/32a.webp)</span> {#ms-138-32a5}

<span class="series-number">976</span> What I say to myself in secret is not known to him: but it is not about the ‘mental process’ involved, although here there may be a physical process that, if it were known to the other, could replace the spoken word. One could therefore also call a physical process hidden here.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#32a.6+32b.1) <span class="fac">[32a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/32a.webp),[32b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/32b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-32a632b1}

<span class="series-number">977</span> “What I think to myself in secret is hidden from him” can only mean that he cannot guess it, for this reason or that; not that he cannot perceive it because it is in my soul.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#32b.2) <span class="fac">[32b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/32b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-32b2}

<span class="series-number">978</span> One looks at a face & says, “What is probably going on behind this face?” – But one does not have to say that. One does not have to regard the exterior as a front behind which the mental forces are at work.

### [Ms-138](/ms-138/#32b.5) <span class="fac">[32b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-138/32b.webp)</span> {#ms-138-32b5}

<span class="series-number">979</span> The idea of the human mind, which one sees or does not see, is very similar to the idea of word-meaning, which is seen as a process or object associated with the word.
